    p_j=-Bamp_(j-1)e_(j-1)+sum_(l=j)^(k-1)Aamp_l e_l,
                                                    1<=j<=k.       (2)

Thus coordinate l is initially Aamp_l, becomes -Bamp_l in cell l+1,
and then vanishes. There is no persistent extra coordinate in this rule.
Set

    P_can=conv{p_0,...,p_k},
    F_can(x)=max_(v in P_can)(<x,v>-||v||^2/2),
    X_0=C p_0.                                                     (3)

The family being screened consists of actual trajectories of this ONE
function starting from this X_0 and having the gradients in (2) on their
whole respective cells. The initial endpoint has proximal point zero.

Let Z_j be the endpoint of cell j and Y_j=Z_j-p_j. Actual GD with these
cell gradients requires

    Y_0=0,  Y_(j+1)=Y_j-r_j p_j-Cp_(j+1).                         (4)

A query inside cell j, with remaining ordinary mass u in [0,C-1], has

    X_(j,u)=Y_j+(1+u)p_j.                                         (5)

Introduce one freely chosen parameter r_k>=0. It is a terminal shape
parameter, NOT an additional physical step. Define

    wpre_0=r_0,  wpre_j=C+r_j (1<=j<=k),
    Rpre_j=sum_(v=0)^(j-1)wpre_v (1<=j<=k+1),
    Lpre_j=Rpre_j+C.

The extra hypothesis of the cancellation rule is

    Bamp_(j-1)=Rpre_j Aamp_(j-1)/(C+r_j), 1<=j<=k.                 (6)

For j<k this says exactly that coordinate j-1 of the proximal point
becomes zero after that coordinate leaves the gradient. For j=k,
(6) just parameterizes the terminal amplitude with nonnegative terminal
height. Indeed that height is
Rpre_k Aamp_(k-1)Bamp_(k-1)-C Bamp_(k-1)^2.
Every positive terminal amplitude with nonnegative height has a unique
r_k>=0 in (6).

Directly expanding (4) coordinate by coordinate gives

    Y_j=-r_j Bamp_(j-1)e_(j-1)
               -Lpre_j sum_(l=j)^(k-1)Aamp_l e_l, 1<=j<=k.        (7)

For example, an older coordinate l<j-1 has coefficient
-Rpre_(l+1)Aamp_l+(C+r_(l+1))Bamp_l=0 by (6). The current coordinate
is C Bamp_(j-1)-Rpre_j Aamp_(j-1)=-r_j Bamp_(j-1).
An untouched coordinate l>=j accumulates the coefficient -Lpre_j.

Put

    Ecan_j=sum_(l=j)^(k-1)Aamp_l^2,  Ecan_k=0,
    Ncan_0=Ecan_0, Ncan_j=Ecan_j+Bamp_(j-1)^2 (j>=1),
    Kcan_j=Ecan_j-Aamp_(j-1)Bamp_(j-1) (j>=1).

Coordinate multiplication gives

    ||p_j||^2=Ncan_j,
    <p_i,p_j>=Kcan_j whenever i<j.                                (8)

These are correlated gradients, generally with negative cross products.
Their full initial norm is C^2 Ecan_0. Also, independently of r_k,

    M_m=Rpre_k+2C.                                                (9)

Both sides equal (k+1)C+sum_(j<k)r_j by counting the physical cells.

## 2. One explicit amplitude law and one smooth convex function

Choose any r_k>0, for example r_k=C. All Rpre_j,Lpre_j are now explicit
finite real numbers. Set Aamp_0=1. Use (6) to determine Bamp_0. For
j=1,...,k-1 recursively set

    Aamp_j^2 = r_j Bamp_(j-1)^2 /
                 [Lpre_j(1+Rpre_(j+1)/(C+r_(j+1)))],
    Aamp_j = positive square root of the preceding expression,
    Bamp_j = Rpre_(j+1) Aamp_j/(C+r_(j+1)).                       (10)

Every amplitude is positive. These are finite formulas; no feasibility
problem or optimization is hidden in the definition. Their key identity is

    Lpre_j Aamp_j(Aamp_j+Bamp_j)=r_j Bamp_(j-1)^2,
                                                       1<=j<k.  (11)

We first justify the function in (3). Projection onto the compact convex
set P_can has a unique minimizer of squared distance. Completing the
square in (3) makes this projection its unique maximizing v. A vector p
in P_can is the projection of x precisely when

    <x-p,v-p><=0 for every v in P_can.                            (12)

Necessity follows by considering the segment from p to v. Sufficiency
follows by expanding ||x-v||^2. Applying (12) at x and x' and adding gives
||p-p'||^2<=<p-p',x-x'>, hence the projection is 1-Lipschitz. Comparing
the two maxima in (3) gives

    0<=F_can(x')-F_can(x)-<p,x'-x>
        <=<p'-p,x'-x><=||x'-x||^2.

Thus F_can is everywhere finite, convex, differentiable, and 1-smooth,
with gradient the projection onto P_can.

The origin is in the interior of P_can. To exhibit its barycentric
coefficients, choose alpha_0>0 and recursively set

    alpha_(l+1)=(Aamp_l/Bamp_l)sum_(i=0)^l alpha_i,

then choose alpha_0 so the total sum is 1. Every coefficient is positive,
and the l-th coordinate of sum alpha_i p_i is zero. The vectors
p_1,...,p_k are linearly independent: their coordinate matrix is triangular
with nonzero diagonal -Bamp_0,...,-Bamp_(k-1). The same coordinate
recursion makes the space of linear relations among all k+1 vertices
one-dimensional. Its positive generating relation has nonzero sum, so
there is no affine dependence. Consequently P_can is a full-dimensional
simplex and the displayed positive convex combination is interior.

Since 0 is an interior point, the only vector x satisfying
<x,v><=0 for all v in P_can is 0. By (12) this is precisely the set
of x with projection zero. Therefore F_can has UNIQUE minimizer 0,
F_can(0)=0, and its true initial squared distance is C^2 Ecan_0.

## 3. All original interpolation pairs, with shared positive heights

Define delta_can_j=<Y_j,p_j>. For j>=1, (7) gives

    delta_can_j=r_j Bamp_(j-1)^2-Lpre_j Ecan_j.                  (13)

For every s<j, coordinate multiplication gives

    <Y_j,p_s>=-r_j Aamp_(j-1)Bamp_(j-1)-Lpre_j Ecan_j
                 <=delta_can_j.                               (14)

For every s>j the corresponding value is

    <Y_j,p_s>=-Lpre_j Kcan_s.                                   (15)

To control ALL future vertices at once, use (6) and (11) to compute

    Kcan_(j+1)-Kcan_j
       =Aamp_(j-1)Bamp_(j-1)-Aamp_j(Aamp_j+Bamp_j)
       =C Bamp_(j-1)(Aamp_(j-1)+Bamp_(j-1))/Lpre_j >0.           (16)

Moreover Kcan_k=-Aamp_(k-1)Bamp_(k-1)<0, so every Kcan_j is negative.
Equation (11) also gives, for 1<=j<k,

    delta_can_j=-Lpre_j Kcan_(j+1)>0.                           (17)

Thus (15) is at most delta_can_j for every s>j, by (16).
The terminal height is delta_can_k=r_k Bamp_(k-1)^2>0. At j=0,
Y_0=0 satisfies every normal comparison with equality. Equations
(14)--(17) establish (12) at every endpoint, including against vertex 0,
which belongs to the convex hull. They retain both orientations and every
older and later vertex.

The same argument covers all original samples inside each ordinary cell.
For any i,j, <p_j,p_i-p_j><=0. For i<j, this follows from
Kcan_j-Ncan_j=-Bamp_(j-1)(Aamp_(j-1)+Bamp_(j-1))<0.
For i>j, Kcan_i<=Ecan_i<=Ecan_j<=Ncan_j. Add u times this inequality
to the endpoint normal, with u>=0. Thus (12) holds at every position (5),
and its actual gradient is p_j. Equations (4)--(5) now verify every
physical GD step on the original word.

There is ONE value array, namely

    f_(j,u)=delta_can_j+(u+1/2)Ncan_j.                          (18)

This is its actual value under F_can. For an explicit all-pair check,
the residual convention in accepted fact 1252b8823bb5a290 is

    Q_ab=f_a-f_b+<g_a,X_b-X_a>+||g_a-g_b||^2/2.

Substituting (5),(18) gives exactly

    Q_((i,u),(j,v))=<Y_j+v p_j,p_i-p_j><=0.                   (19)

The inequalities against the optimum jet (0,0,0) are

    Q_((j,u),star)=0,
    Q_(star,(j,u))=-delta_can_j-u Ncan_j<=0.                   (20)

No independent selection of values for different pairs has occurred.
The normalized terminal objective is exactly

    V_can=(1+2r_k)Bamp_(k-1)^2/(C^2 Ecan_0).                 (21)

Finally, this legal trajectory belongs to the requested increment cone
by an explicit decomposition. In coordinate l<k-1 the only gradient
changes are -(Aamp_l+Bamp_l) at the transition from cell l to l+1,
and Bamp_l at the transition from cell l+1 to l+2. Coordinate k-1
has only its first change before the terminal sample. The Gram of all
gradient increments is the sum, over l, of the outer products of these
two-supported lists. Each is a real PSD block. For the full Gram retain
also the initial-vector and initial-gradient entries C Aamp_l and Aamp_l
in the same scalar coordinate lists. No original initial row or gradient
has been removed; every weight in this finite decomposition is 1.

## 4. Necessary product loss for EVERY legal cancellation-rule member

The amplitudes need not follow (10) in this section. Suppose any positive
amplitudes satisfying (6) give an actual trajectory of (3). The endpoint
normal comparing current j to future vertex j+1 is necessary. Equations
(13),(15) show that it is exactly

    Lpre_j Aamp_j(Aamp_j+Bamp_j)<=r_j Bamp_(j-1)^2,
                                                   1<=j<k.      (22)

Define

    lambda_can_j=r_j Rpre_j/[(C+r_j)(C+Rpre_j)] in (0,1).

Substituting (6) into (22) gives

    Aamp_j^2/Aamp_(j-1)^2
       <= [wpre_(j+1)/wpre_j] lambda_can_j
                                  [Rpre_j/Rpre_(j+2)].          (23)

Indeed Rpre_(j+2)=Rpre_(j+1)+wpre_(j+1), and the right side
expands to r_j Rpre_j^2 wpre_(j+1) divided by
wpre_j^2 Lpre_j(Rpre_(j+1)+wpre_(j+1)). This is precisely the
coefficient obtained from (22).

The products in (23) telescope, giving

    Bamp_(k-1)^2/Aamp_0^2
      <= Rpre_k r_0 Rpre_2 /
             [wpre_k wpre_1(Rpre_k+wpre_k)]
                     * product_(j=1)^(k-1)lambda_can_j.         (24)

For k=1 the empty-product identity still holds, with
Rpre_2=Rpre_1+wpre_1. For k>=2, the quantities r_0,Rpre_2,wpre_1
are physical prefix data, independent of the terminal shape parameter.

Let d_can=2C-1>=1. Combining Ecan_0>=Aamp_0^2 with (21),(24) yields

    V_can <= Rpre_k r_0 Rpre_2/(C^2 wpre_1)
          * (2wpre_k-d_can)/[wpre_k(Rpre_k+wpre_k)]
          * product_(j=1)^(k-1)lambda_can_j
      < 2r_0 Rpre_2/(C^2 wpre_1)
          * product_(j=1)^(k-1)lambda_can_j,  k>=2.              (25)

The last strict inequality follows from d_can>0 and Rpre_k>0.
It is uniform in every legal terminal amplitude and in all amplitudes
before it. Positive endpoint heights have not been set to zero.

## 5. Uniform loss on the actual ruler, over every cutoff

The physical mass recursion is M_(j+1)=2M_j+a_rul c_rul^j. Hence

    A_mass=a_rul/(c_rul-2),
    M_j=A_mass c_rul^j+(1-A_mass)2^j.                           (26)

For m>=128,

    sigma<c_rul<5/2,  3/8<a_rul<sigma-2<c_rul-2<1/2,
    3/4<A_mass<1,
    (3/4)c_rul^j<M_j<=c_rul^j.                                (27)

For explicit rational checks, sigma<17/7 implies
c_rul<2193/896<5/2; a_rul>(127/128)/(5/2)=127/320>3/8;
and a_rul<1/sigma=sigma-2. These prove every inequality used in (27).
Set t_can=a_rul c_rul^ell/C. Then

    0<t_can<c_rul-2<1/2,  M_m/C<(4/3)c_rul^q.                 (28)

For q>=2, the first three checkpoint excesses are
r_0=t_can C, r_1=c_rul t_can C, r_2=t_can C. Therefore

    2r_0 Rpre_2/(C^2 wpre_1)
       =(2t_can/C)[1+t_can/(1+c_rul t_can)]<3/(2C),
    lambda_can_1
       =[c_rul t_can/(1+c_rul t_can)]
           [t_can/(1+t_can)]<5/27.                            (29)

There are exactly 2^(q-1)-1 lowest-level checkpoints among indices
1,...,k-1. Each has lambda_can_j<1/3, because r_j=t_can C.
Index 1 is the first high checkpoint and is not among them. All other
factors are less than 1. Equations (25),(28),(29) give, for q>=3,

    M_m V_can
      < (10/27)(5/2)^q 3^(-(2^(q-1)-1))
      <=625/2916.                                             (30)

At q=3 the expression is 625/2916. Its ratio at q+1 to its value at q
is (5/2)3^(-2^(q-1))<1, proving the last inequality for every q>=3.

When q=2, k=3 and

    M_m/C=4+(2+c_rul)t_can<25/4,
    Rpre_2/C=1+(1+c_rul)t_can<11/4,
    lambda_can_2< (1/3)(11/15)=11/45.

The same product bound now gives the sharper estimate

    M_m V_can < (25/4)(3/2)(5/27)(11/45)=275/648.             (31)

When q=1, there is just one checkpoint. Writing A=Aamp_0,B=Bamp_0,
the terminal numerator before normalization is

    2r_0 AB-(2C-1)B^2 <= r_0^2 A^2/(2C-1).

Since M_m=(2+t_can)C and C=M_(m-1)>=2, this gives

    M_m V_can <=(2+t_can)t_can^2 C/(2C-1)<5/12.              (32)

As 5/12<275/648 and 625/2916<275/648, (30)--(32) prove

    sup_(all cutoffs and legal cancellation amplitudes)
          M_m V_can < gamma_can:=275/648<1/2.                (33)

The no-checkpoint, constant-gradient extension with terminal proximal
point zero has payoff 1/M_m^2 and also obeys this bound for m>=128.
It can be realized by the projection function for the segment [0,p],
starting at M_m p; zero is a closest minimizer. This degenerate extension
is not needed for the simplex construction in Sections 1--3.

In particular (33) is below the legal Huber witness 1/(2M_m-1), whose
existence is part of accepted fact b98bb807d514e80d. The new construction
therefore supplies no gain even over that baseline.

## 6. Comparison with the actual exact benchmark

For completeness, the required sharper scalar bound follows directly
from the EXACT recursions in accepted fact b98bb807d514e80d. Let
rho=log_2(sigma)>1. Their primitive shifted mass C_n=S_circle(n)+1
has join

    Omega(A,B)=[A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB)]/2,

and their dominant denominator B_n has join

    G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.

We prove

    C_n<=(n+1)^rho, B_n<=1+4n^rho.                            (34)

For t>0 set J(t)=(1+t)^rho-1. Differentiating with t=exp(z) gives

    d^2/dz^2 log J(exp(z))
      =rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)
            [(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/J(t)^2 >0.

The bracket is positive because it vanishes at 0 and its derivative
rho[(1+t)^(rho-1)-1] is positive. Convexity at z and -z implies
J(t)J(1/t)>=J(1)^2=(sigma-1)^2=2. For A=t^rho B and
D=(1+t)^rho B, this says (D-A)(D-B)>=2AB, so
D^2-(A+B)D-AB>=0. The positive root of that polynomial is Omega(A,B).
Thus

    Omega(A,B)<=(A^(1/rho)+B^(1/rho))^rho,

including zero arguments by continuity. Induction in the exact primitive
recursion proves the first bound in (34). Also G(A,D)<=4A+D, since
sqrt(D^2+8AD)<=D+4A. Induct in the dominant recursion and use
(k+1)^rho+(n-k-1)^rho<=n^rho to prove its second bound. The last
power inequality follows by integrating the increasing derivative of
x^rho; it includes a zero summand.

Equation (26) gives, with e=exp(1),

    M_m/sigma^m
      =(1+1/m)^m[A_mass+(1-A_mass)(2/c_rul)^m] -> e.           (35)

Indeed a_rul tends to 1/sigma=sigma-2, so A_mass tends to 1,
and 2/c_rul is bounded above by 2/sigma<1. Consequently (33)--(35)
prove the finite and asymptotic exact-profile bounds

    B_n V_can < gamma_can [1+4(2^m-1)^rho]/M_m,
    limsup_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1) sup V_can
       <=4 gamma_can/e=275/(162e)<1.                          (36)

The supremum includes all legal amplitudes and all cutoffs. If q=m-ell
tends to infinity, the right side of (30) tends to zero, so the exact-B
ratio tends to zero as well. No fitted dyadic constant or assumed
asymptotic phase for B_n was used.

## 7. What has, and has not, been excluded

The constructor (10) proves nonemptiness and simultaneous attainability
of every original pair, including optimum and remote pairs. The bound
(33) applies more broadly to every legal projection trajectory satisfying
the stated cancellation rule, even when (22) is strict. It does not assume
an acute cone: in the explicit constructor every distinct-gradient cross
inner product is strictly negative by (16).

The assumptions of the obstruction include (2), the fixed initial point
X_0=Cp_0 (equivalently Y_0=0), the one projection function (3), and the
extinguished-coordinate cancellation in (6). Positive endpoint heights
alone do not evade it. General joint value arrays, different initial
proximal points, a persistent final coordinate, different two-change
histories, and nonzero extinct-coordinate residuals are outside its scope.
No unrestricted upper certificate and no common-array A2 separator is
claimed.

## 8. Exact residual equations for a changed rule

Drop cancellation but retain (2)--(5), positive amplitudes, and the same
projection function. For 1<=j<=k define its effective endpoint parameter

    tau_eff_j=Rpre_j Aamp_(j-1)/Bamp_(j-1)-C.                 (37)

Here Rpre_j uses ONLY physical r_0,...,r_(j-1). If the data are legal,
the optimum normal requires tau_eff_j>=0; it is strictly positive for
j<k, since Ecan_j>0. The frozen proximal coordinate l<k-1 is

    z_ext_l=(r_(l+1)-tau_eff_(l+1))Bamp_l.                    (38)

The exact endpoint coordinates are now z_ext_l for l<j-1,
-tau_eff_j Bamp_(j-1) at l=j-1, and -Lpre_j Aamp_l for l>=j.
Its height is

    delta_j=tau_eff_j Bamp_(j-1)^2-Lpre_j Ecan_j.

For future vertices s>j the normal inequalities are exactly

    Lpre_j(Ecan_j-Kcan_s)<=tau_eff_j Bamp_(j-1)^2.            (39)

For every old vertex 0<=s<j they are exactly

    -1_(s>=1) Bamp_(s-1)z_ext_(s-1)
      +sum_(l=s)^(j-2)Aamp_l z_ext_l
      <=tau_eff_j Bamp_(j-1)(Aamp_(j-1)+Bamp_(j-1)).         (40)

These formulas follow by multiplying the displayed endpoint coordinates
by p_s-p_j. An empty sum is zero. They retain all old residuals, including
their signs. Cancellation is exactly z_ext_l=0.

In particular the next-future inequality and the next-old inequality
give a necessary propagation condition. For 1<=s<k, they read

    Lpre_s Aamp_s(Aamp_s+Bamp_s)
                <=tau_eff_s Bamp_(s-1)^2,
    (tau_eff_s-r_s)Bamp_(s-1)^2
                <=tau_eff_(s+1)Bamp_s(Aamp_s+Bamp_s).

Substitute Bamp_s/Aamp_s=Rpre_(s+1)/(C+tau_eff_(s+1)) and
Rpre_(s+1)=Lpre_s+r_s. If tau_eff_s>r_s, cancel the positive
Bamp_(s-1)^2 and rearrange; if tau_eff_s<=r_s, use tau_eff_(s+1)>=0.
The resulting necessary condition in both cases is

    tau_eff_(s+1)
       >= C Lpre_s(tau_eff_s-r_s)_+
                       /[r_s(Lpre_s+tau_eff_s)].               (41)

Thus a negative extinct residual can increase the future budget in (39),
but the same residual imposes a positive cost in the next old comparison.
Equation (41) is only necessary; it does not replace the remote sums in
(40) or establish a competitive construction.

## 9. An actual-ruler countercheck against forward-only repair

For every actual H_m above with q>=2, the following exact relaxed data
pass ALL future endpoint normal comparisons and ALL optimum comparisons,
with strictly positive later endpoint heights, yet fail an old pair.
They are a counterexample to sufficiency of that one-sided test, NOT a
legal lower-bound witness.

Use the same physical Rpre_j,Lpre_j, and choose

    tau_eff_1=2r_1, tau_eff_2=r_2/100,
    tau_eff_j=r_j for 3<=j<k, and tau_eff_k=C.                 (42)

For k=3 the last assignment is just tau_eff_3=C. Set Aamp_0=1,
Bamp_0=Rpre_1/(C+tau_eff_1), and, for 1<=j<k, set

    Aamp_j^2=tau_eff_j Bamp_(j-1)^2 /
                [Lpre_j(1+Rpre_(j+1)/(C+tau_eff_(j+1)))],
    Bamp_j=Rpre_(j+1)Aamp_j/(C+tau_eff_(j+1)).                 (43)

All amplitudes are positive. The same algebra as in (16), now using
(37),(43), shows that Kcan_(j+1)-Kcan_j is strictly positive and
delta_j=-Lpre_j Kcan_(j+1)>0 for j<k. Since Kcan_k<0 and
delta_k=tau_eff_k Bamp_(k-1)^2>0, every future normal in (39) and
every optimum comparison holds. Adding ordinary remaining mass preserves
these inequalities because <p_j,p_s-p_j><=0, as proved in Section 3.

But the endpoint-2 normal against vertex 1, equivalently the original
residual Q_((1,0),(2,0)), equals

    (tau_eff_1-r_1)Bamp_0^2
       -tau_eff_2 Bamp_1(Aamp_1+Bamp_1)
      =r_1 Bamp_0^2
         [1-2tau_eff_2 Rpre_2/((C+tau_eff_2)Lpre_1)].           (44)

Here r_0=t_can C, r_1=c_rul t_can C, r_2=t_can C. Thus

    Rpre_2/Lpre_1=1+c_rul t_can/(1+t_can)<11/6,
    tau_eff_2/(C+tau_eff_2)<1/200.

The bracket in (44) is greater than 589/600. This is a strictly positive
violation on the actual schedule for every q>=2 and m>=128. One cannot
repair the cancellation rule by independently increasing an endpoint
height and clearing its propagated cost at the following checkpoint.
All the heights belong to the same proposed array, but that array is
incompatible with the retained earlier-vertex inequality.

The remaining quantitative task is to choose nonzero residuals satisfying
ALL of (39)--(40), or change another stated generating-rule assumption,
and obtain an actual payoff at the exact benchmark scale. The original
all-schedule exact-profile target is unresolved. Current TASK and guidance
allocate no routine verifier call, so this entire document remains a draft.

## 10. Tiny exact sanity checks

The local script `check_positive_height_cancellation.py` checks only three
fixed fixtures, with rational c_rul=5/2 and a_rul=2/5. These coefficients
check the general algebra and are not asserted to be the m>=128 parameters.
Rational multiples of sqrt(Aamp_j^2) represent the coordinates, so every
inner product and residual is an exact rational number.

For H_3 at ell=0 and ell=1, the constructor (10) with r_k=C passes all
72 ordered original interpolation pairs, including the optimum, and has
respectively seven and three strictly positive later endpoint heights.
The full original step lists agree. The exact normalized payoffs are
1562301543/5336489025232 and 828765625/160552296576. A third H_2 fixture
checks (42)--(44): all future and optimum comparisons hold, while the old
residual is exactly 6932/395325>0. All three checks together took 0.00521
seconds. They are finite sanity checks; the symbolic proof above, not
these computed values, is the evidence for the universal statements.

# Two orientations of a boundary block excluded from the full primitive cone

Status: complete joint proof DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. This is max2 round22, the
second and last of the two rounds assigned by TASK.md and master_guidance
8211d6128deabdf3. No numerical evaluation, solver, word scan, machine
algebra, replay, verifier, installation, or subordinate agent is used.

Accepted inputs: fact04219554e41d9750, Section10, for the full primitive
certificate identity; fact1252b8823bb5a290 for the interpolation convention
and its explicit realizing function. Every other mathematical ingredient
used below is proved in this document. Earlier draft d3abee1b17da52da
motivates the prefix construction but is not used as a fact.

## 1. Statement and exact scope

For a nonnegative four-step word h=(h0,h1,h2,h3), write

 s=h0+h1+h2+h3, C=1+s.

Retain all twenty ordered multipliers Lambda_ij>=0, i!=j in {0,...,4},
Lambda_ii=0. Let e be the all-one column, T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i,
T_it=0 for t>i, L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda, and

 omega=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2s)^T,
 Dprim=diag(h0(2-h0),h1(2-h1),h2(2-h2),h3(2-h3),-s(s+2)).

The full ordinary primitive cone requires

 L^T e=omega, M=Dprim+sym(LT)>=0 on ALL of R^5.          (1)

Here sym(A)=(A+A^T)/2. No formal gradient is identified with another,
even if a physical step is zero.

**Joint draft result.** No certificate (1) exists in either of these
two sets:

 (P) C>=193/25, h0 in[139/100,141/100],
     h1 in[179/100,181/100], h2,h3 arbitrary nonnegative;

 (R) C>=193/25, h2 in[179/100,181/100],
     h3 in[139/100,141/100], h0,h1 arbitrary nonnegative.

For each word the proof supplies an explicit legal negative-energy
trajectory, selected by stated inequalities. Thus both orientations of
every word in (P) are excluded from the original full certificate cone.
There is no upper cap on C and no assumption that the two free steps
exceed one. This strictly expands the hypotheses covered in the old
prefix-prism draft, which required C<87/10 and both free steps>1.

This is a direct proof for two reversed sets. It is NOT a certificate
map, an existential reversal theorem for the entire four-step cone, a
counterexample to such a theorem, or a resolution of the original
unrestricted asymptotic target. In particular it does not permit imposing
an ordering or symmetry on schedules outside these sets.

## 2. Legal data and full-cone soundness

Use normalized smoothness1. On formal GD data x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i define

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
 V_h=2 sum_(i<4) h_i(f_i-f_4)
       +sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_4||^2.

Fact04219554e41d9750 gives the full identity

 V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij
                         =sum_(i,j)M_ij<g_i,g_j>.       (2)

If all sampled data come from one convex1-smooth function, every Q_ij
is nonpositive by fact1252b8823bb5a290. Hence (1) implies V_h>=0 on
every such trajectory. A single legal trajectory with V_h<0 excludes
all possible arrays in (1), regardless of their support or rank.

All constructions below use saturated values

 f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2.                             (3)

Telescoping the GD updates gives, for these values,

 V_h=||x_0||^2-||x_4+s g_4||^2.                        (4)

Indeed 2h_i f_i+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
=||x_i||^2-||x_(i+1)||^2; the remaining terminal terms complete the
square in (4). In particular, if x_4=g_4 then

 V_h=||x_0||^2-C^2||g_4||^2.                           (5)

For values (3), all interpolation residuals simplify to

 Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,x_j-g_j>.                               (6)

Include the optimum jet x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Whenever (6) is
nonpositive for every ordered finite and optimum pair, an explicit
realizing function is F=H^*, the convex conjugate of

 H(v)=max_j{<x_j,g_j>-f_j+<x_j,v-g_j>+||v-g_j||^2/2},   (7)

with j running over all samples and the optimum. At g_i the j-th
quadratic minus the i-th is Q_ij<=0, and x_i is a subgradient of H
there. The function H is1-strongly convex; its conjugate is everywhere
finite, convex, differentiable and1-smooth, and interpolates the jets.
The optimum jet gives min F=F(0)=0. This is the construction in accepted
fact1252b8823bb5a290; checking (6) below checks every original pair.

Write

 h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d), C=5+a+b+c+d,                     (8)

where a,b,c,d>=-1. Positive shifts will only be assumed where explicitly
proved or specified.

## 3. Endpoint and quadratic witnesses

If a>0, let Htail=1+h1+h2+h3=C-1-a and eta=a/Htail.
Use the scalar function obtained by integrating the derivative
clip(x,[-eta,1]) from0, and start at x0=1. The first step reaches-a.
All subsequent gradients equal-eta and the terminal point equals-eta:
at every suffix query its position is-eta times one plus the sum of
the remaining suffix steps. Thus all these positions are on the lower
plateau, including any repeated positions from zero steps. Its energy is

 V_h=1-C^2 a^2/Htail^2.

Consequently full feasibility implies

 a<=(C-1)/(C+1)<1 when a>0.                            (9)

For a<=0 the bound a<1 holds directly. The upper endpoint is obtained
independently: if d>0, start at C-1-d with derivative clip(x,[-d,1]).
All four queried gradients before the last update equal1 and the final
point and gradient equal-d. Formula (5) gives

 d<=(C-1)/(C+1)<1 when d>0; thus always d<1.            (10)

If an endpoint inequality fails, the displayed scalar function itself
has strictly negative V_h.

The actual unit quadratic f(x)=x^2/2, started at1, has terminal gradient
abcd and saturated values. Hence

 V_h=1-C^2 a^2 b^2 c^2 d^2,
                    C|abcd|<=1                       (11)

is necessary for the full cone. No sign assumption on the shifts is
needed for (11).

## 4. Complete triangle witness at the two middle steps

Suppose b,c>0 while a,d>=-1. Put

 P=2+a=1+h0>0, D=2+d=1+h3>0,
 U=(1+b)+(1+c)+(1+d)=1+b+c+D, C=P+U.

If D<=bc, use the scalar function with derivative min(x,1), starting
at P. Its actual positions and gradients are

 x=(P,1,-b,bc,bc-D+1), g=(1,1,-b,1,1).

Both final positions are at least1. Formula (4) gives

 V_h=P^2-[P+(1+b)(1+c)]^2<0.                          (12)

The derivative min(x,1) is continuous, nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz;
its integral from0 is convex1-smooth and has minimum0. All values in
(12) obey (3), so this is an actual function, including at D=bc.

Suppose instead D>bc. Define

 Ktri=b^2 c+U(D-bc)>0, t=b^2 c/Ktri in(0,1),
 u=(1,0), w=(t,sqrt(t(1-t))), v=-(b u+D w)/(1+c).

The triangle with vertices u,v,w contains0, since
b u+(1+c)v+D w=0 with strictly positive coefficients. Assign

 x=(P u,u,-b u,D w,w), g=(u,u,v,w,w),

and values (3). These are the original four GD updates. For all
interpolation pairs it suffices to check that x_j-g_j is an outward
normal of the triangle at g_j. At j=0 it is h0 u, whose pairings with
v-u,w-u are nonpositive; at j=1,4 it is0. Direct inner products give

 ||u||^2=1, <u,w>=||w||^2=t,
 <u,v>=-(b+Dt)/(1+c), <v,w>=-(b+D)t/(1+c).

At j=2 the normal y=-b u-v satisfies

 <w-v,y>=(-b^2 c+Ktri t)/(1+c)^2=0,
 <u-v,y>=-b(1-t)c/(1+c)<0.

At j=3 the normal h3 w pairs with u-w to0 and with v-w to
-h3 t[1+(b+D)/(1+c)]<=0. The optimum comparisons follow because0
is in the triangle. Thus every original and optimum Q is nonpositive.
The function (7) realizes the entire trajectory, with x4=g4=w.

Its energy is P^2-C^2 t, and expansion using C=P+U gives

 C^2 b^2 c-P^2 Ktri
      =U{bc[P^2+b(P+C)]-P^2 D}.                       (13)

Together with (12), full feasibility therefore requires

                 bc[1+b(P+C)/P^2]<=D.                 (14)

If (14) fails, either (12) applies or the triangle trajectory has
strictly negative energy. The proof allows h0=0 or h3=0 and imposes
no equality on formal gradients in the certificate.

## 5. Direct exclusion of the reflected set (R)

Here c in[79/100,81/100], d in[39/100,41/100], a,b>=-1,
and C>=193/25. Thus

 K=a+b=C-5-c-d>=3/2.                                  (15)

If a>=2/5, endpoint feasibility (9) gives 2/5<=a<1, and b=K-a>0.
The concave function a(3/2-a) has endpoint values11/25 and1/2 on
[2/5,1], so

 ab>=a(3/2-a)>=11/25.

Consequently

 Cabcd >= (193/25)(11/25)(79/100)(39/100)
       =6540963/6250000>1,                            (16)

contradicting the actual quadratic witness (11). Thus any remaining
candidate would have a<2/5 and b>11/10. This implication includes
a<=0 without any use of (11) in that case.

Now 1<=P=2+a<12/5 and D=2+d<=241/100. For positive P the function
(P+C)/P^2 decreases in P and increases in C. Therefore

 (P+C)/P^2>(12/5+193/25)/(12/5)^2=253/144.

The triangle condition (14) would imply

 241/100 >= bc[1+b(P+C)/P^2]
          > (11/10)(79/100)[1+(11/10)(253/144)]
          =3669787/1440000
          =241/100+199387/1440000,                    (17)

a contradiction. This closes every word in (R) at every C>=193/25.
The explicit separating function is the first endpoint cut if (9)
fails, the unit quadratic if (16) applies, and otherwise the applicable
function in Section4. It is not inferred from a reversal theorem.

## 6. Direct exclusion of the prefix set (P)

Here a in[39/100,41/100], b in[79/100,81/100], c,d>=-1,
and C>=193/25. Put P=2+a, Q=3+a+b, D=2+d. Then

 c+d>=3/2, P<=241/100, Q<=211/50<17/4.                 (18)

By (10), a feasible word has d<1. Hence c>1/2, even if d is negative.
In particular the middle-to-tail cut below always has a positive excess.

### 6.1. Every possible final shift reduces to d<3/8

If d<3/8 there is nothing to prove. Suppose d>=3/8. Then d>0, c>0.
If cd>=1, use derivative min(x,1), starting at Q. Its actual data are

 x=(Q,2+b,1,-c,cd), g=(1,1,1,-c,1).

Every value is saturated, and (4) gives

 V_h=Q^2-[Q+(1+c)(1+d)]^2<0.                          (19)

Thus a remaining feasible word must have cd<1.

If bc<1, use derivative clip(x,[-b,1]) and start at P. Its data are

 x=(P,1,-b,bc,-bcd), g=(1,1,-b,bc,-bcd).

The assumed bc<1 and cd<1 put the last two gradients in the quadratic
part, and the terminal point equals its gradient. The energy (5) gives

 Cbcd<=P, hence cd<=P/(Cb)<2/5,                        (20)

where (2/5)(193/25)(79/100)>241/100 proves the last strict inequality.
But 3/8<=d<1 and c+d>=3/2 give

 cd>=d(3/2-d)>=min(27/64,1/2)>2/5,

contradicting (20).

If bc>=1, put alpha=(1+b)/(1+c)<=b and use derivative
clip(x,[-alpha,1]), again starting at P. The third query gradient is
-alpha, the next point is-b+(1+c)alpha=1, and the terminal point is-d.
Since cd<1 and alpha>=1/c, the terminal is in the quadratic part.
Thus (5) gives Cd<=P, whence

 d<=P/C<3/8.                                         (21)

The strict rational comparison is (241/100)/(193/25)<3/8.
This contradicts the current d>=3/8. The equality bc=1 is retained in
this second branch. Every failed inequality above has the explicitly
displayed legal negative-energy function. We conclude, without assuming
that h3>1 in advance, that any surviving candidate obeys

 d<3/8, c>9/8, eta=c/(2+d)>9/19.                     (22)

### 6.2. A legal repaired cut contradicts all remaining candidates

First use the ordinary third cut, with derivative clip(x,[-eta,1])
and initial point Q. Its data are

 xB=(Q,2+b,1,-c,-eta), gB=(1,1,1,-eta,-eta).

They are legal also at h3=0: then d=-1, eta=c and the last query is
repeated. Formula (5) implies eta<=Q/C<1 for a feasible word.
Since b<=81/100, this gives b eta<1. If this cut fails, it already
is the required negative-energy function.

Define a second scalar array following the same physical updates:

 xA=(1,-a,ab,-abc,-ab eta),
 gA=(1,-a,ab,-ab eta,-ab eta).

In both arrays use values (3). Put

 epsilon=(1+eta)/(a^2 b(1-b eta))>0,
 X_i=(xB_i,sqrt(epsilon)xA_i),
 G_i=(gB_i,sqrt(epsilon)gA_i), F_i=fB_i+epsilon fA_i.

The base array is the legal cut just described. The auxiliary array has
zero normal xA_j-gA_j except at j=3, where it is-ab(c-eta)<=0 because
c-eta=eta h3>=0. The only possible positive auxiliary residual is

 Q_A(1,3)=a^2 b(1-b eta)(c-eta).

The corresponding base residual is-(1+eta)(c-eta), and epsilon
cancels their sum exactly. All other auxiliary residuals, including
optimum pairs, are nonpositive: at the only nonzero target normal, every
source gradient except-a is at least-ab eta. Thus all original mixed
Q are nonpositive. If h3=0 the normal vanishes and the same verification
still holds; no division by c-eta is used. Function (7) realizes the data.

Both terminal positions equal their gradients. Define

 Jquad=[(1-b eta)Q^2+(1+eta)/(a^2 b)]/(1+b).

The exact energy is

 V_h=Q^2-C^2 eta^2+epsilon(1-C^2 a^2 b^2 eta^2)
    =(1+b)/(1-b eta) [Jquad-C^2 eta^2].                (23)

On the stated a,b intervals,

 1/(a^2 b)<25/3<b Q^2.

For the first inequality, 25*1521*79=3003975>3000000;
for the second, b Q^2> (79/100)*4^2=316/25>25/3.
Therefore Jquad decreases strictly with eta. Using (22), a,b ranges,
Q<17/4, and 1-b eta>0 gives

 Jquad < [(1-(79/100)(9/19))(17/4)^2
                       +(1+9/19)(25/3)]/(179/100)
       =2150863/163248<53/4.                           (24)

The last inequality has positive cleared numerator
53*163248-4*2150863=48692. In contrast,

 C^2 eta^2>(193/25)^2(9/19)^2
         =3017169/225625>53/4,                        (25)

since 4*3017169-53*225625=110551. Thus (23) is strictly negative.
This closes the entire set (P), including its zero-step boundaries and
all capacities above the threshold.

## 7. Relation to the structural reversal task

For h in (P), rev(h) belongs to (R). Sections5 and6 exclude both by
actual smooth convex functions. These exclusions do not rely on the
unsigned inverse-certificate map, on the extra quadratic-equality
kernel, or on a certificate selection conjecture.

The unit-quadratic equality face only supplies a second kernel vector:
if q_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t) and C^2 q_4^2=1, then a full certificate
has Mq=0, since every unit-quadratic Q is zero. Together with Me=0
this reduces rank but supplies no sign for the transformed multipliers.
The existing full congruence still leaves a sign-selection problem.
No automatic sign theorem or four-step counterexample emerged from
that direct attempt. A bad transformed array alone would not decide
existential feasibility of the reversed cone.

The following remain open at the end of this allocated round:

* Full existential reversal for arbitrary nonnegative four-step words.
* A global ordinary-certificate selection that repairs nonterminal
  inverse signs, including the three-positive-step child at an endpoint
  saturated cut.
* Full threshold-seed existence or a global obstruction outside the
  concrete excluded regions.
* The original all-schedule asymptotic exact-profile problem.

No new fact is claimed, and there was no fact_submit allocation.

# Preserving curvature does not preserve the proposed continuation line

Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED counterexample, invocation70. This is the SAME third policy as the full report, not a new fourth policy. Correctness input: fact_id1252b8823bb5a290 and its strong-conjugate interpolation construction. Target claim: this declared preserving one-sided conjugate penalty keeps x=z+t*r with gradient delta*t*r on the negative side. The actual first post-entry gradient disproves that claim. No full-terminal claim follows.

## 2. The unchanged FULL word and a paid four-query entry

Fix ANY integer m>=7, tau=1/4, and set

    u=1/(4m), lambda=1+sqrt2,
    beta=1-u, c=lambda*(1+u), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(l+1)=(H_l,1+a*c^l,H_l), 0<=l<m,
    b_l=1+a*c^l, h=H_m, n=2^m-1.

Every child uses this SAME enclosing m and tau. No reset, padding,
truncation or permutation occurs. The first four original steps are
exactly (b_0,b_1,b_0,b_2); all n-4 remaining entries are kept.

Write S_l=sum H_l and v_h=a/(c-2). Direct induction on
1+S_(l+1)=2(1+S_l)+a*c^l gives

    1+S_l=v_h*c^l+(1-v_h)*2^l.

Here c-2>1/lambda>a, so 0<v_h<1, and 2<c<3.
Also beta>=27/28, a<1/2. Every first-occurrence bridge ratio
a*c^l/(1+S_l) is smaller than c-2<1. These are the original masses.

Define the literal entry data in R^3, with its standard basis e1,e2,e3:

    M=2+a, s=M-1=1+a, D=beta, E=beta*c,
    Delta=E-M,
    d=a/[2(1+beta)], p=e1,
    q=-d*e1+sqrt(a*d-d^2)*e2, Q=||q||^2=a*d,
    k=beta/(2M), r=-k*q+k*sqrt(Q)*e3, R=||r||^2=2k^2 Q,
    y=-a*e1-q,
    z=y-beta*q-M*r=y-(beta/2)*(q+sqrt(Q)*e3),
    tR=z+s*r.

All quantities are fixed from the known full word before GD. The entry
has M0=M1=1, first ratio a<1/2 and second bridge excess beta. Its third
ordinary block is the actual single step b_0, whose mass is M-1. Thus
this is the earlier Law1's j=0 entry, but its proof is fully repeated.

The apparent boundary cancellation is paid exactly:

    Delta=2u/[lambda(1+u)]-lambda*u^2>0,
    0<Delta<1/14,       0<k<1/4.

Indeed positivity is equivalent to lambda^2*u*(1+u)<2, which follows
from lambda^2<6 and u<=1/28. The upper bound follows by dropping the
negative term and using lambda>1 and u<=1/28. In particular E>M.

The identities needed for EVERY anchor are

    y.q=y.r=z.r=0,      z.q=-beta*Q/2,
    q.r=-kQ,           tR.r=sR,
    y_1=-a+d<0,
    z_1=-a+(1+beta/2)d<0,
    (tR)_1=-a+(1+beta-k)d<-a/2.

Set Dict=conv{0,p,q,r} and

    J(w)=max(0,p.w,q.w,r.w),
    F0(x)=max_(g in Dict){x.g-||g||^2/2}.

The four ORIGINAL queried jets from x_0=e1 are exactly

| query t | x_t | g_t | proximal point x_t-g_t | f_t |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | e1 | p | 0 | 1/2 |
| 1 | -a e1=y+q | q | y | Q/2 |
| 2 | y-beta q=tR+r | r | tR | sR+R/2 |
| 3 | z+r | r | z | R/2 |

At proximal0 every homogeneous plane has height0, and p is the unique
projection maximizer since x_0=p lies in Dict. At y, the q,r,zero
heights are0 and the p-height is negative. At tR the r-height is sR>0,
the q-height is (-beta+k)Q<0, and the p-height is negative by the last
display. At z the r and zero heights are0, the q-height is negative,
and the p-height is negative. These exhaustive guards select the stated
gradients; a support-plane tie does not make the envelope gradient
ambiguous. The updates are precisely b_0,b_1,b_0,b_2 and reach

    x_1=-a e1,
    x_2=y-beta q,
    x_3=x_2-(M-1)r=z+r,
    x_4=x_3-(1+E)r=z-E r.

For F0 the gradients are Euclidean projections onto Dict. If g is
this projection and w=x-g, its normal condition says
w.b<=w.g for EVERY b in Dict, not merely neighboring slopes. Then
F0(x)=J(w)+||g||^2/2. For any two actual samples, including optimum
samples and all future repetitions, direct expansion gives the COMPLETE
ordered identity

    Q_ij=<w_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                         (A)

The inequality is exactly the normal condition at j with b=g_i.
Thus every remote and optimum row is covered. Projection optimality at
two points gives <x-x',g-g'> >=||g-g'||^2; this proves global1-smoothness
and the attained maximum gives convexity and C1 differentiability.
Dict has interior because p,q,r are independent, and on that interior
the projection is the identity. Smoothness1 is sharp.

This entry proof uses no earlier unverified claim. In Law1 terminology
M1=1 so its old tQ endpoint duplicates y and its terminal correction
weight is0; there is no unrestricted use of the earlier positive-weight
formula or omission of its theta<=1 requirement.


## 4. Policy2: maximal completion fixed BEFORE the full run

The original four queried jets of Section2, plus the zero optimum,
give precisely the affine anchor list

    (0,0), (y,0), (z,0), (tR,sR).

The initial query contributes another copy of(0,0); keep that physical
query although its identical affine term need only be printed once.
There is no omitted q-start anchor: its ordinary list is EMPTY, so the
first q anchor is exactly y. The FIRST r query contributes tR with
positive height sR and is retained. The following r query contributes z.

Freeze BEFORE GD the single function

    Hmax(v)=||v||^2/2+max(0,y.v,z.v,tR.v-sR),
    Fmax(x)=sup_v{x.v-Hmax(v)}.                       (B)

The max weights are1 and its offsets are0,0,0,-sR. There is no
function change at time4. Accepted1252 proves that(B) is the maximal
completion of THESE FOUR jets and preserves all of them. Directly,
at g=p only the0 anchor is needed, at g=q the y anchor is active,
and at g=r all0,y,z,tR terms are0. The identities
x_0=p+0, x_1=q+y, x_2=r+tR, x_3=r+z therefore give their exact
gradients and the four exact values in the table.

This fixes Fmax before the run from an explicitly declared four-query
reference prefix. Fmax need not preserve the future jets that F0 would
have produced after time4; they are DIFFERENT policies. In the actual
Fmax run EVERY preterminal jet, including all later ones proved below,
is a jet of this ONE frozen function. No later completion is applied.

### 4.1 Global function, minimum, radius and sharp smoothness

Hmax is finite continuous1-strongly convex with quadratic growth. For
each x its conjugate maximizer g(x) exists uniquely and satisfies
x in partial Hmax(g(x)). Strong subgradient inequalities at x,x'
give <x-x',g(x)-g(x')> >=||g(x)-g(x')||^2. Maximizing inequalities
give differentiability with grad Fmax=g, so Fmax is convex C1 globally
1-smooth. This is also the construction proof of accepted1252.

At v=0 the active affine slopes are precisely0,y,z; the tR term has
strictly negative offset-sR. Consequently

    argmin Fmax=conv{0,y,z},         min Fmax=0.       (C)

To justify completeness, Fmax is differentiable, so x minimizes it
iff grad Fmax(x)=0 iff x belongs to partial Hmax(0). Hmax(0)=0 then
gives the minimum value0. Both nonzero vertices in(C) have negative
first coordinate; hence origin is the unique closest minimizer to e1
and the TRUE radius is exactly1. No smaller denominator is assumed.

For small v=t e1, t>0, the y,z terms are strictly negative and the
tR term remains strictly negative. A nonempty open neighborhood has
Hmax(v)=||v||^2/2. Its conjugate therefore has an open Hessian-I
region. The global smoothness bound1 is SHARP.


## 5. Policy3: a preserving curvature mutation and its exact line exit

A different actual mechanism can lower curvature after the entry.
Define, BEFORE GD and on the SAME full H_m,

    eta=4M/Delta>0,
    Heta(v)=Hmax(v)+(eta/2)*min(v_3,0)^2,
    Feta=Heta^*,             x_0=e1.                 (H)

This changes the function chosen at the beginning, never during GD.
The original affine slopes, weights and offsets are EXACTLY those in
(B); the extra one-sided quadratic is explicit. It is convex C1 and
vanishes with zero gradient when v_3>=0. Old p,q have v_3=0 and old
r has v_3=k*sqrt(Q)>0, so the added term and its derivative vanish
at EVERY original entry gradient. It preserves every value and
subgradient condition for all four original entry jets, including both
r queries and the FIRST r anchor tR. Thus the run still reaches x_4.

Heta remains finite1-strongly convex with quadratic growth. The same
strong-subgradient proof as Section4 gives a finite convex C1 globally
1-smooth Feta. At v=0 the extra term has derivative0, so the COMPLETE
minimum set is STILL conv{0,y,z}, minimum0 and TRUE radius1. In an
open small positive-v_3, positive-e1 neighborhood all affine terms
except0 are negative and the extra term is0; hence smoothness1 is
again sharp. Every actual jet of Feta obeys all original directed rows
by the same necessity proof, including the first new jet below.

The proposed continuation was to keep x=z+t r and use slower negative
side gradient delta*t*r, with

    delta=1/(1+eta/2)=Delta/(E+M).

On the proposed line at x_4=z-E r it asks for
v_prop=-delta*E*r. At that v, 0,y,z affine anchors tie at0 and tR
is strictly below0. Because y is nonzero and perpendicular to q,e3,
x_4-v_prop-eta*(v_prop)_3*e3 has y-component EXACTLY y. An active
convex combination of0,y,z must therefore give weight0 to0 and be
(1-omega)y+omega*z, with 0<=omega<=1. Solving its two remaining
components gives

    omega=1-eta*delta*E/(2M)
         =(M-E)/(E+M)=-Delta/(E+M)<0.                (I)

Thus the proposed gradient does NOT satisfy x_4 in partial Heta(v_prop).
This is an original retained-anchor cap obstruction at the first new
query, preceding any unscreened suffix. For a general eta>=0 the same
negative-line expression could be legal here ONLY if

    eta<=2M/(E-M).

Our declared eta is twice that cap. Retuning independent affine
heights is not the attempted repair; (H) changes the actual curvature.
The exact failure is its invariant-line mapping, not existence or
smoothness of the static function, and not all off-line continuations.

The ACTUAL first new gradient is explicit:

    g_eta,4=k*Delta*q
             -k*(M+E)/(1+eta)*sqrt(Q)*e3.            (J)

It has negative third coordinate. Its y and0 affine values are0.
Since eta>2M/Delta,

    z.g_eta,4=-M*k^2 Q[Delta-(M+E)/(1+eta)]<0,
    tR.g_eta,4-sR=z.g_eta,4+s(r.g_eta,4-R)<0,
    r.g_eta,4=-k^2 Q[Delta+(M+E)/(1+eta)]<0.

Consequently y is active and
x_4=y+g_eta,4+eta*(g_eta,4)_3*e3 is its actual strong subgradient
condition. Uniqueness gives(J), with exact actual value

    2Feta(x_4)=k^2 Q[Delta^2+(M+E)^2/(1+eta)].        (K)

The q and e3 coefficients in(J) have unequal absolute values, so(J)
is not parallel to r. The VERY NEXT initialized update on this changed
function is x_5=x_4-b_0*g_eta,4 and leaves the claimed scalar line.

No actual terminal value of Feta on its off-line suffix is claimed.
The paid result is the exact scoped obstruction(I) and actual exit(J),
not a fabricated full-run success or W_n upper. Its remaining off-line
region mapping is a new mathematical task; the current allocation does

# Fixed sparse-cycle pilot: complete finite observations, no theorem

All five registered jobs finished, with exit code0, within their externally
enforced six-second child caps. The m8 dual-coordinate calibration passed at
every query. The independent higher-precision m10 run agreed throughout the
whole trajectory. The observed terminal payoffs are far below the original
benchmark at all four tested indices, even after using an upper bound for the
benchmark denominator. These are numerical observations, not verified facts,
interval enclosures, an asymptotic obstruction, or the original all-word theorem.

The canonical ledger is CLOSED_EXHAUSTED: 5/5 jobs, 30/30 accounted seconds,
zero further authorized jobs or seconds. There was no correction, failed job,
timeout, installation, solver, benchmark DP, formal submission, or extra run.

## Fixed model and calibration

The full pre-job plan is PLAN.md, shared plan3933b8a70dfca924. Its immutable
source hashes are in freeze_manifest.json. All artifacts are under
/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/sparse_cycle_discovery_20261001/.
The source-only update to final draft37ec6ea9a52bfbdf is accounted for in
PROVENANCE_ADDENDUM.md; no mathematical object changed and no case was replayed.

For each fixed enclosing m, lambda=1+sqrt2, beta=1-1/m,
c=lambda(1+1/m), t_j=1+(beta/c)c^j, d=1+beta*c^(m-2),
N=ceil(pi sqrt(d/6)), theta=pi/(2N), mu=sin(theta)^2,
v_i=cos((2i+1)theta), and z=cos(theta). Starting at x_0=v, the original
word H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,t_j,H_j) is run with the SAME m in every child.
The function is F(x)=sum_i(x_i-x_(i+1 mod N))_+^2/8, with declared L=1.
There is no recalibration to the least smoothness constant and no state reset.

PLAN.md derives the vertex gradient g_i=(r_i-r_(i-1))/4, with
y_i=x_i-x_(i+1) and r_i=max(y_i,0), and independently derives the edge update
y'_i=y_i-(h/4)(2r_i-r_(i-1)-r_(i+1)). Convexity follows from convexity of the
squared positive part. The bound ||D||<=2 for the cycle incidence D, together
with the 1-Lipschitz positive-part map, gives a gradient Lipschitz constant
at most ||D||^2/4<=1. These elementary formulas are joint diagnostic reasoning,
not separately verifier-accepted facts. The minimum set is the constants and
R^2=||v||^2=N/2, as derived in the plan. The initial gradient equals mu*v.

The first child is calibrated against both its running scalar product and
A_m=product_(j=0)^(m-2)(1-mu*t_j)^(2^(m-2-j)). Its factors are checked positive.
The postcentral state is calibrated against -b_m*v, where b_m=A_m(mu*d-1)>0.
The next two vertex and edge states are checked against formulas rederived
in PLAN.md, including the active counts1,3,5. None of the checks substitutes
the scalar formula for the actual evolved vertex or edge state.

At m8, N=25, both implementations completed255 updates and256 gradient
queries each. Maximum discrepancies over ALL queries were:

| Check | Maximum absolute error |
|---|---:|
| Independently evolved vertex states | 2.303713e-15 |
| Vertex differences versus independent edge states | 4.461709e-15 |
| Gradients | 2.185752e-15 |
| Objective values | 1.070650e-17 |
| Running first-child scalar formula | 2.442491e-15 |
| Vertex sum | 2.220446e-15 |
| Independent edge sum | 9.870577e-16 |

All registered equality, factor, sign, and sector checks passed. All m8 raw
queries retain both full states, the reconstructed edge-coordinate vertex
state, actual values, and diagnostics. Discovery first-child and central
checks also passed; m14's largest first-child coordinate discrepancy was
8.053836e-13 and its central discrepancy was3.167605e-13.

## Terminal quantities from the original trajectories

The table uses the actual terminal F(x_n), n=2^m-1. V_m=4F(x_n)/N.
The draft's J_m is recovered WITHOUT another trajectory:

    J_m = 8N F(x_n)/b_m^2,
    V_m = b_m^2 J_m/(2N^2).

Indeed positive homogeneity gives x_n=b_m Phi_(m-1)(-v), so its positive
terminal edge vector is b_m times the draft's normalized suffix edge vector.
This uses the whole inherited original run. The scalar conversion was
computed inside each charged job. The energy-retention column is actual
F(x_n)/F(x_(2^(m-1))); its independently equivalent formula J_m/(4Nz^2)
was also recorded. No additional normalized-suffix evaluation was performed.

The midpoint supplies no terminal lower bound. Four declining J values do
not determine whether J_m tends to zero, stays bounded away from zero, or
changes behavior later.

## Original all-split benchmark, with correct inequality directions

Only accepted fact04219554e41d9750 is used. Its proof gives
B_(2^m-1)>=lambda^m, and its unconditional dyadic-ray enclosure at b=1,j=0
gives B_(2^m-1)<=lambda^m Z_1<=(41209/10000)lambda^m, since B_1=4.
No condition on the seed in that fact is used. All comparison values were
evaluated INSIDE the registered jobs, using these closed forms and no DP.

| 14 | 9.634824306554106e-7 | 3.970414748487882e-6 |

Every observed UPPER product is below1, making these finite instances weak
benchmark witnesses, subject to numerical accuracy. This conclusion does not
misuse a lower product below1. It is not a floating-point-certified rigorous
inequality. The midpoint UPPER products at m12 and m14 are1.666070184560507
and3.268735120544179, respectively; their exceeding1 does NOT establish that
either midpoint beats the exact benchmark. Their lower products are only
0.4042976496785914 and0.7932090369929333.

## Observed sectors and a useful analytical lead

The complete scalar logs show no first-child sector change. At the central
update all N edge signs switch from the initial N-1-edge path to the one
closing edge. Subsequent recorded sector changes each alter exactly TWO
edges and increase the active count by TWO:1,3,5,... through the terminal
active count listed below. Thus these finite numerical runs show only
activation after the reversal, with no observed later deactivation.

| m | Queries with a sector change, including reversal | Total individual edge-status changes | Terminal active count | Last change at physical index |

| 14 | 183 | 735 | 365 | 15040 |

sector_events_copied.json contains every such event copied from the registered
query diagnostics, including original physical index, adjacent steps, and F.
The increasing active set is a potential invariant for an analytical argument,
not an all-depth fact established by these computations.

Energy itself is not numerically monotone. At m10, F goes from
3.6023782185404033e-7 at k=767 to2.292179015911102e-6 at k=768, after the
step839.3417655365569; active counts go53 to55. At m14, F goes from
1.517398814784241e-9 at k=14335 to5.69628975236541e-9 at k=14336, after
step12452.004892731098; active counts go361 to363. A proof must accommodate
these actual large-step bursts even if it succeeds in proving nested activity.
No monotone-energy or total-clock approximation is licensed by the data.

## Independent higher-precision comparison

Job5 recomputed the entire m10 run in independently implemented EDGE
coordinates, including pi, sqrt2, every step constant, N, the initial vector,
and the recursively assembled word. N again rounded to61. NumPy calls the
storage dtype float128, but the recorded significand is64 bits, not113;
eps=1.084202172485504434e-19. The binary64 runs have53-bit significands.
The comparison uses all1,024 queries and all full stored vectors. It is a
whole-trajectory recomputation, not a terminal reevaluation or interval proof.

Maximum absolute vertex discrepancy was1.4540143855644797e-14; maximum
edge discrepancy2.4431411754788357e-14; maximum gradient discrepancy
1.0893171715199127e-14; maximum objective discrepancy5.0606645178198216e-17;
maximum relative objective discrepancy5.171618537049714e-13. There were
ZERO sector-disagreement queries. All registered checks passed.
The higher-precision terminal values were
V_10=3.6803867276941625466e-10 and J_10=0.0028998481105466267767.
This supports accuracy of the finite m10 observations but provides no
independent precision check for m12 or m14 and no mathematical error enclosure.

There were no NumPy overflow, underflow, invalid or division callbacks,
no observed subnormals, and no registered cancellation-near-tie indicators
in any completed case. The flags have exactly the scope stated in PLAN.md;
they do not certify absence of every possible libm/Python-scalar rounding event.

## Raw records, counts, and timing scopes

All original stdout/stderr and query bytes were losslessly compressed BEFORE
interpretation. Every gzip file's checksum and decompressed bytes/checksum
passed archive_audit.json. Original stderr is empty in all five jobs.

Measured child transport totals11.269026977970498 seconds. Within the children,

The budget remains charged at30 seconds, with NO refund based on measured

## Remaining task and exact scope of closure

This pilot completes the assigned finite discovery. It supplies calibrated
data and a candidate nested-activity mechanism, but no verified estimate for
the all-depth chronological J_m. An analytical proof still has to control the
actual suffix, including its growing active set and nonmonotone large-step
energy excursions, with the inherited state and original physical clock.
Neither a positive lower limit nor a vanishing upper limit for J_m is proved.

The single allocated round ends with these shared records. No fact_submit
was authorized or used, so there is no new fact_id and no formal success claim.

remain OPEN. This closes only this exhausted numerical allocation.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000988 -->
# Variable-step dense certificates and a complete alternating-family obstruction

Status: complete joint proof DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. This is max2 round36,
the second and final analytical round allocated after completed34 by
TASK.md and master_guidance604b16e76c7981e3. Scope update
c3f16cd4617d4d93 and source-scope note60f0c805e0e856b2 leave that
ceiling unchanged. The latter's required ordered-Q translation and
fresh variable-step proof are supplied explicitly below. No numerical evaluation,
solver, machine algebra, replay, formal-verifier call, installation,
consult or subordinate agent was used. The original Goal remains OPEN.

The frozen pairs remain r=5,b=1 and r=6,b=1. Here b in the phrase
"ray b=1" is the integer ray parameter; the alternating step excess
called b below is a different, explicitly local real parameter.

Accepted inputs: fact04219554e41d9750, Sections2,3,10--13, for the
benchmark recurrences, balanced primitive scalar rule, full original
certificate identity, physical joins and substitution; and
fact1252b8823bb5a290 for the original smooth interpolation convention.
No unverified shared result is a premise. All new claims used together
are proved in this single draft. The earlier round35 triangle and
two-tight-cut arguments are not used.

This work changes the attempted certificate mechanism. Both strict
triangular halves of the auxiliary multiplier array are factorized,
with factors depending on the actual variable steps. The whole matrix
remainder is zero, and every ordered multiplier is retained. Two
nonconstant, nonbasic physical words have complete strictly positive
arrays of this kind. They are weak seeds: the ENTIRE alternating
specialization of this declared positive-factor, zero-remainder family
has capacity below the appropriate benchmark. This is an obstruction
to the declared ansatz, not to every full certificate or every seed.

All new local notation in this document has the implicit suffix _ls36.
In particular U_i,D_i below are factor sequences, not the benchmark
U_n^ZJ or any earlier trajectory bound. P_i is prefix capacity, R_i
is a reciprocal product, and p_i is an auxiliary scalar product.

## 1. Original certificate, orientation, and the chosen family

For a word h of length r, let s=sum_(i<r)h_i and A=1+s. Retain all
r+1 independent formal gradients g_0,...,g_r and all formal values f_i.
Set

 x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,
 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
 V_h=2sum_(i<r)h_i(f_i-f_r)
       +sum_(i<r)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sA||g_r||^2.

The accepted full certificate interface is

 Lambda_ij>=0 (i!=j), Lambda_ii=0,
 e=(1,...,1)^T,
 T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
 L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
 L_flow^T e=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,
 D_prim=diag(h_i(2-h_i) for i<r, -s(s+2)),
 M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2 >=0 on R^(r+1).       (1)

Accepted04219554e41d9750 proves the original-space identity

 V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_r)>.      (2)

Introduce the differently oriented residual

 Qbar_ij=-2Q_ji
        =2(f_i-f_j)-2<g_j,x_i-x_j>-||g_i-g_j||^2.

We search for an EXACT identity

                       V_h=sum_(i!=j)mu_ij Qbar_ij.          (3)

Then the original array is Lambda_ij=2mu_ji, not 2mu_ij, and its
remainder is M_prim=0 on the whole original space.

The declared family has strictly positive excesses t_i=h_i-1, i<r.
Choose positive U_0,...,U_(r-1) and D_1,...,D_r, set U_-1=0 and
D_(r+1)=0. Set t_r=0 and h_r=1 solely as auxiliary placeholders in
expressions multiplied by D_(r+1) or an empty sum. They are not an
extra physical step. For every 0<=i<j<=r define

 mu_ij=(U_i-U_(i-1))(D_j+t_j D_(j+1)),
mu_ji=(U_(i-1)+t_i U_i)(D_j-D_(j+1)).                      (4)

Equivalently, the original ordered entries are, for every i<j,

 Lambda_ij=2(U_(i-1)+t_i U_i)(D_j-D_(j+1)),
 Lambda_ji=2(U_i-U_(i-1))(D_j+t_j D_(j+1)).                 (4a)

These formulas define EVERY off-diagonal entry, and mu_ii=0. The
family to be excluded below consists of all choices of these positive
factors for which all entries are nonnegative and identity(3) holds.
It is not assumed that every original certificate admits this form.

Nonnegative entries in(4) force U_i>=U_(i-1) and D_j>=D_(j+1):
use j=r in the first formula, and i=0 in the second, respectively.
Conversely these two monotonicities make every entry nonnegative.

## 2. Why the variable-step factorization cancels all mixed terms

For i<j the two telescoping sums are

 sum_(a<=i)mu_(a,j)=U_i(D_j+t_j D_(j+1)),
 sum_(a>j)mu_(a,i)=(U_(i-1)+t_i U_i)D_(j+1).

The coefficient of <g_i,g_j> in sum mu Qbar is exactly

 2(mu_ij+mu_ji)
 -2h_i sum_(a<=i)mu_(a,j)+2h_j sum_(a>j)mu_(a,i).           (5)

Expansion of(4) gives

 mu_ij+mu_ji
 =h_i U_i(D_j+t_j D_(j+1))
       -h_j(U_(i-1)+t_i U_i)D_(j+1),

so(5) is zero for every pair. This calculation allows unequal steps;
it is not an appeal to the constant-step source theorem.

Let d_i=sum_(j!=i)(mu_ij-mu_ji), so that the coefficient of f_i is
2d_i. For i<r put p_(i+1)=U_i D_(i+1), with p_0=0. The weights
entering i from smaller and larger indices are, respectively,

I_i=U_(i-1)(D_i+t_i D_(i+1)) for 1<=i<r,
 J_i=(U_(i-1)+t_i U_i)D_(i+1),

with I_0=0. Therefore

              t_i J_i-I_i=t_i^2 p_(i+1)-p_i.                (6)

If d_i=h_i, the coefficient of ||g_i||^2 is

 -d_i-2I_i-2J_i+2h_i J_i
                   =-h_i+2(t_i^2 p_(i+1)-p_i).             (7)

At the terminal, d_r=-s, the incoming weight is p_r, and its squared
gradient coefficient is s-2p_r. These formulas also directly check the
terminal orientation of the original array.

## 3. Complete variable-step construction with explicit sign tests

For given t_i>0 define

 P_k=1+sum_(i<k)(1+t_i),
 R_k=product_(i<k)t_i^(-1),
 p_k=(R_k^2-1)/2,       0<=k<=r.

Thus P_0=R_0=1 and P_r=A. Assume

 R_r=P_r,
 R_k>1 and P_k>R_k for 1<=k<r.                             (8)

Set U_0=1, and for 1<=k<r set

 U_k/U_(k-1)
   = (P_k+R_k)(1+R_(k+1))/((1+R_k)(P_k-R_k)),
 D_k=p_k/U_(k-1) for 1<=k<=r.                              (9)

All these factors are positive. Their successive D ratios are

 D_(k+1)/D_k
   = (P_k-R_k)(R_(k+1)-1)/((R_k-1)(P_k+R_k)).              (10)

For the exact sign check, U_k>=U_(k-1) and D_k>=D_(k+1) are equivalent
to the following two scalar inequalities for each 1<=k<r:

 -R_k(2+R_k+R_(k+1))
       <=P_k(R_(k+1)-R_k)
       <=R_k(R_(k+1)+R_k-2).                               (11)

No matrix PSD test is being replaced by these inequalities: the FULL
matrix will be proved identically zero. Inequalities(11) concern all
of the actual coefficient signs of this particular factorization.

Here is the complete value-flow verification. Define

 u_k=(sum_(i<k)h_i U_i)/U_k,
 v_k=(sum_(j=k+2)^r h_(j-1)D_j)/D_(k+1).

The exact identities are

 u_k=(P_k-R_k)/(1+R_(k+1)),
 v_k=(P_(k+1)-R_(k+1))/(R_(k+1)-1).                        (12)

For u, start from u_0=0 and use
u_k=(U_(k-1)/U_k)(u_(k-1)+h_(k-1)). Inserting the preceding
formula for u_(k-1) gives
u_(k-1)+h_(k-1)=(P_k+R_k)/(1+R_k), and then(9) proves(12).
For v, start from v_(r-1)=0, which agrees with R_r=P_r, and use
v_(k-1)=(D_(k+1)/D_k)(h_k+v_k). The factor h_k+v_k equals
(P_k+R_k)/(R_(k+1)-1). Substitution in(10) proves the asserted
formula for v_(k-1). Thus both identities hold, including the endpoints.

The sum of the flows over indices 0,...,k telescopes across that cut:

 sum_(i<=k)d_i
   =p_(k+1)(1-t_k+v_k-u_k)=P_(k+1)-1.                     (13)

To check the last equality explicitly, write R=R_(k+1), P=P_k,
t=t_k, so that R_k=tR and P_(k+1)=P+1+t. Then

 p_(k+1)(v_k-u_k)
   =[(R+1)(P+1+t-R)-(R-1)(P-tR)]/2
   =P+t+(t-1)(R^2-1)/2.

Adding p_(k+1)(1-t) leaves P+t=P_(k+1)-1, as claimed.
Differences of(13) give d_i=h_i for i<r, and total flow zero gives
d_r=-s. The function coefficients are now all correct.

The definition of R gives

        t_i^2 p_(i+1)-p_i=(1-t_i^2)/2.

Equation(7) becomes -h_i+1-t_i^2=-h_i t_i, the required coefficient
in V_h. At the terminal, R_r=A gives p_r=(A^2-1)/2, so
s-2p_r=-sA. Section2 cancels all mixed products. This proves(3) as
a polynomial identity in ALL values and ALL original gradients.

Consequently(8)--(11), together with(4) and(9), supply a full original
certificate with Lambda=2mu^T and M_prim=0, with no equality or
identification of formal coordinates. Strict inequalities in the two
factor monotonicities give strictly positive entries on all r(r+1)
ordered off-diagonal pairs.

## 4. Necessary restrictions on the ENTIRE declared factorized family

This section does not assume the special construction(8)--(9). Start
with ANY positive factors in(4) for which all entries are nonnegative
and the original flow and zero-remainder identity hold.

The value coefficients give d_i=h_i for i<r and d_r=-s. Comparing(7)
with the desired -h_i t_i yields

             t_i^2(2p_(i+1)+1)=2p_i+1,   p_0=0.           (14)

At the terminal, s-2p_r=-sA gives 2p_r+1=A^2. Multiplying(14)
therefore proves the necessary exact balance

                         A product_(i<r)t_i=1.             (15)

The positive factors make p_1=U_0D_1>0, so(14) at i=0 forces
0<t_0<1. When r>=2 define

 z=(sum_(j=2)^r h_(j-1)D_j)/D_1>0.

The first value flow is h_0=p_1(1-t_0+z). Substitution of
p_1=(t_0^(-2)-1)/2 gives

                z=(t_0^2+2t_0-1)/(1-t_0)>0.

It follows that EVERY member of this declared family with r>=2 obeys

                         sqrt(2)-1<t_0<1.                  (16)

This is a necessary condition on this positive-factor representation,
not a claimed necessary condition on all primitive schedules.

As a concrete failed high-mass migration, put t=(1/3,b,1/3,b,1/3),
where b is the unique positive root of 2b^3+7b^2-27=0. The polynomial
is increasing on b>0 and has values -9/2 at 3/2 and 46/27 at 5/3.
Thus 3/2<b<5/3, A=7+2b>10, and(15) holds exactly. Nevertheless the
required z above would be -1/3. It cannot equal a strictly positive
weighted sum. Unit-quadratic balance and a large capacity alone do
not extend the source factorization. This diagnostic is NOT asserted
to pass every other primitive test or to be a full-cone survivor.

## 5. Scope-complete obstruction at five alternating steps

Consider every member of the declared factorized family whose actual
physical word is

                  h=(1+a,1+b,1+a,1+b,1+a),  a,b>0.

Here A=6+3a+2b. Equations(15)--(16) force

         A a^3 b^2=1,       sqrt(2)-1<a<1.                 (17)

We prove the strict cap

                              A<29/3.                      (18)

Suppose instead A>=29/3 and write b=(A-6-3a)/2. For fixed
a in [sqrt(2)-1,1], the expression

                    F(A,a)=A a^3(A-6-3a)^2/4

is strictly increasing in A on A>=29/3. All factors are positive
there. At A=29/3, the derivative of a^3(11/3-3a)^2 has sign
11/3-5a on this interval: its exact derivative is
3a^2(11/3-3a)(11/3-5a). Thus its minimum is attained at one of
the two endpoints. At a=1, F(29/3,1)=29/27>1. At a=sqrt(2)-1,
direct multiplication gives

 F(29/3,sqrt(2)-1)=(77285sqrt(2)-109243)/54>1,

because

              2*77285^2-109297^2=108241>0.

Both quantities being compared before squaring are positive. Hence
F(A,a)>1 throughout the assumed region, contradicting(17). This
proves(18) for the ENTIRE declared five-step alternating family,
with arbitrary positive factors and all of their allowed boundary
zero multipliers. No recurrence(9) or extra symmetry of the array is
needed for this obstruction.

## 6. Scope-complete obstruction at six alternating steps

Now consider every member of the same declared family whose word is

               h=(1+a,1+b,1+a,1+b,1+a,1+b),  a,b>0.

Here A=7+3a+3b, and(15)--(16) give

            A(ab)^3=1,       sqrt(2)-1<a<1.                (19)

We prove

                              A<23/2.                      (20)

If A>=23/2, write b=(A-7)/3-a. For each fixed admissible a,
A[a((A-7)/3-a)]^3 is strictly increasing in A in this region.
At A=23/2 the product a(3/2-a) is a positive concave quadratic
in a, so its minimum on [sqrt(2)-1,1] occurs at an endpoint.
The endpoint a=1 gives A(ab)^3=23/16>1. The other gives

 (23/2)[(sqrt(2)-1)(5/2-sqrt(2))]^3
                    =(54901sqrt(2)-77625)/16>1,

since

                2*54901^2-77641^2=114721>0.

Again all compared sides are positive. This contradicts(19), proving
(20) for the entire declared six-step alternating family.

## 7. Two explicit nonbasic certificates; all 30/42 signs and full PSD

The exclusions above are not the consequence of an empty ansatz. We
now give one complete nonconstant algebraic certificate at each length.
Formulas(4),(8)--(9) are finite explicit formulas for EVERY multiplier;
no linear system, optimizer or uncomputed PSD condition remains.

Five steps: set a=2/3 and let b be the unique positive root of

                         16b^3+64b^2-27=0.

The polynomial is strictly increasing on b>0. Its values at 3/5 and
5/8 are -63/125 and 61/32, so

       3/5<b<5/8,
       h=(5/3,1+b,5/3,1+b,5/3),
       A=8+2b,       46/5<A<37/4.                          (21)

The polynomial equation is exactly A(2/3)^3 b^2=1, hence R_5=A.
Define the factors by(9) and all 30 original multipliers by(4) and
Lambda_ij=2mu_ji.

Six steps: set a=2/3 and let b be the unique positive root of

                          8b^4+24b^3-9=0.

Its derivative is positive on b>0. At b=2/3 the value is -25/81;
at b=7/10 it is 1441/1250. Therefore

       2/3<b<7/10,
       h=(5/3,1+b,5/3,1+b,5/3,1+b),
       A=9+3b,       11<A<111/10.                          (22)

Its defining equation is exactly A[(2/3)b]^3=1, hence R_6=A.
Use(9),(4) to define all 42 original multipliers.

We check EVERY hypothesis in Section3 with exact bounds. In both
examples all t_i lie in (or at the interior rational point of)
[3/5,7/10], and R_k>1 because t_i<1. The strict prefix inequalities
P_k>R_k in the five-step case follow from

 P_1=8/3>3/2=R_1,
 P_2>64/15>5/2>R_2,
 P_3>89/15>15/4>R_3,
 P_4-R_4=(A-5)/3>0.

In the six-step case they follow from

 P_1=8/3>3/2=R_1,
 P_2>13/3>9/4>R_2,
 P_3>6>27/8>R_3,
 P_4>23/3>81/16>R_4,
 P_5-R_5=(1-b)A-(1+b)>8/5.

The last inequalities use terminal balance, not an approximate root.
Thus every denominator in(9)--(10) is strictly positive.

Because R_(k+1)>R_k, the first inequality in(11) is strict. For
the second inequality define

 E_k=(1+t_k)R_k-(1-t_k)P_k-2t_k.

It is exactly the second sign inequality after multiplication by
positive factors. At k=1, direct substitution in both examples gives

                       E_1=(13b-7)/6>2/15.

For k>=2 the common rational interval yields

 E_k >= H_k:=(8/5)(10/7)^k-9/5-17k/25.

Here R_k>=(10/7)^k, P_k<=1+17k/10, 1+t_k>=8/5,
1-t_k<=2/5 and 2t_k<=7/5. The lower bound is strictly positive:

 H_2=129/1225>0,
 H_(k+1)-H_k=(24/35)(10/7)^k-17/25>=1/175>0.

Consequently U_0>0, all successive U differences are positive,
all successive D differences are positive, and D_r>0=D_(r+1).
Every one of the 30 or 42 original off-diagonal entries is STRICTLY
positive. Section3 proves the exact flows and identity(3), so in the
notation of(1)

                         M_prim=0 on R^6 or R^7.            (23)

This is a full-space PSD statement, not a compressed Hessian test.
There are no zero physical steps in these examples and no coordinates
were deleted.

These words are genuinely nonbasic in the exact ConPP grammar of
accepted04219554e41d9750. To see this without a numerical comparison,
at any index j set P=1+sum_(i<j)h_i and D=1+sum_(i>j)h_i. Then
PD>=P+D-1=A-h_j. In the five-step example h_j<=5/3 and A>46/5,
and in the six-step example h_j<17/10 and A>11. In both cases

                  PD-A(h_j-1)>=A(2-h_j)-h_j>0.              (24)

But a nonempty word obtained by an exact ConPP join at its root has
some bridge h_j=1+d with dA=PD, by the defining identity in the
accepted join. Equation(24) rules out every such root. The examples
are also nonconstant since b differs from 2/3 by its isolating interval.
They are therefore actual dense nonbasic seeds, with a proved capacity
deficit rather than a claimed asymptotic gain.

## 8. Correct fixed-ray comparison and a finite uniform deficit

Let lambda=1+sqrt(2). The accepted scalar rules give

 C_2=Omega(1,lambda), C_5=lambda C_2,
 C_6=Omega(C_2,lambda^2).

The positive root C_2 satisfies X^2-(1+lambda)X-lambda=0.
We have lambda>239/99 because 2*99^2>140^2. At X=803/200,
the strict negativity of that polynomial follows from

                  239*200600-99*484209=6709>0.

Thus C_2>803/200. Also lambda>241/100, and hence

       C_5>193523/20000>29/3,
       C_5/(29/3)>580569/580000=1+569/580000.               (25)

For the six-step comparison, C_2>4 and lambda^2>29/5. The
polynomial for Omega(4,29/5) has value -23/80 at X=47/4,
so its positive root exceeds 47/4. Monotonicity of Omega yields

              C_6>47/4>23/2,
              C_6/(23/2)>47/46=1+1/46.                   (26)

The ray lower comparison needed here is

                         Z_m>=C_(m-1) Z_1                 (27)

for every positive integer m. Here is a direct proof from the original
recurrences, with no continuous phase limit. Expand a maximizing B_n
tree. Replace EACH primitive empty leaf by an original maximizing
primitive tree of length m-1 and its ONE dominant empty leaf by a
maximizing dominant tree of that same length. The resulting original
tree has m(n+1)-1 internal nodes. The primitive masses are C_(m-1)
and the terminal dominant mass is B_(m-1)>=C_(m-1). Homogeneity and
monotonicity at each join give

                    B_(m(n+1)-1)>=C_(m-1) B_n.

Take n=2^j-1, divide by lambda^j, and use the accepted integer-ray
limits. This proves(27). Thus every actual seed in the declared
alternating families satisfies the STRICT WRONG-WAY comparisons

 r=5: Z_6/(A Z_1)>1+569/580000,
 r=6: Z_7/(A Z_1)>1+1/46.                                 (28)

In particular neither family can satisfy the required A>Z_6/Z_1 or
A>Z_7/Z_1 on the frozen b=1 rays. These comparisons do not transfer
the four-step193/25 threshold or assume any unproved phase optimum.

There is a stronger finite physical statement. Let h be ANY actual
certificate from one of these families. Apply the accepted physical
substitution to a fixed maximizing B_n tree, using this seed of
capacity A. Keep the ONE dominant empty leaf untouched. The actual
word H_A has length mn, with m=r+1, and exact all-function value

                          W_(mn)(H_A)=1/D_A.

Let C=C_r. Repeat the SAME substitution using an original maximizing
basic primitive seed of length r and capacity C. This produces an
original basic dominant tree of length mn and mass D_C, so
B_(mn)>=D_C. The accepted original-coordinate joins and final square
remain in both constructions. Since C>A, monotonicity at the joins
gives D_C>D_A for every n>=1. Therefore

                W_(mn)(H_A)>1/B_(mn),  EVERY n>=1.          (29)

The square-root defect bound also gives, for n>=1,

 B_(mn)W_(mn)(H_A)
   >=[(sqrt(C B_n)-(sqrt(C)-1))^2]/(A B_n)
   >=(C/A)(1-B_n^(-1/2))^2.                               (30)

This pays for the unchanged dominant leaf explicitly. Both(25) and
(26) imply C/A>1+1/2000. On n=2^j-1, the accepted elementary
bound B_n>=2n+1 and 2^30>10^9 imply, for every j>=30,
B_n>400000000, hence B_n^(-1/2)<1/20000. Inserting this in(30)
and using

 (1+1/2000)(1-1/20000)^2
   >(1+1/2000)(1-1/10000)>1+1/4000

proves the uniform finite statement

 W_(m(2^j-1))(H_A)
       >(1+1/4000) U_(m(2^j-1))^ZJ,  EVERY integer j>=30,
       m=6 or m=7 respectively.                            (31)

The actual horizons are 6*2^j-6 and 7*2^j-7. This is a deficit of
this explicit seed-substitution mechanism, not a lower bound against
arbitrary schedules and not a solution of the original Goal.

## 9. What is closed and what remains

The source-inspired ansatz has been fully attempted, not left as a
generic feasibility problem. It produces explicit nonconstant positive
physical words, all multipliers, exact flow and full zero PSD remainder.
It also has an exact scope-complete obstruction on both declared
alternating word families. Every member of those families loses even
to a same-length basic capacity, and its accepted physical lift is
uniformly weaker than the original benchmark at the stated true
horizons. No numerical non-discovery is being used as evidence.

The ansatz restrictions matter: positive triangular factors, the exact
zero remainder, strictly positive excesses, and one of the two stated
alternating word patterns. A general full PSD remainder, a different
word pattern within the factorized class, different triangular ranks,
or a signed component decomposition with nonnegative final entries is
outside this exclusion. None of those alternatives is asserted to
work. There is no lossless reduction from the unrestricted full cone
to the family studied here.

No original-scope improving seed or full asymptotic separation was
obtained in the two-round allocation. The whole assignment has reached
its analytical ceiling36 (attempted ceiling37 including the historical
interruption). This document does not renew an allocation, relaunch a
worker, claim an inspected runtime exit, or request an unauthorized
formal submission. Its mathematics remains an unverified joint draft.

## 10. Primary source and retrieval record

Source of the factorization IDEA only: Salah R. Chikhi,
"Last-Iterate Performance of Gradient Descent and Relaxed Proximal
Point via s-Composability", arXiv:2609.13765v2 (20 September2026),
https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.13765v2, Section4, especially formulas(10)--(14).
The PDF was downloaded into this worker's downloads directory; all15
pages and the full constant-step certificate proof were read.

The paper's theorem concerns a CONSTANT h=1+t satisfying
(1+rh)t^r=1. Its residual Qbar_ij equals -2 times the project's Q_ji.
Its s-composability potential has additional terminal/optimum terms;
the history inequality in its Section4 is the intrinsic V_h used here.
Neither its constant-step result nor its proximal objective result is
silently used for a variable word or for the project's last iterate.
Sections2--7 above supply the complete variable-step proof instead.
Thus no additional external theorem is used as a black box or treated
as a fact-graph predecessor. If formal verification is later selected,
the source can be recorded as proof-pattern attribution, with the full
joint proof and accepted predecessors retained.

Matlas theorem searches returned unrelated statements twice; the
matched source was located through web search and opened on arXiv.
The first relative shared-store read failed with "No such file or
directory"; the absolute project shared store was then read.
The PDF text extraction command failed with "pdftotext: command not
found" and the consequent text search found no file. The already
present local pypdf reader then extracted all15 pages successfully.
No installation or mathematical computation was performed.
