# The new positive-energy thirty-one-step state fails the full affine next join

Status: **COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED PROOF CANDIDATE**, worker max,
completed-round target 30. No numerical/model/solver evaluation, arithmetic
replay, installation, subordinate agent, or formal verifier call was used.
This is not a fact. The accepted mathematical premises are
`1252b8823bb5a290` (original interpolation inequalities and their constructive
realization) and `b98bb807d514e80d` (the original benchmark and scalar lower
bounds). The previous complete joint finding `7ffb4d0516ace32f` is a source
of defining data, not an accepted predecessor. Its full derivation is
included as Appendix A of the combined evidence file.

## 1. Result and exact scope

Take the COMPLETE nine-dimensional thirty-one-step data from the defining
construction in section 2, for any sigma in [9999/10000,1]. This includes
its two old value deficits, two newly lifted atomic blocks, nonzero
terminal residual when sigma<1, and positive terminal energy when sigma<1.
Use two copies as the children of the physical next word

    h_63=(h_31,1+r,h_31), r=lambda^2 A.

Allow independent similarities E,F with E^T E=ell I, F^T F=nu I,
ell,nu>=0; their entire cross Gram; the physical relative translation;
ALL function values; one common minimizer; and a free unqueried terminal
gradient. Impose all original interpolation rows and the full Gram PSD
condition. There is no restriction on ambient dimension, orientation,
transverse coupling, value re-selection, or relative child scale.

The candidate conclusion is that this full model has only its zero-
trajectory face. In particular it has no positive-gap sixty-three-step
parent with these fixed child vectors. More precisely, if ell>0 and all
original queried rows were nonpositive, they would imply

    Q_(36,30)+Q_(30,31)+Q_(31,39)
       +Q_(39,38)+Q_(38,37)+Q_(37,36)
          > ell gamma^2 e / 6 > 0.                         (1)

Here gamma^2 is the spatial-square scale of the child's LAST ATOM and
e=(K-1)eta>0 is its unscaled atomic receiver energy. Every index in (1)
is queried in the actual parent. The contradiction uses additional
original queried rows to price the translation and independent scales;
it does not claim the six-cycle is positive for arbitrary unconstrained
maps. At ell=0, two rows at the physical bridge force nu=0.

This resolves this particular released fixed-child affine extension at
the level of a complete UNVERIFIED joint argument. It does not exclude
arbitrary sixty-three-step trajectories or upper-bound W_63. The positive
energy and changed minimizer hull do not repair this fixed geometry: the
obstruction cancels every freely selected value before using an optimum.

The same proof isolates a local geometric motif that prevents closure:
the first and last seven-step pieces impose priced positive gradient
dependencies, an adverse initial-to-first-right-gradient correlation,
and a final residual difference parallel to the terminal gradient.
Section 11 gives its explicit necessary state inequality. Intervening
geometry and value choices do not remove that interface.

## 2. Complete defining data, including values and distance state

All norms and inner products are Euclidean; smoothness is one. Set

    s=sqrt(2), lambda=1+s, theta=s-1,
    a=(311s-16)/300, b=(590+8s)/300, rho=599/600,
    z=b-1, K=a+rho, m=a+b, eta=z/K,
    A=1+2a+b=(143+105s)/50,
    r_0=theta A, r_1=A, r_2=lambda A, r=lambda^2 A.

Take orthogonal U,V with ||U||=1, ||V||^2=eta. Define

    p=mU+KV, w_atom=rho V,
    g=(U,U,V,w_atom),
    x=(p,bU+KV,KV,w_atom),
    f_atom,j=<g_j,x_j>-||g_j||^2/2, 0<=j<=3,
    y_j=x_j-g_j, E_j=<g_j,y_j>.

The word is h_3=(a,b,a). In particular

    y_1=zU+KV, y_2=(K-1)V, y_3=0,
    <V-U,y_1>=0,
    e=E_2=(K-1)eta, d_atom=(1-rho)e=e/600,
    <w_atom-V,y_2>=-d_atom,
    R^2=||p||^2=m^2+Kz, N=||w_atom||^2=rho^2 eta,
    D=<p,w_atom>=rho z.                                (2)

Fix sigma in the stated interval and put

    delta=1-sigma, chi=delta/sigma,
    alpha=sigma r_0 sqrt(N)/R, k=alpha^2.

On the direct sum of one line and the orthogonal complements of
w_atom and p, define the isometries

    L u=(<u,w_atom/sqrt(N)>,u-<u,w_atom/sqrt(N)>w_atom/sqrt(N),0),
    T v=(-<v,p/R>,0,v-<v,p/R>p/R).

Thus <Lu,alpha Tv>=-sigma r_0<u,w_atom><p,v>/R^2.
Set t=-delta r_0 Lw_atom=chi alpha Tp. The seven-step samples are

    X_i=Lx_i, G_i=Lg_i, F_i=f_atom,i,                   i=0..3,
    X_(4+i)=t+alpha Tx_i, G_(4+i)=alpha Tg_i,
    F_(4+i)=k f_atom,i,                                i=0..3.

The word is h_7=(h_3,1+r_0,h_3). Write P_1=X_0, w_1=G_7.
The outgoing vector at bridge r_1 is d_1=t-r_1 w_1.
It equals alpha T(chi p-r_1 w_atom) and is nonzero: if chi>0
its U component before T is nonzero, and if chi=0 its V component
is nonzero. The same argument, followed by the positive similarity
B_1, shows t_2-r_2 w_2 below is nonzero.

For any child initial vector P_c of norm R and nonzero outgoing vector
d_c, define a source-matching pair of maps into a fresh direct sum by

    E_c u=(<u,d_c/||d_c||>,u-<u,d_c/||d_c||>d_c/||d_c||,0),
    B_c v=(||d_c||/R)(<v,P_c/R>,0,v-<v,P_c/R>P_c/R).

They satisfy B_c P_c=E_c d_c and

    <E_c u,B_c v>=<u,d_c><P_c,v>/R^2.                 (3)

Use (3) first with P_c=P_1,d_c=d_1, and denote its maps E_1,B_1
and squared right scale n=||d_1||^2/R^2. Set c_2=d_atom,
c_6=k d_atom, and c_i=0 otherwise. The fifteen-step data are

    Z15_i=E_1 X_i, J15_i=E_1 G_i, f15_i=F_i-c_i,       i=0..7,
    Z15_(8+i)=B_1 X_i, J15_(8+i)=B_1 G_i,
    f15_(8+i)=n F_i,                                  i=0..7.

Their word is h_15=(h_7,1+r_1,h_7), initial P_2=E_1 P_1,
terminal gradient w_2=B_1 w_1, and terminal residual t_2=B_1 t.
Use (3) again with P_c=P_2,d_c=t_2-r_2 w_2. Denote its maps E_2,B_2
and squared right scale q_scale=||t_2-r_2w_2||^2/R^2. Put

    eps_lift=chi k D/100,
    L_i=eps_lift for i=4..7,
    L_i=n eps_lift for i=12..15,
    L_i=0 for i=0..3,8..11.

The full CURRENT thirty-one-step child, denoted (Z_i,G_i,f_i), is

    Z_i=E_2 Z15_i, G_i=E_2 J15_i, f_i=f15_i,           i=0..15,
    Z_(16+i)=B_2 Z15_i, G_(16+i)=B_2 J15_i,
    f_(16+i)=q_scale(f15_i+L_i),                       i=0..15.
                                                                  (4)

These are the nine-dimensional vectors and the exact values in the
complete input, on h_31=(h_15,1+r_2,h_15). The source used the name
t_scale for q_scale; it is
renamed here to distinguish it from a vector translation. Define

    P=Z_0, w=G_31, Y_i=Z_i-G_i,
    gamma^2=q_scale n k>0, ||w||^2=gamma^2 N,
    H_i=f_i-<G_i,Z_i>+||G_i||^2/2,
    Delta_i=f_i-||G_i||^2/2.

In particular the COMPLETE value/residual transport is

    Y_i=E_2(Z15_i-J15_i),
    Delta_i=f15_i-||J15_i||^2/2,
    H_i=f15_i-<J15_i,Z15_i>+||J15_i||^2/2,            i=0..15;
    Y_(16+i)=B_2(Z15_i-J15_i),
    Delta_(16+i)=q_scale(f15_i-||J15_i||^2/2+L_i),
    H_(16+i)=q_scale(f15_i-<J15_i,Z15_i>+||J15_i||^2/2+L_i).
                                                                  (5)

Neither c_2,c_6 nor either new atomic offset has been deleted. The input
joint derivation in Appendix A proves all 1056 original rows for (4),
constructs the single static conjugate interpolant, and identifies

    argmin F_31=conv{0,E_2 E_1 t,E_2 B_1 t},
    dist(P,argmin F_31)=R.                              (6)

For sigma=1 the hull is {0}. For sigma<1 the endpoint has

    tau_res=chi D/N, nu_res=chi^2 R^2/N,
    Delta_31=tau_res gamma^2 N/100>0,
    H_31=-(99/100)tau_res gamma^2 N,
    <w,Y_31>/||w||^2=tau_res,
    ||Y_31||^2/||w||^2=nu_res.

The actual doubled-gap payoff is

    V31=gamma^2 N(1+tau_res/50)/R^2.                    (7)

Equations (5)-(7) are carried as part of the input, with the full joint
proof appended. The NEW exclusion below does not require input
interpolation feasibility, these input values, or the particular
minimizer in (6). It depends only on the explicit vectors (4), and
allows all values and the common minimizer to be reselected.

## 3. The local motifs retained by the entire new child

Put v=G_4 and set

    u_left=Y_31, u_right=Y_7, c_dep=sigma r_0,
    c_parallel=(K-1)/rho.

Every assertion in the following list follows from (2)-(4), since the
later containing maps are similarities and have zero relative translation:

    ||P||=R, ||v||^2=k,
    Y_3=Y_27=0,
    u_right=-delta r_0 G_3,
    u_left=-delta r_0 G_27,
    Y_30-u_left=c_parallel w,
    P=mG_0+aG_2+G_3,
    c_dep G_3+mG_4+aG_6+G_7=0,
    c_dep G_27+mG_28+aG_30+w=0.                    (8)

For the dependency, the first internal bridge is
t+alpha Tp=-r_0 Lw_atom, while alpha Tp=mG_4+aG_6+G_7
and t=-delta r_0 G_3. Moving terms gives its coefficient
c_dep=(1-delta)r_0. The last seven-step piece is an exact scaled
copy, so the identical calculation gives the last dependency.

There are isometric atom maps J_first,J_last with

    (G_4,G_6,G_7,u_right)
       =alpha J_first(U,V,w_atom,chi p),
    (G_28,G_30,w,u_left)
       =gamma J_last(U,V,w_atom,chi p).              (9)

The signs and scales in (9) follow from t=chi alpha Tp. In particular
G_27=-gamma J_last p/c_dep. Define the nonnegative anchor price

    Danchor=chi(D+R^2/c_dep).                        (10)

Then

    <w-G_27,u_left>=gamma^2 Danchor,
    <G_7-G_3,u_right>=k Danchor.                     (11)

Both quantities vanish at sigma=1 and must be retained otherwise.
They replace the false equality of old zero-residual anchor heights.

For the residual-shifted right initial vector define

    Pshift=P-u_right=P+delta r_0 G_3,
    Rshift^2=||Pshift||^2
            =R^2+2delta r_0 D+delta^2 r_0^2 N,
    Dshift=-<Pshift,v>
          =m(sigma r_0 D/R^2+chi k)>0.              (12)

Indeed <P,v>=-sigma r_0 Dm/R^2 by the first cross identity, whereas
<u_right,v>=chi k m. Thus the adverse signed correlation is changed
and explicitly paid; it is not copied from a zero-residual child.

## 4. The full released affine model and its normalization

Take arbitrary similarities E,F from R^9 into any common Euclidean
space, with E^T E=ell I_9,F^T F=nu I_9 and ell,nu>=0. Define

    t_join=E(u_left-rw)-FP.

The actual parent samples have

    X_i=EZ_i, g_i=EG_i,                                  i=0..31,
    X_(32+j)=t_join+FZ_j, g_(32+j)=FG_j,                  j=0..30,
    X_63=t_join+FZ_31, g_63=g_free.

The bridge X_32=E(u_left-rw) is exactly the step 1+r from X_31.
All other queried updates follow from the actual child word. All
parent values f_parent,i are free. Add one free common optimum
(z_opt,0,0). Impose every distinct original ordered row

    Q_ij=f_parent,i-f_parent,j+<g_i,X_j-X_i>
                                      +||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0.      (13)

There are 4160 such rows on {star,0,...,63}. A Gram description uses
the 9 columns of E, 9 columns of F, z_opt and g_free. Its diagonal
frame blocks are ell I_9 and nu I_9; ALL other blocks are free and the
ENTIRE twenty-by-twenty Gram is PSD. In particular every singular
block range condition is retained. This is equivalent to the
Euclidean formulation above by Gram factorization.

Normalize by an actual common-minimizer distance ||EP-z_opt||, or by
the nearest-minimizer distance of the resulting static interpolant
when constructing a witness. The family's free scale is closed under
rescaling to distance one. No inherited surrogate radius replaces
that choice. Necessity of (13) follows from accepted
`1252b8823bb5a290`; sufficiency would give ONE static convex smooth
function for the entire run. Our contradiction arises entirely in
the queried rows, before either normalization or the optimum is used.

## 5. Residual shifting is a change of coordinates, with its costs kept

Subtract the common vector E u_left from ALL parent positions. Write
the left intrinsic child coordinates as Z_i-u_left, and the right
ones as Z_j-u_right. The new relative translation is the exact vector

    tbar=t_join+F u_right-E u_left
        =-r Ew-F Pshift.                              (14)

The actual problem has not been restricted. This affine rewrite is
invertible, preserves each original row, and shifts the common
optimum too. In these coordinates left sample 31 and right local
sample 7 have residual zero. Earlier anchors 27 and local 3 generally
DO NOT have zero residual.

Define intrinsic parent heights, using the free parent values, by

    hL_i=f_parent,i-<EG_i,E(Z_i-u_left)>+ell||G_i||^2/2,
    hR_j=f_parent,32+j-<FG_j,F(Z_j-u_right)>+nu||G_j||^2/2.

The right definition deliberately excludes tbar. Direct expansion of
(13) gives all within-copy rows and both cross orientations:

    Q_(i,k)=hL_i-hL_k+ell<G_i-G_k,Y_k-u_left>,
    Q_(32+j,32+l)=hR_j-hR_l+nu<G_j-G_l,Y_l-u_right>,
    Q_(i,32+j)=hL_i-hR_j+<EG_i,tbar>
                         +<EG_i-FG_j,F(Y_j-u_right)>,
    Q_(32+j,i)=hR_j-hL_i-<FG_j,tbar>
                         +<FG_j-EG_i,E(Y_i-u_left)>.
                                                                  (15)

Assume all original queried rows are nonpositive and put

    H_L=hL_31, H_R=hR_7, DeltaH=H_R-H_L.

No relation between H_L and H_R is imposed. In particular their
equality is NOT inherited from either input endpoint value.

## 6. Exact return paths and the new anchor-height interval

Apply the two opposite original rows between left 27 and 31. Since
Y_31-u_left=0 and Y_27-u_left=-u_left, (11) and (15) give

    H_L-ell gamma^2 Danchor <= hL_27 <= H_L.          (16)

The right pair at global 35 and 39 gives

    H_R-nu k Danchor <= hR_3 <= H_R.                 (17)

The left reverse atomic path consists of the actual rows
Q_(31,30),Q_(30,29),Q_(29,28). In shifted coordinates its receiving
residuals are gamma J_last y_2,gamma J_last y_1,gamma J_last y_0.
The first normal is -ell gamma^2 d_atom; the next two are zero by
(2) and the duplicate U gradients. Thus

    hL_28,hL_30 >= H_L-ell gamma^2 d_atom.            (18)

The right reverse path Q_(39,38),Q_(38,37),Q_(37,36) likewise gives

    hR_4,hR_6 >= H_R-nu k d_atom.                    (19)

For the FIRST right atom, use Q_(35,34),Q_(34,33),Q_(33,32).
Its residuals have been shifted by -u_right. Telescoping those shifts
without dropping them gives

    hR_j >= hR_3-nu d_atom+nu<G_j-G_3,u_right>, j=0,2.     (20)

For example Q_(35,34) has normal
-nu d_atom-nu<G_3-G_2,u_right>; its height inequality is exactly
the j=2 case of (20). The next two steps have unshifted normals zero
and telescope the gradient-difference terms to give the j=0 case.

Define

    Cdep=A+c_dep, Kdep=Cdep-1,
    Jprice=(A-1)d_atom+c_dep Danchor,
    Vprice=(a d_atom+c_dep Danchor)/m,
    Aplus=A+delta r_0,
    Bprice=(A-1)d_atom+Aplus k Danchor.                (21)

All are nonnegative; Cdep,Kdep are positive. The additional c_dep
Danchor term is the precise new price required by the changed child.

## 7. What the original rows force on translation and scales

The cross rows into global 39 and into left 31 have zero receiving
residuals in (15). They therefore imply, at every queried source used
below,

    <EG_i,tbar> <= H_R-hL_i,
    <FG_j,tbar> >= hR_j-H_L.                         (22)

Multiply the first inequalities by the positive coefficients of the
LAST dependency in (8), and use (16),(18). Multiply the second by
the coefficients of the FIRST dependency in (8), and use (17),(19).
Both vector sums are zero. The resulting bounds are

    -ell gamma^2 Jprice/Cdep <= DeltaH
                             <= nu k Jprice/Cdep.    (23)

Use -w=c_dep G_27+mG_28+aG_30 in the first part of (22):

    <Ew,tbar> >= -Kdep DeltaH-ell gamma^2 Jprice
              >= -Kdep nu k Jprice/Cdep-ell gamma^2 Jprice.
                                                                  (24)

Use -m v=aG_6+G_7+c_dep G_3 in the second part:

    <Fv,tbar> <= -(a+1+c_dep)DeltaH/m+nu k Vprice
              <= [(a+1+c_dep)/m]ell gamma^2 Jprice/Cdep
                                                        +nu k Vprice.
                                                                  (25)

Finally Pshift=mG_0+aG_2+(1+delta r_0)G_3. Multiply (20) and
the height for source 3 by these coefficients, using (17). The
shift correction in the weighted sum is

    <P-A G_3,u_right>=delta r_0(A N-D)>=0,

because A N>D from the bounds proved in section 8. Dropping only
this NONNEGATIVE term yields

    <F Pshift,tbar> >= Aplus DeltaH-nu Bprice.        (26)

All these bounds hold for arbitrary free values, correlations, and
scales. The zero-optimum rows were not used. The changed endpoint
energy has not been presumed zero: all free values remain in the
heights until (23)-(26) are deduced from original rows.

## 8. Rational estimates, proved from the current data

Squaring positive rationals proves 14142/10000<s<14143/10000.
Substitution into section 2 gives

    7/5<a<3/2, 341/100<m<171/50,
    241/100<K<121/50, 1<z<201/200,
    29/5<A<6, 12/5<r_0<49/20<5/2,
    14<R^2<71/5, 2/5<N<21/50,
    99/100<D<11/10, 1/2<e<3/5,
    rho>99/100, 0<=chi<=1/9999, delta<=1/10000.      (27)

For the less direct bounds: R^2=m^2+Kz is between
(341/100)^2+241/100 and (171/50)^2+(121/50)(201/200).
The latter is 14.1285<71/5. Also N=rho^2 z/K is greater than
(99/100)/(121/50)>2/5 and smaller than (201/200)/(241/100)<21/50.
Finally e=z(1-1/K) lies between 1-100/241>1/2 and
(201/200)(71/121)<3/5. All decimal expressions here are exact
terminating rationals, not numerical evaluations.

It follows that

    c_dep>239/100, c_dep<5/2, Cdep>8, Kdep<8,
    0<k<1/5, 841/25<r<36,
    0<c_parallel<3/2, 1+c_parallel/r<21/20,
    (a+1+c_dep)/m<3/2,
    0<=Danchor<1/1400,
    0<Jprice/e<1/84, 0<Jprice<1/140,
    0<=Vprice/e<1/560,
    Aplus<61/10, 0<Bprice<3/500,
    14<Rshift^2<71/5,
    Dshift/Rshift^2>1/25.                            (28)

Here are explicit checks of every price estimate. The bound on k
uses (5/2)^2(21/50)/14<1/5. Since lambda^2>29/5 and
lambda<5/2, the bounds on r follow from A>29/5 and r=lambda^2 A;
the upper r<36 follows more tightly from lambda^2<6 and A<6.
The bounds on c_parallel and the ratio 1+c_parallel/r follow from
K<121/50,rho>99/100,r>841/25. The displayed ratio involving m is
less than 5/(17/5)<3/2.

Since R^2/c_dep<(71/5)/(239/100)<6, (10) gives
Danchor<(71/10)/9999<1/1400. With e>1/2 this gives
Danchor/e<1/700. Hence

    Jprice/e < 5/600+(5/2)/700=1/84,
    Vprice/e < [(3/2)/600+(5/2)/700]/(17/5)=1/560.

Thus Jprice<(3/5)/84=1/140. Also

    Bprice<5/1000+(61/10)(1/5)(1/1400)<3/500.

The extra terms in Rshift^2 beyond R^2 are less than
2(1/10000)(5/2)(11/10)+(1/10000)^2(5/2)^2(21/50).
Adding this bound to 14.1285 gives less than 71/5, and
Rshift^2>=R^2>14. Finally (12) and (27) give

    Dshift/Rshift^2
       >= sigma r_0 Dm/(R^2 Rshift^2)
       > [(999/1000)(12/5)(99/100)(341/100)]/(71/5)^2
       > 1/25.                                      (29)

For the final rational comparison its numerator is
8.09405784>8.0656=(71/5)^2/25. The discarded chi k term in
Dshift is nonnegative. The inequality A N>D used in section 7
follows from A N>(29/5)(2/5)>11/10>D.

Equations (24)-(25) now give two convenient coarse estimates:

    <Ew,tbar> >= -nu e/420-ell gamma^2 Jprice,
    <Fv,tbar> <= (3/16)ell gamma^2 Jprice+nu e/2800,

where the upper bound remains valid if the actual left-hand side
is negative. Multiplying the latter by the positive number
1+c_parallel/r<21/20 and using the nonnegative displayed upper bound,

    <Ew,tbar>-(1+c_parallel/r)<Fv,tbar>
                  >= -nu e/360-ell gamma^2 e/70.      (30)

The rational checks are

    1/420+(21/20)/2800=463/168000<1/360,
    [1+(21/20)(3/16)]/84=383/26880<1/70.

These are charges for the NONZERO new residuals. No old equality of
anchor heights is used in (30).

## 9. Both independent scales are forced into a bounded ratio

Assume ell>0 and put T_aff=nu/(ell gamma^2). The exact bridge (14)
gives

    nu Rshift^2=r^2 ell gamma^2 N+2r<Ew,tbar>+||tbar||^2.

Use (24), before its coarse version, and the upper bound in (23).
Nonnegativity of the last norm gives

    T_aff >= [r^2 N-2r Jprice]
               /[Rshift^2+2r Kdep k Jprice/Cdep].     (31)

The numerator is positive. In fact

    2Jprice/(rN)<1/800,
    2r Kdep k Jprice/(Cdep Rshift^2)<3/400.

For the first, use Jprice<1/140, r>841/25, N>2/5.
For the second, use Kdep/Cdep<1,r<36,k<1/5,
Jprice<1/140,Rshift^2>14, giving 9/1225<3/400.
Consequently

    T_aff > (799/806)r^2 N/Rshift^2
          > (99/100)r^2 N/Rshift^2>0.                (32)

In particular a nonzero left copy cannot have nu=0. Conversely
||F Pshift+tbar||^2=r^2 ell gamma^2 N, combined with (26), gives

    nu Rshift^2 <= r^2 ell gamma^2 N-2Aplus DeltaH+2nu Bprice.

Use the lower bound in (23) to obtain

    T_aff <= [r^2N+2Aplus Jprice/Cdep]
                    /[Rshift^2-2Bprice] < 40.         (33)

Indeed its numerator is less than
36^2(21/50)+2(61/10)(1/140)/8<545; its denominator is
greater than 14-6/500>139/10. Their ratio is less than
5450/139<40. Every step used a full Euclidean norm identity;
there is no relaxation of PSD or an imposed scale matching rule.

## 10. The exact original six-cycle and its strict sign

Sum the six original rows in (1), using (15). Every free height
cancels. The receiving residuals at 31 and right local 7 are zero
in the SHIFTED coordinates; the right return path costs exactly
-nu k d_atom. Using Y_30-u_left=c_parallel w gives the exact identity

    S_cycle=<Fv,E(gamma J_last y_2)>-ell gamma^2 e
                                  +<Ew-Fv,tbar>-nu k d_atom
           =(c_parallel Dshift/r)nu-ell gamma^2 e
                +<Ew,tbar>-(1+c_parallel/r)<Fv,tbar>
                                                   -nu k d_atom.   (34)

In the second equality use gamma J_last y_2=c_parallel w and
r Ew=-tbar-F Pshift. This equality retains every translation term
from the actual new child. There is no perpendicular error term,
since the current atom has w_atom=rho V exactly.

Normalize by E_rec=ell gamma^2 e>0. Since c_parallel N/e=rho,
(29),(32) imply

    [(c_parallel Dshift/r)nu]/E_rec
       > (99/100)r rho Dshift/Rshift^2
       > (99/100)^2 (841/25)(1/25)
        =8242641/6250000>131/100.                     (35)

Using (30), (33), and k d_atom/e<1/3000, the combined translation
and return-path costs divided by E_rec are strictly less than

    T_aff(1/360+1/3000)+1/70
       <40(7/2250)+1/70=28/225+1/70.                 (36)

Substituting into (34) proves

    S_cycle/E_rec > 131/100-1-28/225-1/70 >1/6.       (37)

For the last comparison, subtracting 1/6 gives
31/100-28/225-1/70-1/6=29/6300>0. This proves (1).
All six Q entries would have to be nonpositive, so ell>0 is impossible.

If ell=0, E=0 and the physical bridge has X_31=X_32=0,
g_31=0, g_32=FG_0. The sum of the two opposite original rows is
||FG_0||^2=nu, because ||G_0||=1. Hence nu=0. Every queried
position and gradient then vanishes, as does X_63. Two opposite
rows between 63 and a queried point force g_free=0. Equal-position
rows force equal sampled values; the star rows force that common
value to equal the optimum value zero. A constant function realizes
this zero-gap face. Its nearest-minimizer distance is zero, so it is
not a positive-distance adversary. No point with positive normalized
gap remains in the full released fixed-child model.

## 11. The failed state law and a portable exact interface

The proof uses the current actual31 vectors only through (8)-(12),
the original return paths, and the positive scale gamma. Thus any
intervening geometry retaining these first/last seven-step motifs
has the same obstruction at r=lambda^2 A. Changing its intermediate
maps, all its values, the optimum, or the final unqueried gradient
cannot fix the contradiction. These are structural conditions to
escape; this statement is not a claim about all conceivable children.

For a portable exact necessary condition, set

    hcoef=(a+1+c_dep)/m,
    Acycle(r)=c_parallel Dshift/r
       -k[d_atom+(1+c_parallel/r)Vprice
                     +(Kdep-(1+c_parallel/r)hcoef)Jprice/Cdep].

When Kdep-(1+c_parallel/r)hcoef>=0, combining the FIRST lines of
(24),(25) and the upper DeltaH bound in (23) directly in (34) gives

    S_cycle >= nu Acycle(r)-ell gamma^2(e+Jprice).

Therefore every legal full affine join with ell>0 must satisfy

    T_aff Acycle(r)<=e+Jprice,                        (38)

together with (31),(33). If Acycle(r)>0, the lower bound (31)
can be substituted into (38), giving a condition solely in the
displayed motif parameters and bridge. The coefficient hypothesis
holds at the present bridge since Kdep>7 while
(1+c_parallel/r)hcoef<(21/20)(3/2)<2.
Equation (37) resolves the present parameters; (38) is not offered
as an untested replacement for that resolved region.

The new component missing from a scalar kappa law is explicit:
Danchor, the signed correlation Dshift, and the original short return
cost d_atom jointly constrain BOTH scale and translation through
Jprice,Vprice,Bprice. The terminal energy alone records none of these.
The coordinate shift makes two residuals zero but introduces exactly
these positive prices and changes the initial norm to Rshift. Treating
that shift as a free zero-endpoint reduction would be incorrect.

For completeness, the input's true-distance law (7) is

    kappa31=(lambda^3 A)^2 V31,
    formal_kappa63=(1-2tau_res/r+nu_res/r^2)
                                     kappa31^2/(1+tau_res/50).

The denominator is the actual positive endpoint-energy cost. Even
this correctly priced expression is not a realizable next state:
(37) forbids its proposed full affine construction. Consequently
there is no all-depth preserved family consisting of these actual
states and this fixed-child join, including horizon-dependent choices
of sigma within [9999/10000,1] when identical copies of that selected
child are joined. Larger contractions, distinct child families, or different
first/last motifs are outside this conclusion and require new analysis.

The earlier finite internal surplus is also NOT a new objective lower
bound beyond the accepted scalar witness on this same word. From the
complete input's rational estimates n<1/5, q_scale<21/100,
k<1/5, N<21/50, tau_res<1/3000, R^2>14, one gets

    V31 < [(21/100)(1/5)(1/5)(21/50)(101/100)]/14
         <1/3000.

On exactly h_31, its physical capacity is C31=lambda^3 A<375/4.
Accepted fact `b98bb807d514e80d` gives the scalar Huber lower bound

    W_31(h_31)>=1/(2C31-1)>1/200.

Thus V31 is less than one fifteenth of this already accepted lower
bound. The exact original profile comparison remains B_31 V31,
not kappa31. No claim about W_31 being small follows from this
comparison of witnesses. No improvement over the scalar bound is
claimed for the finite construction.

The original arbitrary-h/non-dyadic interface remains entirely open:
an adversary must be selected for EACH prescribed nonnegative physical
schedule, use one static convex smooth function per run, and meet the
original all-split denominator B_n with its actual initial-distance
normalization. This single recursively specified diagnostic word and
its excluded affine family supply none of that uniform implication.

## 12. Audit support, retrieval, and bounded handoff

Status: **COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED PROOF CANDIDATE**, worker max,

## 1. Input fifteen-step construction and its full affine model

For every sigma in [9999/10000,1], the changed seven-step geometry
defined below has a legal fifteen-step parent on its specified next
word. The parent uses an explicit rank-one cross coupling and changes
only two values in its left copy:

    f_2 -> f_2-d_atom,
    f_6 -> f_6-alpha^2 d_atom,
    d_atom=(1-rho)(K-1)eta>0.                         (1)

Every position and gradient of both children is retained. All other
values are retained with their appropriate spatial-square scaling.
One explicit static, globally defined, convex 1-smooth function realizes
all sixteen samples. All 272 distinct original ordered interpolation
pairs, including the optimum, hold. The exact minimizer set is a convex
hull of three specified residuals; the true initial distance is R.

The physical word is

    h_3=(a,b,a),
    h_7=(h_3,1+r_0,h_3),
    h_15=(h_7,1+r_1,h_7).                            (2)

Its true-distance normalized payoff V_15 and capacity state satisfy

    W_15(h_15) >= V_15 = beta^2 alpha^2 N/R^2,
    kappa_15=(lambda^2 A)^2 V_15 > 1+1/1000.         (3)

This is a finite capacity surplus, not a comparison replacing the
original denominator B_15 by capacity squared. The exact original
benchmark ratio of this witness is B_15 V_15. It supplies neither an
arbitrary-h result nor an all-depth construction.

For clarity, the FULL released affine parent model has arbitrary
similarities E,F of the fixed child space with E^T E=ell I and
F^T F=nu I, ell,nu>=0, arbitrary cross Gram, and physical relative
translation t_join=E d-F P. Its left samples are (E X_i,E G_i), its
right queried samples are (t_join+F X_j,F G_j), and its final point is
t_join+F X_7 with a free terminal gradient. Every value and one common
minimizer are free; the full Gram, including optimum and terminal
vectors, is PSD. All original rows are imposed. The normalization uses
distance to an actual common minimizer, and a claimed witness must price
its nearest-minimizer distance.

We construct a point of this full model by choosing ell=1, nu=beta^2,
t_join=0 and an explicit F. This is a SUFFICIENT CONSTRUCTION, not a
restriction asserted lossless for the full model. The model's free
terminal gradient is legally selected as F G_7. Its incoming rows are
checked too. Independent scales, translations, values, and PSD range
conditions are not discarded in an impossibility claim.

## 2. Exact atom and its original rows

Use Euclidean inner products throughout. Define

    s=sqrt(2), lambda=1+s, theta=s-1,
    a=(311s-16)/300, b=(590+8s)/300, rho=599/600,
    z=b-1, K=a+rho, m=a+b, eta=z/K,
    A=1+2a+b=(143+105s)/50,
    r_0=theta A=(67+38s)/50, r_1=A,
    Dlen=m+K=A-1/600.

Take orthogonal U,V in R^2, with ||U||=1 and ||V||^2=eta.
Put w=rho V and p=m U+K V. The atom is

    g=(U,U,V,w),
    x=(p,bU+KV,KV,w),
    f_i=<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2, 0<=i<=3.

It obeys the three updates (a,b,a). Its residuals y_i=x_i-g_i
and energies E_i=<g_i,y_i> are

    y_0=(m-1)U+KV, E_0=m-1,
    y_1=zU+KV,     E_1=z,
    y_2=(K-1)V,    E_2=e=(K-1)eta,
    y_3=0,         E_3=0.

For any jet define aheight_i=f_i-<g_i,x_i>+||g_i||^2/2 and
Delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2. The original convention is

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =aheight_i-Delta_j+<g_i,y_j>.                (4)

For the atom aheight_i=0 and Delta_i=E_i. Its complete table of
<v-g_j,y_j>, for receiver j and possible source gradient v, is

| j | v=0 | v=U | v=V | v=w |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 | -(m-1) | 0 | -a | -a-(1-rho)z |
| 1 | -z | 0 | 0 | -(1-rho)z |
| 2 | -e | -e | 0 | -(1-rho)e |
| 3 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |

All entries are nonpositive. The duplicate U occurs at both actual
indices 0 and 1. This checks every atom pair and every optimum pair.
Write

    R^2=||p||^2=m^2+Kz,
    N=||w||^2=rho^2 eta,
    D=<p,w>=rho z,
    d_atom=(1-rho)e=e/600.                          (5)

The symbol R here is a coordinate radius, distinct from the project's
objective convention W=2R.

## 3. The internally translated seven-step geometry

Fix sigma in [9999/10000,1]. Put

    delta=1-sigma, chi=delta/sigma,
    alpha=sigma r_0 sqrt(N)/R, k=alpha^2.

Let e_axis=w/sqrt(N), p_axis=p/R. In the Euclidean direct sum
the real line direct-sum e_axis^perp direct-sum p_axis^perp define isometries

    L u=(<u,e_axis>,u-<u,e_axis>e_axis,0),
    T v=(-<v,p_axis>,0,v-<v,p_axis>p_axis).

Thus

    <L u,alpha T v>
        =-sigma r_0 <u,w><p,v>/R^2.                 (6)

Define the INTERNAL translation t=-delta r_0 Lw. In particular
t=chi alpha T p. The seven-step jets are

    X_i=Lx_i, G_i=Lg_i, F_i=f_i,                  0<=i<=3,
    X_(4+i)=t+alpha T x_i,
    G_(4+i)=alpha T g_i, F_(4+i)=k f_i,           0<=i<=3.

The bridge lands at -r_0 Lw=t+alpha T p. Hence this is an actual
trajectory on h_7. Write P=X_0=Lp and w_1=G_7=alpha T w.
Its endpoint is X_7=w_1+t, not w_1.

Let Y_i=X_i-G_i and use aheight_i,Delta_i for these seven-step jets.
For 0<=j<=3 one has

    Y_j=Ly_j, aheight_j=0, Delta_j=E_j,
    Y_(4+j)=alpha T(chi p+y_j),
    aheight_(4+j)=-chi k <p,g_j>,
    Delta_(4+j)=k E_j.                             (7)

All original child pairs hold. Within each atom this follows from the
table and spatial scaling; translation cancels within the right atom.
For a left source i and right receiver 4+j, (4) gives

    Q_(i,4+j)=-kE_j+<Lg_i,t+alpha T y_j> <=0.

For a right source 4+i and left receiver j it gives

    Q_(4+i,j)=-<alpha T g_i,t>-E_j
                                  +<alpha T g_i,Ly_j> <=0.

Every atom gradient and residual has nonnegative U,V coordinates.
Thus (6) makes the mixed terms nonpositive, while
<Lg_i,t><=0 and <alpha T g_i,t>=chi k<p,g_i>>=0.
Finally aheight<=0 and Delta>=0 check all optimum rows.

## 4. The two allowed value changes inside the left child

Set c_2=d_atom, c_6=k d_atom, and c_i=0 at every other child index.
The modified left child has values F_i-c_i, unchanged X_i,G_i,

    aheight_i^L=aheight_i-c_i,
    Delta_i^L=Delta_i-c_i.                          (8)

Every child interpolation row remains nonpositive. The only rows that
can increase are those into receiver 2 or 6. For receiver 2, its own
atom's other sources have slacks e,e,d_atom. Every source from the
other atom has slack at least e, by the second displayed cross formula
in section 3. Thus adding c_2=d_atom is legal; decreasing the source's
value can only help. For receiver 6, its own atom's other sources have
slacks k e,k e,k d_atom, and every source from the other atom has slack
at least k e, by the first displayed cross formula. Adding c_6 is legal.
These statements also cover the row between the two changed samples.

The optimum rows remain legal: aheight_i^L<=0, and the changed positive
energies are Delta_2^L=rho e and Delta_6^L=k rho e. No endpoint value
changes. In particular both endpoint energies Delta_3,Delta_7 stay zero.

These are changes to one final list of jets. We do not change a function
online while an algorithm runs; section 9 builds a single function from
the entire list before the actual run.

## 5. Top bridge and explicit full Gram realization

The actual outgoing vector at the next bridge is

    d=t-r_1 w_1=alpha T(chi p-r_1 w).

Use the auxiliary scalars

    omega=r_1 rho-chi K,
    Mout=r_1 D-chi R^2,
    Hout=||chi p-r_1 w||^2
        =r_1^2 N-2chi r_1 D+chi^2 R^2,
    beta=||d||/R, nscale=beta^2=k Hout/R^2.         (9)

All are positive except chi, which may be zero. In particular d!=0.
In the real line direct-sum d^perp direct-sum P^perp define

    E_top u=(<u,d/||d||>,u-<u,d/||d||>d/||d||,0),
    T_top v=(<v,P/R>,0,v-<v,P/R>P/R),
    B_top=beta T_top.

These maps give a realization in dimension five and satisfy

    B_top P=E_top d,
    <E_top u,B_top v>=<u,d><P,v>/R^2.               (10)

The actual parent samples are

    Z_i=E_top X_i, J_i=E_top G_i, fhat_i=F_i-c_i,    0<=i<=7,
    Z_(8+j)=B_top X_j, J_(8+j)=B_top G_j,
    fhat_(8+j)=nscale F_j,                           0<=j<=7.

The bridge equality in (10) verifies the step 1+r_1. All other steps
follow from the child recursions. The complete Gram of these vectors
and optimum zero is PSD by this explicit Euclidean factorization; all
range conditions are present. The final gradient J_15 is an allowed
choice for the full model's free unqueried gradient.

## 6. Rational scalar estimates used in the full pair check

The following deliberately loose rational bounds follow from
14142/10000<sqrt(2)<14143/10000, proved by squaring, and the defining
formulas:

    341/100<m<171/50, 241/100<K<121/50,
    1<z<201/200, 29/5<Dlen<59/10,
    29/5<r_1<6, 12/5<r_0<49/20,
    14<R^2<71/5, 2/5<N<21/50, 0<D<11/10,
    e>1/2, rho>99/100, 0<=chi<=1/9999.             (11)

For example R^2> (341/100)^2+241/100>14 and
R^2<(171/50)^2+(121/50)(201/200)<71/5.
Since rho^2>99/100, N>99/242>2/5; also
N<eta<(201/200)/(241/100)<21/50.
The bound e>1/2 follows from (K-1)eta>(141/100)(50/121)>1/2.

Consequently omega>0, Mout>0, and

    Hout>67/5, 1/8<nscale<1/5.                     (12)

Indeed Hout>(29/5)^2(2/5)-2(1/9999)6(11/10)>67/5.
Using sigma^2>999/1000, the lower estimate for nscale is greater than

    (999/1000)(12/5)^2(2/5)(67/5)/(71/5)^2 >1/8.

For the upper estimate discard the negative term in Hout and use
chi^2 R^2<1/1000000. The upper bound becomes

    (49/20)^2(21/50)[36(21/50)+1/1000000]/14^2 <1/5.

Define the auxiliary quantities

    Caff=K Dlen/(r_0 r_1 rho^2),
    Tfirst=Mout Dlen/(sigma r_0 rho Hout),
    Lcoef=omega eta sigma r_0 D/R^2,
    Tsecond=Lcoef Dlen/(R^2 nscale).

The crucial strict endpoint bound is

    rho Caff<1.                                    (13)

It has the exact algebraic certificate

    r_0 r_1 rho-K Dlen
       =(-381891+284836 sqrt(2))/9000000>0,

where already sqrt(2)>7/5 proves positivity. In particular
Caff<600/599 and Caff-1<1/599.

We also have

    Tfirst<=Caff,
    Tsecond-Tfirst < (11/5)chi   if chi>0,
    Lcoef/z<1/2,
    Tsecond+chi(Lcoef-z)/(nscale z)<=Caff.           (14)

At chi=0 the second difference is zero and the other non-strict
relations continue to hold. Here are derivations. The inequality
Tfirst<=Caff is equivalent to

    (1+chi)(r_1 D-chi R^2)r_1 N <= D Hout.

After cancelling the constant terms, the difference of the left side
and right side is

    chi r_1 [r_1 DN-R^2 N+2D^2]
                 -chi^2 R^2(r_1 N+D) <=0.

The bracket is negative because R^2 N>28/5, whereas
r_1 DN+2D^2<6(11/10)(21/50)+2(11/10)^2<28/5.
Next z omega=Mout+chi m^2, so

    Tsecond-Tfirst=chi m^2 Dlen/(sigma r_0 rho Hout).

The coefficient is less than 11/5 by (11)-(12): its numerator is
less than (171/50)^2(59/10), and its denominator exceeds
(999/1000)(12/5)(99/100)(67/5); comparison of these two rational
products gives the asserted bound. Also

    Lcoef/z <= r_0 r_1 N/R^2
             < (49/20)6(21/50)/14 <1/2.

Thus chi(1-Lcoef/z)/nscale >=(5/2)chi, with strict inequality when
chi>0. Subtracting this from the second bound gives the last part of
(14). These estimates keep the internal residual cost; it is precisely
the term chi(Lcoef-z) that must not be dropped at the critical row.

For later use, Tfirst,Tsecond<6/5. This follows from Caff<600/599,
the second difference bound, and chi<=1/9999. Moreover

    nscale z(Caff-1)<d_atom/2.                      (15)

If Caff<=1 it is immediate. Otherwise the left side is less than
(1/5)(201/200)/599=201/599000, while d_atom/2>1/2400;
the comparison is 482400<599000.

Put b_j=<p,y_j> and l_j=<w,y_j> for the atom. Their table is

| j | E_j | b_j | l_j |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 | m-1 | R^2-m | D |
| 1 | z | z Dlen | D |
| 2 | e | (K-1)z | rho e |
| 3 | 0 | 0 | 0 |

For j=0,1,2 one has b_j<R^2 and l_j/E_j<=rho. Also
b_1/E_1=Dlen, while b_j/E_j<(4/5)Dlen for j=0,2.
For j=0, the numerator is less than 54/5 and
(4/5)Dlen(m-1)>(4/5)(29/5)(12/5)>54/5.
For j=2, b_2/E_2=K<(4/5)Dlen follows from (11).

Finally the two bounds needed in the reverse orientation are

    sigma r_0 r_1 D m rho/R^4 <1/3,
    (sigma r_0 D/R^4) Mout <1/10<nscale.             (16)

For the first, bound its numerator by (49/20)6(11/10)(171/50)
and its denominator below by 196. For the second, use
Mout<=r_1D and bound the numerator by (49/20)6(11/10)^2.
Both resulting rational inequalities are strict.

## 7. Every forward cross row

Abbreviate the following four exact child correlations:

    u_i=<G_i,d>, v_i=<P,G_i>,
    p_i=<P,Y_i>, q_i=<d,Y_i>.

From (6)-(9) their entries are

    u_0=u_1=0,
    u_2=sigma r_0 rho eta Mout/R^2, u_3=rho u_2,
    u_4=u_5=k chi m, u_6=-k omega eta, u_7=rho u_6;

    v_0=v_1=m, v_2=z, v_3=D,
    v_(4+j)=-(sigma r_0 D/R^2)<p,g_j>;

    p_j=b_j,
    p_(4+j)=-sigma r_0 D(chi+b_j/R^2);

    q_j=sigma r_0 Mout l_j/R^2,
    q_(4+j)=k[-chi Mout+chi b_j-r_1 l_j],           0<=j<=3.
                                                               (17)

In particular p_0,p_1,p_2>0, p_3=0, p_4,p_5,p_6<0,
and p_7<=0. All q_0,q_1,q_2>0, q_3=0, q_4,q_5,q_6<0,
and q_7=-k chi Mout<=0. To see the strict late signs, use
chi b_j<=chi R^2<1/600, while r_1 l_j>1 for j=0,1,2.

Equation (4) and the cross identity (10) give every forward cross row:

    Q_(i,8+j)=aheight_i-c_i-nscale Delta_j+u_i p_j/R^2.
                                                               (18)

All aheight_i-c_i are nonpositive. Therefore only positive products
u_i p_j need further consideration; zero products cause no problem.

First let the receiver be j=0,1,2. Positive products can have source
i=2,3,4,5. For i=4,5, aheight_i=-chi k m and u_i=chi k m,
so their sum with the mixed term is chi k m(b_j/R^2-1)<=0.
For i=2 the ratio of u_2 b_j/R^2 to nscale E_j is

    Tfirst * b_j/(Dlen E_j).

At j=0,2 it is less than (6/5)(4/5)<1. At j=1, (14)-(15)
give Q_(2,9)<=nscale z(Caff-1)-d_atom<-d_atom/2.
For i=3 the same ratio is multiplied by rho, and (13)-(14)
make it strictly smaller than one, including at j=1.

Now let the receiver be 4+j, for atomic j=0,1,2,3. A positive product
can only have source i=6 or 7. Formula (18) becomes exactly

    Q_(6,12+j)/k
       =chi(Lcoef-z)+Lcoef b_j/R^2-nscale E_j-d_atom,
    Q_(7,12+j)/k
       =rho[chi(Lcoef-z)+Lcoef b_j/R^2]-nscale E_j.
                                                               (19)

For j=3, E_j=b_j=0 and Lcoef<z, so both are nonpositive.
For j=0,2 drop the nonpositive chi term and use
Tsecond b_j/(Dlen E_j)<(6/5)(4/5)<1.
For j=1 use the last inequality of (14). It gives

    Q_(6,13)/k <=nscale z(Caff-1)-d_atom<-d_atom/2,
    Q_(7,13)/k <=nscale z(rho Caff-1)<0.

Receivers with p_j=0 and products of opposite signs were already
covered by (18). This exhausts all 64 forward cross rows, including
every row into the actual final sample 15.

## 8. Every reverse cross row and the total original system

The other orientation is

    Q_(8+i,j)=nscale aheight_i-Delta_j+c_j+v_i q_j/R^2.
                                                               (20)

For early receivers j=0,1,2, a positive product requires i=0,1,2,3.
Their v_i are at most m. The positive term divided by E_j is at most

    m sigma r_0 Mout rho/R^4
       <=m sigma r_0 r_1 D rho/R^4<1/3.

But Delta_j-c_j>=rho E_j, so (20) is nonpositive. Other source signs
only help. Receiver j=3 has q_j=Delta_j=c_j=0, leaving
nscale aheight_i<=0.

For a late receiver 4+j, a positive product requires a late source
4+i. Set Patom_i=<p,g_i>, which is one of m,m,z,D and is positive.
Using (7) and (17), combine the source height and mixed term exactly:

    nscale aheight_(4+i)+v_(4+i)q_(4+j)/R^2
      = k Patom_i {chi[-nscale+(sigma r_0 D/R^4)Mout]
               +(sigma r_0 D/R^4)[-chi b_j+r_1 l_j]}.
                                                               (21)

For j=3 the last bracket is zero; (16) makes (21) nonpositive.
For j=0,1,2 discard its nonpositive chi terms. The remaining term,
divided by k E_j, is at most

    m sigma r_0 r_1 D rho/R^4<1/3.

The receiver has Delta_(4+j)-c_(4+j)>=k rho E_j. Thus (20) is
nonpositive. Early sources have v_i>0 and q_(4+j)<=0, so they are
already safe. This checks all 64 reverse cross rows, including rows
whose source is the final gradient.

Sections 3-4 check all 56 ordered within-left rows; section 3 and
scaling check all 56 within-right rows. Sections 7-8 check all 128
cross rows. The 32 optimum incidences hold because every aheight is
nonpositive and every Delta is nonnegative, including after (1).
Thus all 272 distinct ordered rows on {star,0,...,15} are proved.
No sampled gradient, remote pair, or original position has been removed.

## 9. One global function and the TRUE radius

Let yhat_i=Z_i-J_i and Deltahat_i=fhat_i-||J_i||^2/2. On the same R^5
define

    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max(0,max_(0<=i<=15)(<yhat_i,v>-Deltahat_i)),
    F_parent(Z)=sup_v(<Z,v>-H(v)).                  (22)

All rows just proved, together with accepted fact `1252b8823bb5a290`,
show that this is one finite, everywhere differentiable, convex,
1-smooth function satisfying F_parent(Z_i)=fhat_i, grad F_parent(Z_i)=J_i,
and F_parent(0)=0=min F_parent. Its trajectory from Z_0 is exactly (2).

Only samples 3,7,11,15 have Deltahat_i=0; their residuals are
0,E_top t,0,B_top t. All other Deltahat_i are strictly positive.
It follows directly that

    argmin F_parent=conv{0,E_top t,B_top t}.         (23)

For completeness, at sufficiently small epsilon>0 in any fixed
direction v,

    H(epsilon v)=epsilon^2||v||^2/2
       +epsilon max(0,<E_top t,v>,<B_top t,v>).

Every point of the displayed convex hull has conjugate value zero.
If Z is outside it, let z be its closest point and v=Z-z. The elementary
projection inequality gives <Z,v> greater than the maximum of the
three support values. The displayed local expression then gives
F_parent(Z)>0. This proves both inclusions in (23).

The exact nearest-minimizer distance is R. Indeed

    <P,t>=-delta r_0 D<=0,
    <P,d>=sigma r_0 D Mout/R^2>0,
    <E_top P,B_top t>=<P,d><P,t>/R^2<=0.

Hence <Z_0,z><=0 for every minimizer z in (23), and
||Z_0-z||^2=R^2+||z||^2-2<Z_0,z>>=R^2.
Zero attains equality. At sigma<1 the internal residuals are nonzero;
they have not been identified with zero or hidden in a surrogate radius.

The endpoint value is fhat_15=nscale k N/2. Thus (3)'s exact payoff is
2F_parent(Z_15)/dist(Z_0,argmin F_parent)^2=nscale k N/R^2.
Rescaling F_parent(RZ)/R^2 makes the true initial distance one without
changing this normalized payoff or any step. Multiplying the function
by a desired L_smooth>0 gives the same normalized payoff with steps
h_i/L_smooth. This is the original doubled-gap W=2R convention.

Set

    r_2=lambda A=(353+248s)/50, c_lift=1/100,

Thus Mout_1=Mout and Hout_1=Hout from section 5. The symbol t_scale is a

    L_i=0,                 i=0,1,2,3,8,9,10,11,

Section 14 proves that the modified values fhat_i+L_i remain legal
on the identical fifteen-step vectors. No within-atom interpolation

For the new top join define actual maps on the five-dimensional child

    T_2 v=(<v,P_2/R>,0,v-<v,P_2/R>P_2/R),

Both E_2 and T_2 are isometries. These formulas give

The ambient space has dimension nine. The thirty-one-step samples are

    Xnew_i=E_2 Z_i, Gnew_i=E_2 J_i, Fnew_i=fhat_i,

The physical word is h_31=(h_15,1+r_2,h_15). Its bridge equation is

All vectors at all original indices are retained. The final gradient
is a permitted choice of the full model's free unqueried vector.
Equation (29) factors the entire Gram, including optimum zero and the
terminal vector, so PSD and every range condition hold.

## 13. Additional rational bounds

We use the bounds already derived in section 6, and prove here every

    1+1/2000 < kappa_3 < 1+1/1000,

The lower kappa_3 bound was derived in section 10. For the upper bound,

Indeed s<10/7, and

    14<r_2<15, 0<q_axis<1/5,

    99/100<v_scale<101/100,

    Cpair_2 < (49/20)15(11/10)^2/196 < 1/4.

Also Cpair_1/n<(1/10)/(1/8)=4/5. Define the residual factor

        =1-2chi D/(r_2N)+chi^2 R^2/(r_2^2 N).

The coarse bounds in (11) give

Now sigma^2>999/1000 and (31) give

    m sigma r_0 r_2 D rho/R^4
        <(171/50)(49/20)15(11/10)/196<3/4.

For the positive mixed terms introduce, for j=1,2,

At j=1 these are exactly the quantities in section 6. Hence

At j=2 we have

using Caff<600/599, r_2/r_1=lambda>12/5, and sigma>999/1000.

        =chi m^2 Dlen/(sigma r_0 rho Hout_2)<=chi.

which is positive and smaller than 1001/1000. These scalar estimates

## 14. The lifted fifteen-step values satisfy every internal row

We must check the RIGHT child in (30), whose values are fhat_i+L_i.
Within an atomic block the increments are constant, so every original
row there is unchanged. In a seven-step constituent, the late atom
gains eps_lift while the early atom gains zero, with the corresponding
scale in the second constituent. The late intrinsic heights are

    -chi k Patom_i+eps_lift <= -(1-c_lift)chi k Patom_i <= 0,
    Patom_i=<p,g_i> in {m,m,z,D},                       (36)

before the already paid nonnegative deficits are subtracted. We used
D<=Patom_i. The mixed terms at this first internal join are nonpositive,
as derived in section 3. Thus the early-to-late rows improve; the
late-to-early rows stay nonpositive by (36). The deficits at indices
2 and 6 remain legal: their only adverse receiver changes have the
atomic slacks checked in section 4, and the new constants cancel
within those atoms. Optimum rows also hold: heights are nonpositive
and energies only increased.

It remains to check the top cross rows of the fifteen-step child.
Let lift7_i=eps_lift for i=4,5,6,7 and lift7_i=0 otherwise. Relative to (18)
and (20), the forward row changes by lift7_i-n lift7_j and the reverse row
changes by n lift7_i-lift7_j.

For a forward row with early source i<4 the change is nonpositive.
For late sources i=4,5 and early receivers j=0,1,2, the sum of the
source height and positive mixed term is bounded above by

    chi k m(b_j/R^2-1)+eps_lift
       <=chi k[-m^2/R^2+c_lift D] <= 0.             (37)

Here b_j<=b_0=R^2-m, since b_0-b_1=am>0 and
b_0-b_2=m(m-1)+z>0. The last bracket is negative by (11):
m^2/R^2>(341/100)^2/(71/5)>4/5, while c_lift D<11/1000.
For j=3 the mixed term is zero and (36) applies. Late sources 6,7
and early receivers have a nonpositive product. Sources 4,5 and late
receivers also have a nonpositive product. Their heights remain
nonpositive by (36).

The only other forward cases are sources 6,7 and late receivers 4+j.
Equation (19) gets the extra term (1-n)eps_lift. After division by k
this is at most c_lift chi D<1/800000. At atomic j=0,2, the original
proof has a negative margin at least nE_j/25>1/400, since its product
ratio is less than (6/5)(4/5)=24/25. At j=1 the source-6 margin is
d_atom/2>1/2400. The source-7 margin is, by (31),
nz(1-rho Caff)>1/80000. These are all larger than the possible
increment. At j=3 the source-6 expression is at most
-chi z/2-d_atom and the source-7 expression is at most -chi D/2.
Adding c_lift chi D leaves both nonpositive, also at chi=0.
This covers all forward rows of the lifted child.

For reverse rows with early receivers, a late source has nonpositive
mixed term and height bounded in (36); early sources are unchanged.
The zero-residual receiver 3 leaves just this nonpositive height.
For late receivers, early sources have nonpositive mixed term, and
the receiver's increased value helps. If both source and receiver
are late, the original expression (21) changes by
(n-1)eps_lift<=0. This covers every reverse row, including all
endpoint directions. Consequently all 272 original ordered rows of
the lifted fifteen-step child, with its optimum zero, are legal.

## 15. Complete boundary table for the actual next join

Index the four atoms of the fifteen-step child by b=0,1,2,3 and write
its original index as 4b+j, 0<=j<=3. Put

    s_b=(1,k,n,nk),
    d_b=(d_atom,k d_atom,0,0).

Here d_b is a value-deficit coefficient, not an outgoing vector.
For the atom let Patom_j=<p,g_j>, b_j=<p,y_j>, l_j=<w,y_j> as before,
and define Zeta_j=<w,g_j>. Their entries include

    Patom_j=(m,m,z,D),
    Zeta_j=(0,0,rho eta,N),
    l_j=(D,D,rho e,0).

Let barY_i=Z_i-J_i, barH_i=fhat_i-<J_i,Z_i>+||J_i||^2/2,
and barDelta_i=fhat_i-||J_i||^2/2. The exact intrinsic data are

    barH_(4b+j)=-d_b 1_(j=2),                b=0,2,
    barH_(4b+j)=-chi s_b Patom_j-d_b 1_(j=2),    b=1,3,
    barDelta_(4b+j)=s_b E_j-d_b 1_(j=2).            (38)

In particular barDelta_(4b+j)>=rho s_b E_j for every b,j.
The increments (28) are c_lift chi s_b D for b=1,3 and zero
otherwise. Thus every barH_i+L_i is nonpositive, and every
barDelta_i+L_i is nonnegative.

Write

    V_i=<P_2,J_i>, Pval_i=<P_2,barY_i>,
    U_i=<J_i,d_2>, Qval_i=<d_2,barY_i>.

The following is a complete four-block table, derived from (6), (10),
and d_2=B_top alpha T(chi p-r_2 w):

| b | V_(4b+j) | Pval_(4b+j) |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | Patom_j | b_j |
| 1 | -q_axis Patom_j | -q_axis(chi R^2+b_j) |
| 2 | Cpair_1 Patom_j | Cpair_1 b_j |
| 3 | -Cpair_1 q_axis Patom_j | -Cpair_1 q_axis(chi R^2+b_j) |

| b | U_(4b+j) | Qval_(4b+j) |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | Cpair_2 sigma r_0 Mout_1 Zeta_j/R^2 | Cpair_2 sigma r_0 Mout_1 l_j/R^2 |
| 1 | Cpair_2 k(chi Patom_j-r_1 Zeta_j) | Cpair_2 k(-chi Mout_1+chi b_j-r_1 l_j) |
| 2 | n sigma r_0 Mout_2 Zeta_j/R^2 | n sigma r_0 Mout_2 l_j/R^2 |
| 3 | nk(chi Patom_j-r_2 Zeta_j) | nk(-chi Mout_2+chi b_j-r_2 l_j) |
                                                               (39)

For example a vector in the left seven-step part pairs with
B_top alpha T(chi p-r_2w) by multiplying its pairing with the old
outgoing vector d by <P,alpha T(chi p-r_2w)>/R^2=Cpair_2.
A vector in the right seven-step part instead gets the factor n.
The first table follows by taking the same old cross identity with
E_top P. These arguments derive every table entry, without a new
PSD relaxation or a sign assumption on an unlisted coordinate.

The signs are as follows. In blocks 0,2, Pval is positive for j<3
and zero for j=3; U is positive only for j=2,3. In blocks 1,3,
Pval is nonpositive; U is nonnegative for j=0,1 and strictly negative
for j=2,3. Qval is positive in blocks 0,2 for j<3 and zero for j=3;
it is negative in blocks 1,3 for j<3, and nonpositive for j=3.
For the latter sign, chi b_j<=chi R^2<1/600 and r_1 l_j>1 for j<3;
Mout_j>0. At chi=0 the endpoint entries vanish, consistently with
the same inequalities.

## 16. Every forward cross row of the thirty-one-step parent

For any 0<=i,j<=15, (29) and the universal original-row identity (4)
give exactly

    Q_(i,16+j)=barH_i-t_scale(barDelta_j+L_j)
                                      +U_i Pval_j/R^2.          (40)

If the last product is nonpositive there is nothing to cancel:
both preceding terms are nonpositive. We check every possible
positive product, retaining the zero-energy receivers separately.

First suppose the receiver lies in block 0 or 2 and its atomic
index is j<3. Its energy is at least rho s_b E_j. The ratios of its
positive Pval to its block scale are b_j and (Cpair_1/n)b_j, respectively.
Both are at most b_j. For sources in blocks 0,2 at atomic indices
2,3, the mixed term divided by t_scale s_b E_j is at most,
respectively,

    (Cpair_2/v_scale)Tfirst_1 < (1/3)(101/100)<1/2,
    Tfirst_2 < 1/2.                                 (41)

We used b_j/E_j<=Dlen, and the endpoint source j=3 multiplies
the corresponding atomic-index-2 expression by rho. Since rho>99/100,
these products fit within the receiver energy. The other positive
sources can only be atomic U samples in blocks 1,3. For block 1,
the sum of its height and the product is at most
chi k m[-1+Cpair_2 Pval_j/R^2]<=0. For block 3 it is at most
chi nk m[-1+Pval_j/R^2]<=0. Here Pval_j<=R^2. Receivers at atomic
index 3 in blocks 0,2 have Pval=0 and are already covered.

Now the receiver is in block 1 or 3. A positive product requires
a source at atomic index 2 or 3 in block 1 or 3. For an atomic-index-2
source, put s_source=k and theta_source=Cpair_2 if its block is 1, and put
s_source=nk and theta_source=1 if its block is 3. Let r be r_1 or r_2
according to that source block. Let theta_receiver=1 in receiver
block 1 and Cpair_1 in receiver block 3. The source height plus product,
before its nonpositive paid deficit is subtracted, is exactly

    s_source { chi(theta_source theta_receiver Lcoef_r-z)
             +theta_source theta_receiver Lcoef_r b_j/R^2 }.   (42)

Here Lcoef_r means Lcoef_1 or Lcoef_2. For an endpoint source this
whole expression is multiplied by rho. For atomic receiver j<3,
the non-chi part of (42), divided by t_scale s_b E_j, is at most

    (Cpair_2/v_scale)Tsecond_1 < 2/5       (source block 1),
    Tsecond_2 < 1/2                   (source block 3).          (43)

For receiver block 3 these ratios are further multiplied by
Cpair_1/n<4/5. Thus the energy rho s_b E_j pays for the non-chi part.

The chi part is nonpositive except possibly for source block 3
and receiver block 1. In that remaining case it is at most

    nk chi z/1000,

by (34). The added receiver energy in (40) is

    t_scale eps_lift = nk chi z (v_scale c_lift rho).

It is at least the required amount since
v_scale c_lift rho>(99/100)(1/100)(99/100)>1/1000.
The same bound covers the endpoint source, whose coefficient is smaller.
At atomic receiver j=3, b_j=E_j=0, so this chi calculation alone
proves the row. If chi=0 both expressions vanish. This exhausts
all 256 forward cross rows, including every incoming terminal row.

## 17. Every reverse cross row and the full original system

The other orientation is exactly

    Q_(16+i,j)=t_scale(barH_i+L_i)-barDelta_j
                                      +V_i Qval_j/R^2.         (44)

The height term is nonpositive. Products of opposite signs are
therefore already covered. Consider first a receiver in block 0 or 2
with atomic index j<3. A positive product requires a source in block
0 or 2, whose V_i is at most m. Dividing the mixed term by the
receiver's s_b E_j, and using l_j/E_j<=rho, gives at most

    Cpair_2 m sigma r_0 Mout_1 rho/R^4
        < Cpair_2/3 < 3/4,                    receiver block 0,
    m sigma r_0 Mout_2 rho/R^4
        <= m sigma r_0 r_2 D rho/R^4<3/4,   receiver block 2.

These are smaller than rho, so barDelta_j>=rho s_b E_j suffices.
Their atomic endpoint receivers have Qval=barDelta=0 and leave
only the nonpositive height term.

For receivers in blocks 1,3, a positive product requires a source
in blocks 1,3. Let s_source=k,nk and a_source=1,Cpair_1 in these two
source blocks. Its height satisfies

    barH_i+L_i<=-(1-c_lift)chi s_source Patom_i.

Let s_receiver=k,nk and theta_receiver=Cpair_2,1 in receiver blocks
1,3, with r=r_1,r_2 and M_r=Mout_1,Mout_2 respectively. The height term
plus mixed term in (44) is at most

    chi Patom_i[-t_scale(1-c_lift)s_source
           +a_source q_axis s_receiver theta_receiver M_r/R^2]
      +a_source q_axis Patom_i s_receiver theta_receiver
                                      (-chi b_j+r l_j)/R^2.    (45)

The coefficient in the first line is nonpositive. Indeed
a_source/s_source<=1/k, since Cpair_1/n<1. After division by s_source,
the positive term is at most Cpair_1 Cpair_2 for receiver block 1, and
n Cpair_2 for receiver block 3. Both are at most n Cpair_2 because Cpair_1<n.
On the other hand,

    t_scale(1-c_lift)=n v_scale(1-c_lift)>n Cpair_2

by (32). The -chi b_j term in the second line is also nonpositive.
For atomic receiver j<3, its remaining term divided by s_receiver E_j
is at most

    q_axis m r_2 rho/R^2
       =m sigma r_0 r_2 D rho/R^4<3/4<rho.

The receiving energy pays for it. At atomic index j=3 both b_j and
l_j vanish, so the first-line estimate proves the row directly.
This exhausts all 256 reverse cross rows, including every outgoing
terminal row.

The left child contributes 240 ordered within-child rows by sections
2-8. The lifted right child contributes 240 by section 14 and scaling.
The two cross orientations contribute 512 by sections 16-17. The 64
optimum incidences hold by (38) and (36): all physical heights are
nonpositive and all energies are nonnegative. These are precisely
1056 distinct ordered rows on {star,0,...,31}. No original gradient,
queried pair, final sample, or range condition has been removed.

## 18. One global function, its minimizers, and exact normalization

Define ynew_i=Xnew_i-Gnew_i and
Deltanew_i=Fnew_i-||Gnew_i||^2/2. On the same R^9 put

    Hnew(v)=||v||^2/2
               +max(0,max_(0<=i<=31)(<ynew_i,v>-Deltanew_i)),
    F_31(x)=sup_v(<x,v>-Hnew(v)).                    (46)

All original rows have just been proved. Applying the constructive
interpolation proof of accepted fact `1252b8823bb5a290` proves that
F_31 is finite, convex, everywhere differentiable, and 1-smooth,
with F_31(Xnew_i)=Fnew_i, gradient Gnew_i, and minimum value zero.
This is one static function chosen before the run, not a sequence
of changed functions.

If chi>0, the zero energies occur at left indices 3,7,11,15 and
right indices 19,27. Their residuals are, respectively,

    0, E_2 E_top t, 0, E_2 B_top t, 0, 0.

Every other energy is positive: in particular the newly lifted right
endpoint energies at 23 and 31 are t_scale eps_lift and
t_scale n eps_lift. Thus

    argmin F_31=conv{0,E_2 E_top t,E_2 B_top t}.      (47)

To check this directly, for any fixed v and sufficiently small
epsilon>0, the maximum of affine terms in Hnew(epsilon v) retains
only the zero-energy slopes. Its linear term is the support function
of the hull in (47). Points in that hull have conjugate value zero.
For a point outside, its difference from the closest point of the
hull is a strict separating direction; sufficiently small epsilon
in that direction gives a positive conjugate value. This proves
both inclusions. At chi=0 all translations and increments vanish,
and the same formula gives the singleton {0}.

Section 9 proved that <Z_0,z><=0 for every
z in conv{0,E_top t,B_top t}. The isometry E_2 preserves this relation,
and ||Xnew_0||=R. Therefore for every minimizer z in (47),

    ||Xnew_0-z||^2=R^2+||z||^2-2<Xnew_0,z>>=R^2.

Zero attains equality. The TRUE initial distance is exactly R. Neither
internal residual nor the new endpoint energy has been hidden in a
surrogate distance to a more remote minimizer.

Let N_3=||Gnew_31||^2=t_scale N_2. With
tau=chi D/N and nu_res=chi^2 R^2/N from section 10, the exact terminal
quantities are

    Deltanew_31=c_lift tau N_3,
    2F_31(Xnew_31)=N_3(1+2c_lift tau),
    <Gnew_31,ynew_31>/N_3=tau,
    ||ynew_31||^2/N_3=nu_res.                       (48)

The actual normalized payoff and capacity state are consequently

    V_31=t_scale n k N(1+tau/50)/R^2,
    kappa_31=(lambda^3 A)^2 V_31
        =Fshape_2 kappa_15^2(1+tau/50)>1+1/600.     (49)

Indeed tau<1/3000, r_2>14, and nu_res>=0 give
Fshape_2>1-1/21000. Section 10 gives kappa_15>1+1/1000.
It therefore suffices that
1/500-1/21000-1/10500000>1/600; subtracting 1/600 leaves
1/3500-1/10500000>0.

The exact comparison to the ORIGINAL benchmark is

    W_31(h_31)>=V_31,
    V_31/U_31^ZJ = B_31 kappa_31/(lambda^6 A^2),     (50)

where B_31 is defined in accepted `b98bb807d514e80d`. Capacity squared
has not replaced B_31. Rescaling F_31(Rx)/R^2 gives true distance one
and the same normalized payoff; multiplication by any smoothness
constant L_smooth>0 gives the corresponding steps h_i/L_smooth.
This is the original doubled-gap W=2R convention.

## 19. Why copied values fail and what the new boundary actually costs

The extra values are essential for this concrete join when chi>0.
If L_i in (28) were zero, the actual queried row with source 15 and
receiver 23 would be

    Q_(15,23)=chi n k rho(Lcoef_2-z)>0.              (51)

It follows from (39): source 15 is block 3's endpoint, and right
local 7 is block 1's endpoint. To see the strict sign uniformly,
sigma kappa_3>1+1/3000 by section 10, while
delta r_0D/R^2<1/50000 by (11). Equation (35) implies Lcoef_2/z>1.
The new receiver increment subtracts t_scale eps_lift from this
row. Inequalities (34) and (32) show that this more than pays its
positive part. The reverse row is separately checked in section 17;
there is no invalid two-row or three-row sign cancellation.

Thus the fixed-value continuation fails, while this value-reselected
point of the full affine model succeeds at the level of the joint
draft. This is not a separator of the released affine family and
not a claim that it was necessary to change the queried geometry.

The full new boundary, not just its scalar payoff, is

    ynew_i=E_2 barY_i, Deltanew_i=barDelta_i,
    Hnewheight_i=barH_i,                             0<=i<=15,
    ynew_(16+j)=B_2 barY_j,
    Deltanew_(16+j)=t_scale(barDelta_j+L_j),
    Hnewheight_(16+j)=t_scale(barH_j+L_j),            0<=j<=15.
                                                               (52)

Here Hnewheight denotes Fnew-<Gnew,Xnew>+||Gnew||^2/2 and must not
be confused with the conjugate function Hnew in (46). In particular
the endpoint height is -(1-c_lift)tau N_3, its energy is
c_lift tau N_3, and the earlier two paid deficits remain in the left
child and in the appropriately scaled right copy. All these data
must be retained or re-priced by any further parent.

For orientation only, at the next physical bridge
r_3=lambda^2 A, the outgoing norm would be

    ||ynew_31-r_3 Gnew_31||^2
        =N_3(r_3^2-2r_3 tau+nu_res).

If another origin-aligned join with copied endpoint value were legal,
its FORMAL objective-state law would be

    kappa_63,formal
       =(1-2tau/r_3+nu_res/r_3^2)
                           kappa_31^2/(1+tau/50).   (53)

The denominator is required: the actual31 gap is larger than its
squared terminal-gradient norm. Dropping that factor would confuse
the new positive energy with a free norm gain. Formula (53) is not
a feasibility assertion, and no actual63 construction or separator
is claimed here. This round has tested the assigned NEXT nonempty
extension, namely actual15 to actual31.

## 20. Scope, audit, and bounded handoff

This manuscript includes every new assertion it uses: the atom, both
internal joins, the actual15 value deficits, the new atom-wise lifts,
both orientations of all31 cross rows, one explicit static function,
its full minimizer set, true radius, and the resulting boundary state.
The two accepted predecessors used are `1252b8823bb5a290` for
interpolation/realization and `b98bb807d514e80d` for benchmark notation.
No unverified shared finding is a mathematical predecessor.

The counterexample screen explicitly rejects the copied-value
shortcut through (51). It also keeps sigma=1: then chi=eps_lift=0,

fully released parent model were used; no impossibility statement

or a fixed final gradient.

Retrieval was conditioned on the mature affine-interpolation program.
Matlas returned the smooth interpolation statement in arXiv:1910.01845,
Theorem 5, plus two unrelated results. No new external theorem was
imported. The exact local Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur paper,

Reference: https://arxiv.org/abs/1502.05666.

All new mathematics remains UNVERIFIED. This is a complete finite
