# Independent parameters at every stage of the two actual spiral families

Status: COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical candidate, worker max2,
one allocated round56. Steering: TASK.md and master_guidance
7b252271b54d4ab9; plan875f1db305544d71. No formal gate is authorized.
Every new assertion below belongs to this single joint proof attempt.
The preceding common-pair draft bfff4aa387a9d818 is provenance, not a
verified premise. All geometry, gluing, radius and comparison arguments
needed for this extension are included here. The only accepted input is
fact_id04219554e41d9750, used solely for the original benchmark ray and
its saved enclosure. No restriction of the interpolation scope of
1252b8823bb5a290 is asserted or used.

The parameters below are chosen independently for every selected stage,
with knowledge of the entire physical word, BEFORE defining one static
function. The objective never changes during gradient descent. The two
families retain their different full-step and half-step definitions;
their factors are never spliced into a third family.

## 1. Physical words and local constants

All spaces, norms and inner products are finite-dimensional Euclidean.
The vector e_i is the standard coordinate vector indexed from zero;
the planar e(theta) defined below is a separate symbol.
For a finite nonnegative word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), gradient descent at
advertised smoothness one is x_(t+1)=x_t-h_t grad f(x_t). Write

    E(f,h)=2(f(x_n)-min f)/dist(x_0,argmin f)^2.

The initial distance in each instance below is positive. For advertised
smoothness L>0, define the normalized payoff and physical update by

    E_L(f,h)=2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L*dist(x_0,argmin f)^2),
    x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)*grad f(x_t).

Thus E_1 is the displayed E. Multiplying the constructed objective f
by L>0 and using the physical steps h_t/L preserves every iterate and gives

    E_L(L*f,h)=E_1(f,h).
The unrestricted worst-case value W_n(h) takes the supremum over the
full original smooth-convex function class; a bound on either family
below is not an upper bound on W_n(h).

Choose any ordered checkpoint subset of size k and decompose the
ORIGINAL word, without deleting or rearranging a physical entry, as

    h=(block_0,b_0,block_1,...,b_(k-1),block_k),
    s_i=sum block_i.

Empty and all-zero blocks are allowed, and ordinary steps have no upper
bound. For EACH i<k choose independently

    kappa_i>0, 0<epsilon_i<=1/2,
    theta_sp,i=arctan(1/epsilon_i),
    C_i=sqrt(1+epsilon_i^2) exp(kappa_i theta_sp,i),
    T_i=(exp((1-kappa_i^2)theta_sp,i/kappa_i)-1)/(1-kappa_i^2)
                                                  if kappa_i<1,
    T_i=theta_sp,i/kappa_i                         if kappa_i>=1,
    H_sp,i=C_i[1+(1+kappa_i)T_i]-1,
    A_i=epsilon_i+(1+H_sp,i)/epsilon_i.                  (1)

The branches defining T_i meet continuously at kappa_i=1. In particular
C_i>1 and A_i>=2C_i. These C_i are local arc constants, distinct from
the original scalar benchmark sequence C_n defined in Section 10.

The source family requires the local eligibility condition

    b_i>=2(1+epsilon_i^(-2)).                            (2S)

The main family uses the following actual normal correction:

    qmax_i=1                                      if kappa_i<=epsilon_i,
    qmax_i=sqrt((1+epsilon_i^2)/(1+kappa_i^2))
             exp(kappa_i[theta_sp,i-arctan(1/kappa_i)]) otherwise,
    N_i=max(0,kappa_i qmax_i-epsilon_i),
    b_i>=B_i:=1+max(epsilon_i^(-2),N_i/epsilon_i).       (2M)

All eligibility endpoints are included. There is no constraint linking
parameters in neighboring stages, and no ordering between kappa_i and
epsilon_i is assumed. In particular, the kappa_i>=1 branch is retained.

The hierarchy for the comparison is fixed by ONE enclosing pair
integer m>=1536 and real 0<tau<=1/4:

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-tau/m,
    c=lambda(1+tau/m), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    H_(m,tau)=H_m, S_j=sum H_j, n_m=2^m-1,
    H_base(h)=1/(1+2 sum h).                            (3)

Every child uses exactly this enclosing (m,tau). Parameters are allowed
to depend arbitrarily on m, tau, all selected positions, and the complete
word, subject only to (1) and the appropriate eligibility conditions.

## 2. One local curve: exact ordered backward states

This section is a local lemma with kappa,epsilon denoting ONE stage's
parameters. Its proof is repeated for every i with its own pair; no
cross-stage identification is made. Suppress the subscript i in (1).
For 0<=theta<=theta_sp set

    R(theta)=C exp(-kappa theta),
    e(theta)=(cos theta,-sin theta),
    t(theta)=(-sin theta,-cos theta),
    v(theta)=R(theta)e(theta)=(p(theta),-q(theta)),
    n(theta)=e(theta)+kappa t(theta),
    K=conv({0} union {v(theta):0<=theta<=theta_sp}).      (4)

The endpoint slopes are (C,0) and (epsilon,-1). With Delta=phi-theta,

    <n(theta),v(phi)>/R(theta)
        =exp(-kappa Delta)(cos Delta+kappa sin Delta).

The derivative in Delta is
-(1+kappa^2)exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta. Since |Delta|<pi/2, the maximum
is one at Delta=0. The origin has smaller support value. Thus n(theta)
supports the full K at v(theta), and (0,1) also supports K at (C,0).
Differentiating the coordinates gives

    p'=-R(kappa cos theta+sin theta)<0,
    q'=R(cos theta-kappa sin theta),
    p>=epsilon, q>=0, p>=epsilon q.                    (5)

The sign of q' gives exactly qmax in (2M), including both branches.

For a chronological local list alpha_1,...,alpha_r>=0, initialize
r_r=v_r=(epsilon,-1). Suppose r_j=(u_j,-y_j), with current projected
arc point v_j=v(theta_j). For alpha=alpha_j, use the following backward
definition. If

    y_j<=kappa[u_j+(alpha-1)C],                          (6)

set v_(j-1)=(C,0), r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha(C,0). Otherwise let theta be the
unique root in (0,theta_j] of

    <r_j,e(theta)-kappa^(-1)t(theta)>/R(theta)=1-alpha, (7)

and set v_(j-1)=v(theta), r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha v(theta).
These are exact definitions; no root finder or recurrence is executed.

Here is a proof that the definition exists and that v_j=Proj_K(r_j).
Inductively u_j>=epsilon, y_j>=1, and y_j/u_j<=1/epsilon. Before any cap,

    r_j=R(theta_j)[(1+nu_j)e(theta_j)+kappa nu_j t(theta_j)],
    nu_j>=0.                                           (8)

It holds at j=r with nu_r=0. Under (6), let
mu=u_j+(alpha-1)C>=y_j/kappa>0. Then

    r_(j-1)-v(0)=mu n(0)+(0,kappa mu-y_j)

is a nonnegative combination of supporting normals, proving the
projection. It also gives y_(j-1)<=kappa(u_(j-1)-C). Consequently every
earlier backward step remains capped, including zero steps.

If (6) fails the current state cannot already be capped. By (8), the
left side of (7) is one at theta_j; at zero it is smaller than 1-alpha.
Its derivative is

    (kappa+kappa^(-1))(u_j cos theta+y_j sin theta)/R(theta)>0.

There is therefore exactly one root, with theta=theta_j when alpha=0.
The polar angle of r_j is at least theta_j by (8), so <r_j,t(theta)>
is nonnegative at the root. Equation (7) then states that
r_j+(alpha-1)v(theta) is a nonnegative multiple of n(theta), proving
the preceding projection and (8). Adding alpha(p,-q) and using (5)
preserves u_j>=epsilon, y_j>=1, and y_j/u_j<=1/epsilon.

Define the ACTUAL ordered outputs

    P=sum_(j=1)^r alpha_j p(theta_(j-1)),
    Q=sum_(j=1)^r alpha_j q(theta_(j-1)),
    s_loc=sum_(j=1)^r alpha_j.

Then r_0=(epsilon+P,-1-Q), P>=epsilon Q, and 0<=P<=Cs_loc.
For consecutive uncapped angles theta_old<=theta_new, put
Delta=theta_new-theta_old. Taking the tangent component of the exact
backward update gives

    nu_old=B_D+A_D nu_new,
    A_D=exp(-kappa Delta)(cos Delta+sin Delta/kappa),
    B_D=exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta/kappa.                (9)

Both coefficients are nonnegative. Write d_sp=1-kappa^2. Direct
differentiation gives
A_D'-(d_sp/kappa)A_D=-(1+kappa^(-2))exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta<=0.
If kappa<1, A_D<=exp(d_sp Delta/kappa), B_D<=Delta/kappa, and
1+d_sp B_D<=exp(d_sp Delta/kappa). Therefore

    1+d_sp nu_old<=exp(d_sp Delta/kappa)(1+d_sp nu_new).

Multiplication along the decreasing angles proves nu_j<=T. If
kappa>=1, A_D<=1 and B_D<=Delta/kappa give nu_j<=T by summing angle
differences. Hence every uncapped y_j is at most C[1+(1+kappa)T]. A cap
leaves y_j unchanged, proving

    0<=Q<=H_sp, P>=epsilon Q, P<=Cs_loc.               (10)

Every actual arc slope also satisfies

    p+q>=1,
    P+Q>=s_loc,
    (epsilon+P)^2+(1+Q)^2>=(1+epsilon+s_loc)^2/2.       (11)

Indeed R>=sqrt(1+epsilon^2)>=1 and cos theta+sin theta>=1. The last
line is (x+y)^2<=2(x^2+y^2), with x=epsilon+P and y=1+Q. These are
inequalities for the actual ordered outputs, not definitions by mass.

Two further estimates are needed for main-family gluing:

    y_j>=q(theta_j), u_j-p(theta_j)>=-N.               (12)

Before a cap, (8) gives y_j-q_j=R nu_j(sin theta_j+kappa cos theta_j)
>=0; after a cap q_j=0. A nonnegative horizontal component of the
normal in (8) gives u_j>=p_j. Otherwise theta_j>arctan(1/kappa),
so p_j<=kappa qmax, while u_j>=epsilon. At a cap u_j>=C. This proves
both claims with the exact N in (2M).

## 3. Local envelopes, exact zero wedges, and sample values

Choose a local checkpoint b_loc>=1+epsilon^(-2) and a positive sigma
such that

    sigma(P+epsilon b_loc)<=1,
    sigma[P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon]<=1.                 (13)

Set

    z_circ=(1-sigma(epsilon+P),sigma(1+Q))=(c_1,c_2),
    U=1-sigma(P+epsilon b_loc), Y=sigma Q,
    V={0,v(0),v_0,...,v_r},
    phi(z)=sigma max_(v in V)<v,z-z_circ>,
    f_loc(x)=min_z[phi(z)+||x-z||^2/2].                (14)

All actual planes and both endpoints remain present. By (5), each
(p,-q)=[(p-epsilon q)/C](C,0)+q(epsilon,-1), with nonnegative
coefficients. Thus the COMPLETE zero set of phi is

    Z_phi={z:z_1<=c_1,
             epsilon(z_1-c_1)-(z_2-c_2)<=0}.           (15)

The endpoint planes prove necessity; the nonnegative representation
proves sufficiency. Conditions (13) imply c_1>=c_2/epsilon>=0, so phi
vanishes on z_1<=0,z_2>=0. Also U>=0, and for any slope in V,

    <v,(U,Y)-z_circ>=sigma[(1-b_loc)epsilon p+q]<=0.

Consequently phi(U,y)=0 whenever y>=Y. These two zero regions use only
THIS stage's epsilon and do not require the next stage's epsilon.

For completeness, if J is a nonnegative finite maximum of affine
functions with a zero, its envelope env_1 J has a unique proximal
minimizer z at every x. This follows from coercivity and strict
convexity of J(z)+||x-z||^2/2. The vector g=x-z belongs to partial J(z).
For a second proximal pair z',g', subgradient monotonicity gives

    ||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>.

The trial point z yields
env_1 J(x+w)<=env_1 J(x)+<g,w>+||w||^2/2. The subgradient inequality
at z, applied to the proximal point z' for x+w, yields
env_1 J(x+w)>=env_1 J(x)+<g,w>+||w-(z'-z)||^2/2. These inequalities
prove differentiability with gradient g and its 1-Lipschitz property.
Convexity follows by taking convex combinations of trial pairs in the
jointly convex defining objective. Nonnegativity and attainment give
min env_1 J=0 and argmin env_1 J={J=0}.

At each x_j^loc=z_circ+sigma r_j, projection of r_j onto K shows that
z_j^loc=x_j^loc-sigma v_j is proximal: for every w in V,
<w-v_j,r_j-v_j><=0, so sigma v_j is an active subgradient of phi there.
Thus the exact local gradient and value are

    grad f_loc(x_j^loc)=sigma v_j,
    f_loc(x_j^loc)=sigma^2[<v_j,r_j>-||v_j||^2/2].      (16)

The local chronological path starts at (1,0), follows ALL alpha_j,
reaches (1-sigma P,sigma Q), and its checkpoint b_loc sends it to
(U,Y+sigma b_loc). During the ordinary block the first coordinate is
at least U and the second belongs to [0,Y]. These claims follow by
subtracting successive alpha_j sigma v_(j-1) and noting p,q>=0.

## 4. Source assembly with independent stage parameters

For each i<k apply Sections 2--3 with its own (kappa_i,epsilon_i),
the local list block_i/2, and b_loc=b_i/2. In THIS source assembly,
P'_i,Q'_i and r'_(i,j),v'_(i,j) always denote these HALF-step outputs
and states. Use exactly

    sigma'_i=1/[C_i s_i/2+epsilon_i b_i/2+A_i].          (17)

By (10) and (1), both conditions (13) hold. Define the resulting local
objects phi'_i,f'_i,c'_(i,1),c'_(i,2),U'_i,Y'_i by (14). Let

    G'_i=sigma'_i b_i/2=b_i/(C_i s_i+2A_i+epsilon_i b_i),
    D'_0=1, ell'_0=0,
    D'_(i+1)=D'_i G'_i,
    ell'_(i+1)=D'_i sigma'_i Q'_i,
    delta'=D'_k/(1+s_k).                               (18)

For d>0 define Hub_d(z)=0 for z<=0, z^2/2 for 0<=z<=d, and
dz-d^2/2 for z>=d. Its derivative is respectively 0,z,d, so it is
convex and 1-smooth. In R^(k+1), with x_0=e_0, define the SINGLE
STATIC function

    F(x)=(1/2)sum_(i<k)(D'_i)^2
          f'_i((x_i-ell'_i)/D'_i,x_(i+1)/D'_i)
                 +(1/2)Hub_(delta')(x_k-ell'_k).        (19)

All scales are positive and finite and are fixed before the run.
Every term is nonnegative, and zero is a common minimizer by (15).
Each scaled local term has its 1-smooth upper quadratic bound on the
two coordinates before the factor 1/2. Every coordinate is counted at
most twice, including the tail. Hence

    F(x+w)<=F(x)+<grad F(x),w>+||w||^2/2.

This proves the advertised global smoothness. More explicitly, for a
convex differentiable function satisfying this upper bound, apply it
to V(z)=F(z)-<grad F(y),z> at x with w=-grad V(x). Since y minimizes
V, this gives

    F(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y>
                      >=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.  (20)

Adding the exchanged inequality and applying Cauchy--Schwarz proves
that grad F is 1-Lipschitz on the full space.

At the start of stage i, coordinates j<i are frozen at
ell'_j+D'_j U'_j, the current coordinate is ell'_i+D'_i, and later
coordinates are zero. During this stage, x_i>=ell'_i and
0<=x_(i+1)<=ell'_(i+1). Every preceding component is at its OWN
post-checkpoint zero region: its first local coordinate is its U'_j,
and its second is at least Y'_j. Every future component is at its OWN
pre-activation zero region, with first local coordinate at most zero
and second zero. For j=i+1 the first assertion uses
x_(i+1)<=ell'_(i+1); for j>i+1 it uses ell'_j>=0. These separate tests
retain epsilon_j in each wedge and never transfer an epsilon value
from a neighbor. The tail is also inactive until its stage.

Thus the current summand is the only one with nonzero gradient. At
every ordinary query and at the query for the checkpoint, its exact
point is the embedded local x_j^loc, and its gradient and value are

    grad F(x)=D'_i sigma'_i v'_(i,j)/2 on coordinates (i,i+1),
    F(x)=(D'_i sigma'_i)^2
                [<v'_(i,j),r'_(i,j)>-||v'_(i,j)||^2/2]/2. (21)

All other gradient coordinates vanish. A physical step h_t therefore
implements the local step h_t/2 exactly. At checkpoint b_i the next
coordinate becomes ell'_(i+1)+D'_(i+1), and coordinate i freezes at
ell'_i+D'_i U'_i. This proves the induction through every physical
query, including zero steps and all eligibility equalities.

In the tail, if u is the already executed physical tail mass, then
x_k-ell'_k=D'_k-delta' u/2>=delta'. Therefore

    grad F(x)=delta' e_k/2,
    F(x)=[delta'(D'_k-delta' u/2)-(delta')^2/2]/2,
    2F(x_n)=(D'_k)^2/[2(1+s_k)].                      (22)

All earlier summands remain zero because the tail coordinate is at
least ell'_k. This includes the unqueried terminal sample.

The COMPLETE minimum set of (19) is the polyhedron

    C_source={x:x_i<=zeta'_i,
      epsilon_i x_i-x_(i+1)<=epsilon_i zeta'_i-D'_i c'_(i,2), i<k;
      x_k<=ell'_k},
    zeta'_i=ell'_i+D'_i c'_(i,1).                       (23)

Necessity and sufficiency follow from nonnegativity of all summands
and the exact local wedges (15). It contains zero. When k>=1, e_0
violates its first horizontal constraint, so
0<rho_S^2:=dist(e_0,C_source)^2<=1.

Here is an exact finite, non-executed formula for the TRUE distance,
retaining all later rows. Let A_rad have rows e_i^T and
(epsilon_i e_i-e_(i+1))^T for i<k, followed by e_k^T. Let t_rad be
their right sides from (23). For each linearly independent row subset I,
let A_I be the submatrix, r_I=A_I e_0-(t_rad)_I,
K_I=A_I A_I^T, and lambda_I=K_I^(-1)r_I. Include the empty subset
with value zero. Then

    rho_S^2=max_(I independent,lambda_I>=0) r_I^T K_I^(-1)r_I. (24)

For any feasible x and any nonnegative row weights lambda,
||e_0-x||^2>=2lambda^T r_I-||A_I^T lambda||^2 by completing a square.
This proves each listed value is a lower bound. At the actual
projection x_*, e_0-x_* is in the cone of active row normals. To
justify that assertion, the finitely generated cone is closed: remove
linear dependences from any nonnegative representation until an
independent subset remains, giving a finite union of closed injective
images of orthants. If the normal were outside this cone, its residual
from projection onto the cone would have nonpositive inner product
with each active row and positive inner product with e_0-x_*. A small
move in that residual direction would preserve the active inequalities
and, by their strict slacks, every inactive inequality, contradicting
projection optimality. Reduce the normal's nonnegative representation
to an independent active subset. Activity then gives r_I=K_I lambda_I
and equality in (24). This is an identity, not a projection computation.

A quantitative bound uses just the two FIRST rows, while keeping the
entire polyhedron in the feasible set. Put
z_bar=(c'_(0,1),c'_(0,2),0,...,0). Then

    e_0-z_bar=sigma'_0(P'_0-epsilon_0 Q'_0)e_0
       +sigma'_0(1+Q'_0)(epsilon_0 e_0-e_1).           (25)

Both coefficients are nonnegative by (10), and the first two rows
are equalities at z_bar. Thus <e_0-z_bar,x-z_bar><=0 for every x in
the FULL C_source, whether or not z_bar is feasible for later rows.
Expanding squared distance and then using (11) with s_loc=s_0/2 gives

    rho_S^2>=(sigma'_0)^2[(epsilon_0+P'_0)^2+(1+Q'_0)^2]
            >=(sigma'_0)^2(s_0+2)^2/8.                (26)

Combining (18), (22) and (26), with D'_1=sigma'_0 b_0/2, proves

    E(F,h)<= b_0^2/[(s_0+2)^2(1+s_k)]
            *product_(i=1)^(k-1)
               [b_i/(C_i s_i+2A_i+epsilon_i b_i)]^2.  (27)

This is an UPPER comparison of the actual payoff, with the actual
half-step outputs used to prove its radius bound. It does not identify
the true distance with a one-stage terminal projection. For k=0,
the source family is F=Hub_(1/(1+s_0))/2 from 1. Its closest radius
is exactly 1 and its payoff is 1/[2(1+s_0)].

For any two actual samples a,b, including every minimizer and the
terminal sample, (20) implies the complete directed residual
Q_ab=F(x_a)-F(x_b)+<grad F(x_a),x_b-x_a>
+||grad F(x_a)-grad F(x_b)||^2/2 is nonpositive. All physical gradients
belong to R^(k+1), so their full Gram matrix is positive semidefinite.
No interpolation row is removed.

## 5. Main assembly with independent stage parameters

For each i<k use its own parameters in Sections 2--3, now with the
FULL physical ordinary list block_i and b_loc=b_i. Write P_i,Q_i and
r_(i,j),v_(i,j) for these full-step outputs and states. Define exactly

    M_i^tr=P_i+max(epsilon_i b_i,epsilon_i+(1+Q_i)/epsilon_i),
    sigma_i=1/M_i^tr,
    D_0=1, ell_0=0,
    D_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(b_i-1),
    ell_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(1+Q_i).                       (28)

Conditions (13) hold by definition. Let phi_i,c_(i,1),c_(i,2),U_i,Y_i
be the corresponding (14). Define one STATIC finite maximum and its
single envelope in R^(k+1), starting at e_0:

    Phi_i(z)=D_i^2 phi_i((z_i-ell_i)/D_i,z_(i+1)/D_i), i<k,
    delta=D_k/(1+2s_k), Phi_k(z)=delta max(z_k-ell_k,0),
    J(z)=max_(0<=i<=k)Phi_i(z), f=env_1 J.             (29)

All coefficients and planes are fixed before the run. The origin is
a zero of J by (15), so Section 3 proves that f is everywhere finite,
convex, differentiable, 1-smooth, and has attained minimum zero.

We check every old and future plane at each proposed proximal point.
In the active local block, the second proximal coordinate is
sigma_i(1+Q_i-y_j+q_j), which belongs to [0,sigma_i(1+Q_i)]. Its
nonnegativity follows from y_j<=1+Q_i; its upper bound is (12).
Its first proximal coordinate minus U_i is

    sigma_i[epsilon_i(b_i-1)+u_j-p_j]>=0,             (30)

using the full eligibility (2M) and (12). Embed this local proximal
point, leaving the earlier frozen coordinates unchanged and all later
coordinates zero. The immediately preceding term is zero because its
first local input is its own U_(i-1), and its second input is at least
ell_i/D_(i-1)=sigma_(i-1)(1+Q_(i-1))>=Y_(i-1). Earlier terms have
the same property from the frozen-coordinate induction. The immediately
following term is zero because its first local input is at most
(D_i sigma_i(1+Q_i)-ell_(i+1))/D_(i+1)=0 and its second is zero.
Every further term has first input -ell_j/D_j<=0 and second zero.
The future tail is zero as well. Each of these tests uses that term's
own epsilon_j in (15); no equality of neighboring epsilons is used.

The current Phi_i is nonnegative at the proposed proximal point and
attains the maximum J. Its local active plane is therefore active in
J. The vector x-z=D_i sigma_i v_(i,j) is its subgradient, proving that
z is the actual global proximal point. Thus all ordinary queries and
checkpoint queries have exactly

    grad f(x)=D_i sigma_i v_(i,j) on coordinates (i,i+1),
    f(x)=(D_i sigma_i)^2
                   [<v_(i,j),r_(i,j)>-||v_(i,j)||^2/2]. (31)

All other gradient coordinates are zero. At the start of each stage,
earlier coordinates are frozen at ell_j+D_j U_j, the current coordinate
is ell_i+D_i, and later coordinates vanish. A full physical ordinary
step is exactly the corresponding full local step. At b_i, the endpoint
gradient D_i sigma_i(epsilon_i,-1) gives frozen coordinate
ell_i+D_i U_i and new coordinate

    D_i sigma_i(Q_i+b_i)=ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1).

This proves the state induction through every physical entry. Zero
steps retain the identical gradient and value by the local definition;
the proof places no upper bound on an ordinary step.

In the tail, if u<=s_k is its already executed mass, the actual last
coordinate is ell_k+D_k-delta u. Its proposed proximal coordinate
is ell_k+D_k-delta(u+1)>=ell_k because delta=D_k/(1+2s_k).
The preceding term is in its post-checkpoint zero region; every earlier
term remains zero. Thus the tail plane is active and

    grad f(x)=delta e_k,
    f(x)=delta(D_k-delta u)-delta^2/2,
    2f(x_n)=D_k^2/(1+2s_k).                            (32)

This includes s_k=0, with the endpoint proximal coordinate equal to
ell_k. It proves the advertised physical terminal value, without an
extra hidden step.

The COMPLETE minimum set is

    C_main={x:x_i<=zeta_i,
      epsilon_i x_i-x_(i+1)<=epsilon_i zeta_i-D_i c_(i,2), i<k;
      x_k<=ell_k},
    zeta_i=ell_i+D_i c_(i,1).                          (33)

The point z_bar=(c_(0,1),c_(0,2),0,...,0) is feasible for (33).
For the first stage it is its center. For the second stage its first
local input is zero, because c_(0,2)=ell_1; all later local first
inputs are nonpositive. For stages i>=1, each second local input is zero. When k=1,
the terminal coordinate is exactly ell_1; when k>1 it is zero<=ell_k.
The first-stage normal identity (25), now unprimed and using full-step
outputs, proves <e_0-z_bar,x-z_bar><=0 for every feasible x. Therefore
z_bar is the TRUE closest minimizer and

    rho_M^2=dist(e_0,C_main)^2
           =sigma_0^2[(epsilon_0+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2]>0.  (34)

There is no epsilon_1 in this normal calculation or feasibility test.
Combining (28), (32), and (34) yields the EXACT actual payoff

    E(f,h)=(b_0-1)^2/
       {[(epsilon_0+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2](1+2s_k)}
        *product_(i=1)^(k-1)[(b_i-1)/M_i^tr]^2.        (35)

For k=0 use f=Hub_(1/(1+2s_0)) from 1. Its closest radius is 1 and
its payoff is H_base(h). Every ordered interpolation residual is
nonpositive: writing z_a=x_a-grad f(x_a), expansion gives

    Q_ab=J(z_a)-J(z_b)+<grad f(x_a),z_b-z_a><=0

by the subgradient inequality. This includes all physical samples,
the terminal point, and every minimizer; the full original Gram matrix
is automatically positive semidefinite. No pair of interpolation rows
or gradient coordinates is removed.

## 6. The unchanged hierarchy and a local parameter dichotomy

Let

    r_h=c(c-2), v_h=a/(c-2)=beta/r_h,
    M_j=1+S_j, d_j=a c^j, u_j=d_j/M_j.                 (36)

Here u_j is hierarchy notation, distinct from the local horizontal
coordinate in Section 2. Writing x=tau/m gives

    r_h=1+(4+2sqrt(2))x+lambda^2 x^2>1>beta,
    M_j=v_h c^j+(1-v_h)2^j,
    0<v_h<1, 1<=M_j<=c^j, a<=u_j<=c-2.              (37)

The formula solves M_(j+1)=2M_j+d_j with M_0=1, by direct
substitution. Its inequalities follow from 2<c and 0<v_h<1. Throughout
the stated domain,

    2<c<121/50, 1/4<a<=u_j<21/50<1/2,
    0<r_h-1<2/m, r_h<2.                              (38)

For example, sqrt(2)<283/200 implies lambda<483/200, and
c<=lambda(1+1/6144)<484/200. Also beta>3/4, c<3 give a>1/4.
The expansion in (37) and 4+2sqrt(2)<7, lambda^2<6 give
r_h-1<7/(4m)+3/(8m^2)<=31/(16m)<2/m.

For EACH local pair with C_i<r_h, and only for that pair, (1) gives

    epsilon_i^2<C_i^2-1<4/m+4/m^2<=6/m<=1/256,
    epsilon_i<1/16, theta_sp,i>23/16,
    kappa_i theta_sp,i<log r_h<2/m,
    kappa_i<1/768<1/16, C_i<769/768.                   (39)

The angle estimate uses pi>3 and arctan epsilon_i<epsilon_i.
Set X_i=(1-kappa_i^2)theta_sp,i/kappa_i. The exponential branch in
(1) applies because kappa_i<1 is now proved. We have

    X_i>((1-kappa_i^2)theta_sp,i^2/2)m
         >(134895/131072)m>m.

The exact identity
1+(1+kappa_i)T_i=(exp(X_i)-kappa_i)/(1-kappa_i)>=exp(X_i)
therefore yields

    A_i>16 C_i exp(m).                               (40)

This is a statement about each stage separately and permits arbitrary
mixtures with stages having C_i>=r_h.

Among any nonempty selected subset choose a globally minimum tree
level j. Both complete H_j children of that checkpoint contain only
smaller levels, so they contain no selected point. For the adjacent
ordinary sums s,t and its physical step b,

    s>=M_j-1, t>=M_j-1, b=1+d_j.                      (41)

Deleting a selection only reclassifies its FULL physical b as ordinary,
merging sums into s+b+t. The original word, order, horizon and all
parameters of the remaining selected stages stay fixed. In all deletion
arguments below we compare explicitly defined upper functionals, never
assert monotonicity of actual backward states or actual functions.

## 7. Source: a common denominator for every mixed parameter choice

On H_m every ordinary block has s_i<=S_m<c^m<exp(m), since
exp(1)>5/2>c. The exact source denominator is

    C_i s_i+2A_i+epsilon_i b_i.

If C_i>=r_h, A_i>=2C_i makes this at least r_h(s_i+4). If C_i<r_h,
(40), r_h<2 and exp(m)>1 give

    C_i s_i+2A_i+epsilon_i b_i>32 exp(m)
                      >2(exp(m)+4)>r_h(s_i+4).        (42)

Thus every stage, with no shared C_i,A_i or epsilon_i, has the same
valid denominator lower bound. Combining it with the actual first
radius comparison (27) gives the single upper functional

    E(F,H_m)<=Psi_S,
    Psi_S=b_0^2/[(s_0+2)^2(1+s_k)]
                *product_(i=1)^(k-1)[b_i/(r_h(s_i+4))]^2. (43)

This comparison holds for every eligible subset, including subsets
formed by deleting selections while retaining all remaining parameters.
It is neither an asserted equality for actual transfer factors nor
a lower certificate used in the wrong direction.

We now prove all-count deletion for (43), including its exceptional
first factor. While k>=2 choose a global minimum level j. Put
M=M_j, d=d_j, u=d/M, b=1+d and let s,t be its adjacent sums.
By (38),(41), s,t>=M-1 and 0<u<1/2, and
u(2+u)<=c(c-2)=r_h.

FIRST SELECTED MINIMUM. Cancelling all unchanged factors, deletion is
valid exactly when

    b(s+b+t+2)<=(s+2)r_h(t+4).                        (44)

The right side minus the left increases with s and t: the derivatives
are respectively r_h(t+4)-b and r_h(s+2)-b, both positive on (41).
At s=t=M-1 the difference is

    M^2[r_h-u(2+u)]+M[4r_h-2-2u]+3r_h-1>=0.        (45)

Every displayed coefficient is nonnegative since r_h>1, u<=1/2.

NONFIRST, NONLAST SELECTED MINIMUM. The required comparison is

    b r_h(s+b+t+4)<=r_h^2(s+4)(t+4).

Divide by r_h. The difference again increases in s,t since both
derivatives have the form r_h(other+4)-b>0. At s=t=M-1 it equals

    M^2[r_h-u(2+u)]+M[6r_h-2-4u]+9r_h-3>=0.        (46)

This proves the interior case with all neighboring original parameter
differences already paid by (42).

LAST, NONFIRST SELECTED MINIMUM. The ratio before deletion to after
deletion is at most

    b^2(1+s+b+t)/[(s+4)^2(1+t)],                     (47)

because r_h>=1. It decreases with t. Its s derivative has the sign
2-s-2b-2t<0. Thus (41) bounds it by

    (d+1)^2(2M+d)/[(M+3)^2 M]
       <=(5/2)[(M/2+1)/(M+3)]^2<=5/8.               (48)

Each deletion makes Psi_S nondecreasing. Repeating until one selected
checkpoint remains is a finite symbolic induction, not an executed
recurrence. There is no attempt to extend its exceptional first factor
to an invented empty-selection formula.

For the last singleton set X=1+s,Y=1+t, so X,Y>=M and
S_m=X+Y+d-1. Its ratio to H_base(H_m) is

    (d+1)^2(2X+2Y+2d-1)/[(X+1)^2Y].                 (49)

It decreases in X,Y. The X derivative has sign 4-2X-4Y-4d<0;
the Y derivative has sign -(2X+2d-1)<0. Its maximum is at X=Y=M.
Source eligibility (2S) and epsilon_i<=1/2 give d>=9, hence M>=9/u.
Therefore

    (d+1)/(M+1)=u+(1-u)/(M+1)
                   <=10u/(9+u)<70/157<9/20,
    4+2u-1/M<2(121/50).

The strict last comparison in the first line is 1400<1413. Substitution
into (49) proves, for EVERY nonempty stagewise source selection,

    E(F,H_m)<(9801/10000)H_base(H_m).                 (50)

No common local pair was used in (42)--(50). Different parameters at
the first stage are paid by its genuine first-wedge distance estimate;
different parameters at all other stages are paid before deletion.

## 8. Main: actual full-step denominators with stage-dependent coefficients

First, for any one local pair define

    A_floor=epsilon+epsilon^(-1),
    C_floor=min(C,A_floor).

The function p(theta)+q(theta)/epsilon has minimum at an endpoint.
Its derivative divided by R is
(epsilon^(-1)-kappa)cos theta-(1+kappa/epsilon)sin theta. If its first
coefficient is nonpositive it is nonpositive throughout; otherwise it
changes sign once from positive to negative. The endpoint values are
C and A_floor. Every actual slope thus gives

    M_i^tr>=P_i+epsilon_i+(1+Q_i)/epsilon_i
       >=min(C_i,epsilon_i+epsilon_i^(-1))s_i
                                      +epsilon_i+epsilon_i^(-1). (51)

For C_i>=r_h, the inequality epsilon_i+epsilon_i^(-1)>=5/2>r_h
implies M_i^tr>=r_h(s_i+1). For C_i<r_h we need a surplus from the
actual chronological suffix, not an assertion M_i^tr>=A_i.

### 8.1 Exact discrete turning on each complete child

Fix ONE stage with C_i<r_h and suppress its subscript in this
subsection only. In addition to (39), the expansion in (37) gives

    kappa*m*theta_sp<44/25, log c<9/10.                (52)

Indeed r_h-1<7/(4m)+3/(8m^2)<44/(25m). Also
exp(9/10)>1+9/10+81/200+243/2000=4853/2000>121/50>c.

Run the Section 2 FULL-step backward definition on a complete H_j,
0<=j<=m, with its unchanged enclosing (m,tau). At each actual reverse
step alpha let y_mass=1+the sum of all steps strictly after it in this
H_j. If the step's tree level is ell, its full right child H_ell lies
after it, so y_mass>=M_ell. Define x_mass=alpha/y_mass. By (37),

    x_mass=(1+d_ell)/y_mass<=c-2+1/y_mass<3/2.         (53)

In reverse order y_mass starts at 1 and its successive increments are
the actual alpha's. This is mass bookkeeping retaining the exact order.

For x>=0, log(1+x)>=2x/(2+x), since the difference vanishes at zero
and has derivative x^2/[(1+x)(2+x)^2]>=0. Put
ell_mass=log(1+x_mass). Then

    x_mass<=(1+x_mass/2)ell_mass
                <=(c/2+1/(2y_mass))ell_mass.

Using log(1+x)<=x and (53),

    ell_mass/y_mass<=x_mass/y_mass
       =(1+x_mass)[1/y_mass-1/(y_mass+alpha)]
       <=(5/2)[1/y_mass-1/(y_mass+alpha)].

Both finite sums telescope in the ACTUAL reverse order. Thus

    Hharm_j:=sum_(steps of H_j) alpha/y_mass
                      <=(c/2)log M_j+5/4.             (54)

The empty H_0 has Hharm_0=0 and is included.

At a backward step write the PRE-update state as
r=rho_loc(cos phi_pol,-sin phi_pol), and the slope about to be added
as v(theta)=R(theta)e(theta). Put gamma_step=phi_pol-theta.
Section 2 implies 0<=gamma_step<pi/2: before a cap the current state
angle is at least its projected angle and the new projected angle is
no larger; at a cap theta=0. These statements include zero steps.
In the uncapped case, equation (7) is equivalent to

    sin gamma_step=kappa cos gamma_step
                       +kappa(alpha-1)R(theta)/rho_loc.

The cap test (6) gives the same relation with <= and R(theta)=C.
Consequently in BOTH branches,

    sin gamma_step<=kappa[1+alpha C/rho_loc].          (55)

Before making a small-angle estimate, (11) already gives the
unconditional bound rho_loc>=y_mass/sqrt(2). Combining (53), C<2,
sqrt(2)<3/2 and kappa<1/768 yields

    sin gamma_step<(11/2)kappa<11/1536<1/100.

As gamma_step is in [0,pi/2), this implies
cos gamma_step>eta_turn:=999/1000. Projection of the exact addition
r+alpha v(theta) onto r/rho_loc then gives

    rho_new>=rho_loc+alpha R(theta)cos gamma_step
                              >=rho_loc+eta_turn alpha.

The initial endpoint radius is at least 1, so induction improves the
PRE-update radius bound to

    rho_loc>=eta_turn y_mass.                         (56)

No absence of caps was assumed in deriving this bound.

Let delta_pol=phi_pol-phi_new>=0 be the polar-angle change in the
step and z_step=alpha R(theta)/rho_loc. Resolving the exact vector
addition along the old radial and tangent directions gives

    tan delta_pol=z_step sin gamma_step/
                              (1+z_step cos gamma_step).

The stronger relation preceding (55) gives
sin gamma_step<=kappa(1+z_step) in both branches. The denominator
is at least cos gamma_step(1+z_step). As delta_pol<=tan delta_pol,

    delta_pol<=kappa z_step/cos gamma_step
       <=[C/eta_turn^2]kappa alpha/y_mass
       <=(101/100)kappa alpha/y_mass.                 (57)

The last inequality uses C<769/768<501/500 and
(501/500)(1000/999)^2<101/100. Thus the full discrete change, including
the cap possibility, is bounded before summation.

For an uncapped POST-update state, (8) gives
0<=phi_new-theta=arctan(kappa nu/(1+nu))<=kappa. For a capped state
the proof of (6) gives y_new<=kappa(u_new-C), hence
phi_new<=arctan kappa<=kappa. In either case theta>=phi_new-kappa.
Summing (57), then applying (54), (52), and M_j<=c^j, gives every
projected angle the bound

    theta>=theta_sp-kappa[(101/100)Hharm_j+1]
       >theta_sp-(44/(25 theta_sp))(111/100)
       =theta_sp-1221/(625 theta_sp)>1/16.             (58)

For the middle inequality, the explicit uniform estimate is

    [(101/100)Hharm_j+1]/m
       <=(101/100)[1089/1000+5/(4m)]+1/m<111/100.

For the last inequality, theta_sp>23/16 implies
theta_sp(theta_sp-1/16)>(23/16)(22/16)=253/128>1221/625.
This excludes caps on a complete H_j ONLY AFTER caps have been included
in every preceding step of the argument. No continuous-flow formula
or approximation has replaced the discrete iteration.

Since R(theta)>=1 and theta>1/16, every actually used slope has
q(theta)>=sin(1/16)>1/32. The last bound follows by integrating
cos t>=1-t^2/2>1/2 on [0,1/16]. Together with p+q>=1 and
epsilon<1/16, it gives

    p+q/epsilon=p+q+(epsilon^(-1)-1)q>47/32.

Because 47/32-C>1/4 under (39), the actual complete-child output obeys

    P(H_j)+Q(H_j)/epsilon-C S_j>=S_j/4.               (59)

For j=0 both sides are zero; for a nonempty child the bound is strict.
The proof was for ONE low-C stage and hence holds separately for every
low-C stage, regardless of the parameters in surrounding stages.

### 8.2 From the suffix to an arbitrary incoming block

Let lev_i be the tree level of the i-th currently selected checkpoint.
For i>=1 set j_i=min(lev_(i-1),lev_i). The actual ordinary block_i ends
with the entire word H_(j_i). To see this directly, number physical
positions from zero. Induction in (3) gives level(t)=the exponent of 2
in t+1. Both selected endpoints have t+1 divisible by 2^(j_i), so their
distance is at least 2^(j_i). The last 2^(j_i)-1 positions before the
later selected checkpoint are precisely the unchanged H_(j_i).
The preceding selected position lies at or before that child's start
minus one. This proves the suffix claim, including an empty H_0.

The backward definition processes this suffix FIRST from that stage's
endpoint (epsilon_i,-1). Thus it has exactly the suffix outputs in
(59) with THAT stage's local parameters. Additional earlier ordinary
steps can cap; they still satisfy p+q/epsilon_i>=C_i by (51), because
C_i<r_h<epsilon_i+epsilon_i^(-1) in this low band. Hence

    P_i+Q_i/epsilon_i-C_i s_i>=S_(j_i)/4.

Also epsilon_i+epsilon_i^(-1)-C_i>1/4. The actual transfer definition
(28) therefore gives

    M_i^tr>=C_i(s_i+1)+M_(j_i)/4
           >=C_i(s_i+1)+(r_h-C_i)M_(j_i),            (60)

since 0<r_h-C_i<2/m<1/4. This never asserts the false replacement
M_i^tr>=A_i; it derives a different bound from the actual ordered block.

Define, FOR EACH REMAINING SELECTED STAGE, the effective coefficient

    gamma_i=min(C_i,r_h), Delta_i=r_h-gamma_i,
    D_i^cmp=gamma_i(s_i+1)+Delta_i M_min(lev_(i-1),lev_i), i>=1. (61)

Equations (51) and (60) jointly prove M_i^tr>=D_i^cmp for every
stage, including arbitrary mixtures of high-C and low-C stages. Always
1<=gamma_i<=r_h and Delta_i>=0. Each gamma_i remains attached to its
original physical checkpoint after a deletion; no remaining local
parameter or physical step is retuned.

Using (11) only for the FIRST true radius in (35) yields the common
upper functional for all these mixed instances:

    E(f,H_m)<=Psi_M,
    Psi_M=2(b_0-1)^2/[(s_0+1)^2(1+2s_k)]
                    *product_(i=1)^(k-1)[(b_i-1)/D_i^cmp]^2. (62)

This is a comparison of the exact physical payoff (35). The actual
P_i,Q_i remain order-dependent. The comparison denominators in (61)
are recalculated by their own definitions on a smaller selected subset;
they are never identified with either set of actual backward outputs.

## 9. Main: deletion with TWO DIFFERENT neighboring coefficients

While at least two selections remain choose a globally minimum tree
level j. Put M=M_j, d=d_j, u=d/M, b=1+d and X=1+s,Y=1+t, so
X,Y>=M by (41). The new incoming mass plus one after deletion is

    1+s+b+t=X+Y+d.                                    (63)

FIRST SELECTED MINIMUM. Let xi=gamma_next be the effective coefficient
of the next selected checkpoint. Its incoming comparison denominator
before deletion is xi Y+(r_h-xi)M. Cancellation in (62) shows that the
first-factor comparison is exactly

    d(X+Y+d)<=X[xi Y+(r_h-xi)M].                      (64)

The difference increases in X,Y: its derivatives are
xi Y+(r_h-xi)M-d and xi X-d, both positive for X,Y>=M because
xi>=1>u. At X=Y=M the difference is

    M^2[r_h-u(2+u)]>=0.

This pays the true first-radius factor. No coefficient of the deleted
first stage needs to match xi, and epsilon_0 was already paid in (34).

NONFIRST, NONLAST SELECTED MINIMUM. Let eta=gamma_i and
xi=gamma_(i+1), with NO relation imposed between them. The two incoming
denominators are

    eta X+(r_h-eta)M, xi Y+(r_h-xi)M.                 (65)

After deletion, the next stage retains xi. Its newly determined
adjacent-level suffix H_J is contained in the new incoming block, by
Section 8.2. Therefore M_J<=X+Y+d and

    D_new^cmp=xi(X+Y+d)+(r_h-xi)M_J
                                      <=r_h(X+Y+d).  (66)

It suffices to prove the mixed original comparison inequality

    d r_h(X+Y+d)
       <=[eta X+(r_h-eta)M][xi Y+(r_h-xi)M].          (67)

The right side minus the left has X derivative at least
eta r_h M-d r_h=r_h M(eta-u)>0. Its Y derivative is at least
r_h M(xi-u)>0. Thus its minimum on X,Y>=M is at X=Y=M, where it equals

    r_h M^2[r_h-u(2+u)]>=0.                          (68)

This proves (67) for every eta,xi in [1,r_h]. In particular it covers
high-high, low-low with different coefficients, and BOTH ordered mixed
cases. The new suffix may have larger tree level; the entire resulting
increase of its comparison denominator is paid by (66). There is no
common-parameter hypothesis or unproved deletion of an actual state.

LAST, NONFIRST SELECTED MINIMUM. Equation (61) gives D_i^cmp>=1+s.
The selected-to-deleted ratio in (62) is therefore at most

    d^2[1+2(s+b+t)]/[(1+s)^2(1+2t)].                 (69)

It decreases in s,t: differentiation in s leaves a negative multiple
of s+2b+2t, and in t a negative multiple of s+b. At s=t=M-1 it equals

    u^2[(4+2u)M-1]/(2M-1)<=u^2(3+2u)<=1.            (70)

The first comparison follows after cross multiplication from
2(1+u)(M-1)>=0. The second uses 0<u<=1/2 and monotonicity of
u^2(3+2u), which equals one at u=1/2.

Every minimum-level deletion makes Psi_M nondecreasing, for the SAME
remaining independently parameterized stages. Tied minimum levels
also obey all the displayed inequalities. Repeat to one eligible
checkpoint. Its upper ratio to H_base, with X=1+s,Y=1+t, is

    2d^2(2X+2Y+2d-1)/[X^2(2Y-1)].                   (71)

It decreases in X,Y. Indeed the derivative in X of the variable
fraction has sign -2X-4Y-4d+2<0, and in Y sign -4X-4d<0. At
X=Y=M this is

    2u^2[2+u+(1+u)/(2M-1)].

Main eligibility (2M) gives d>=epsilon_i^(-2)>=4, so
M=d/u>200/21>19/2. Using u<21/50,

    2+u+(1+u)/(2M-1)<121/50+(71/50)/18
                                  =2249/900<5/2.

Consequently EVERY nonempty independently parameterized main selection
satisfies the finite uniform bound

    E(f,H_m)<(441/500)H_base(H_m).                    (72)

The argument retains the actual maximum in M_i^tr, all ordered
ordinary blocks, the exact first closest radius, all physical steps,
and all original eligibility constraints. A shared local pair is used
nowhere in (61)--(72).

## 10. Original benchmark and its saved accepted enclosure

Only fact_id04219554e41d9750 supplies the benchmark inputs in this
section. For x,y>=0 let

    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2.

Set C_0=B_0=1 and, for every integer n>=1,

    C_n=max_(0<=j<n)Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n)G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.    (73)

These are the ORIGINAL ALL-split recurrences. They are definitions,
not recurrences executed in this round. The accepted fact gives the
positive finite limit Z_1=lim_m lambda^(-m)B_(2^m-1), and for every
integer j>=0,

    lambda^(-j)B_(2^j-1)<=Z_1
             <=lambda^(-j)(sqrt(B_(2^j))+3/100)^2.     (74)

The accepted fact's Section 8 certifies the public fixed interval table
/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/audit_c78/input_interval_table.csv,
SHA256 2a7585bc0bade9f85461456c13d60bbe618f85c7cc816e1ddc5b4c1a1d611e68.
In this round its header, row 1024 and matching hash were read; no
generator, checker, recurrence, or arithmetic program was run. With
D80=1208925819614629174706176, the saved accepted upper endpoint is

    D80 B_1024<=19229705732238461978760749948<15910 D80.

Since sqrt(2)>140/99, we have
lambda^10=3363+2378sqrt(2)>6725; the last numerator difference after
multiplication by 99 is 82. Also sqrt(15910)<127. Applying (74) at
j=10 gives the written scalar bound

    Z_1<(15910+381/50+9/10000)/6725
                               <15918/6725<237/100.  (75)

For the physical hierarchy, (37) implies
v_h>1-9/(4m)>255/256 and lambda^(-m)<1/256. For the first bound,
r_h-beta<9/(4m) follows by expanding (37), using tau<=1/4,
5+2sqrt(2)<8, lambda^2<6, and m>=2; division by r_h>1 improves it.
The exact physical mass identity is

    (1+2S_m)/lambda^m
      =2v_h(1+tau/m)^m+2(1-v_h)(2/lambda)^m-lambda^(-m). (76)

For m>=2, the first three binomial terms give
(1+tau/m)^m>=1+tau+tau^2/4. At tau=1/4, (76) is therefore strictly
greater than 2(255/256)(81/64)-1/256=20623/8192>5/2. Uniformly for
1/5<=tau<=1/4, it is greater than
2(255/256)(121/100)-1/256=6161/2560>12/5. Thus, for EVERY m>=1536,

    B_(n_m)H_base(H_(m,1/4))<237/250,
    B_(n_m)H_base(H_(m,tau))<79/80, 1/5<=tau<=1/4.     (77)

For each fixed 0<tau<=1/4, v_h tends to 1, (1+tau/m)^m tends to
exp(tau), and (2/lambda)^m tends to zero. Equations (74),(76) give

    lim_m B_(n_m)H_base(H_(m,tau))=Z_1/(2exp(tau)).    (78)

No supremum over local parameters is exchanged with a limit.

## 11. Boundary and cross-stage audit

ZERO BLOCKS. For an empty or all-zero local list, P=Q=0 and every
backward state and projected slope equals (epsilon,-1). In the main
family (28) becomes sigma=1/max(epsilon b,epsilon+epsilon^(-1)),
D_next=D sigma(b-1), ell_next=D sigma. In the source family it becomes
sigma'=1/(epsilon b/2+A), D'_next=D' sigma' b/2, ell'_next=0.
These different shifts are retained in (23) and (33); they are not
interchanged. Arbitrary distinct epsilons in two neighboring such
stages still satisfy the individual pre- and post-zero-region tests
in Sections 4--5. In particular the main closest center lands exactly
at the next stage's shifted first coordinate zero, whereas the source
center need not satisfy the later rows. Formula (24), rather than a
false singleton-radius extrapolation, keeps all those source rows.

ARBITRARILY LARGE ORDINARY STEPS. A local step with
alpha>=1+(1/kappa-epsilon)/C caps immediately at the endpoint by (6),
giving P=C alpha,Q=0 for a single-step list. Once a longer backward
list caps, all earlier steps remain capped by the normal calculation.
The unrestricted gluing statements cover both cases. The stronger
angle bound (58) is used only for complete hierarchy children; a
prefix before that child is allowed to cap in (60). Thus neither
large steps nor chronology have been discarded in deriving (61).

ELIGIBILITY ENDPOINTS. Conditions (13), (15), (30), and every
activation and tail check are non-strict when equality is permitted
in (2S) or (2M). The strict payoff margins arise from the hierarchy
bounds (38), not from excluding endpoints. The source physical
checkpoint is b_i but its local checkpoint is b_i/2; the main local
checkpoint is the full b_i. They produce respectively b_i and b_i-1
in their transfer numerators, exactly as derived in (18),(28).

DIFFERENT NEIGHBORING PARAMETERS. In the geometry, each old or future
plane is tested against its own epsilon_i wedge (15). The main closest
center uses ell_1=sigma_0(1+Q_0), independent of epsilon_1. In the
source comparison, low C_i pays its own enormous A_i through (42),
and high C_i pays C_i(s_i+4). Both yield the SAME comparison lower
bound before deletion; no neighboring A_i or C_i is substituted.
In the main comparison, all mixed possibilities reduce to independent
eta,xi in [1,r_h]. The exact two-coefficient inequality is (67), and
the possible change to a larger adjacent child is paid by (66).
Finally (64) deals separately with the first factor. These are the
cross-stage interfaces a common-parameter proof would not by itself
establish.

WORD AND RADIUS PROVENANCE. Every selected b_i is an original physical
entry at its original position, with level defined by the exponent of
2 in position+1. Each ordinary block is the intervening original
chronological list. Every child uses the same enclosing (m,tau).
The parameter policy is any deterministic choice of the local pairs
and subset with full knowledge of that word; all scales are then
the positive exact definitions (17)--(19) or (28)--(29). The physical
trajectory and scalar query values are (21),(22) or (31),(32).
Source normalization uses the full distance (24); main normalization
uses the exact closest center (34). The advertised global smoothness
is one in both families. No smaller local Lipschitz constant is used
to retune a physical step or improve a displayed normalized score.

## 12. Complete joint stagewise obstruction candidate

THEOREM (UNVERIFIED joint analytical statement).
Fix any integer m>=1536 and real 0<tau<=1/4, and let the physical word
H_(m,tau) be exactly (3), with n_m=2^m-1 and S_m its physical sum.
For every ordered subset of its original positions, of any size
1<=k<=n_m, and for every independently chosen local array
(kappa_i,epsilon_i)_(0<=i<k) with kappa_i>0 and 0<epsilon_i<=1/2,
chosen before defining one static objective, the following statements
hold for the two separate constructions.

(a) If each selected physical b_i satisfies (2S), the source half-sum
is exactly (17)--(19). It is an everywhere finite convex differentiable
1-smooth function, has attained minimum zero, and follows every
original physical step with the exact query data (21),(22). Its full
minimum set is (23), its TRUE squared closest radius is (24), and

    E(F,H_(m,tau))<(9801/10000)/(1+2S_m).             (79S)

(b) If each selected physical b_i satisfies (2M), the main envelope
is exactly (28)--(29). It is an everywhere finite convex differentiable
1-smooth function, has attained minimum zero, and follows every
original physical step with the exact query data (31),(32). Its full
minimum set is (33), its TRUE closest center and radius are (34), its
exact terminal payoff is (35), and

    E(f,H_(m,tau))<(441/500)/(1+2S_m).                (79M)

These bounds are finite and uniform at m>=1536 over all counts, all
positions, all eligible endpoints, and all independently chosen stage
parameters, including arbitrary mixtures of C_i>=r_h and C_i<r_h.
All original ordered interpolation inequalities hold, with no omitted
query, terminal, or minimizer row. For any L>0, multiplication of the
constructed objective by L and use of physical steps h_t/L gives the
same conclusions with the normalized payoff E_L defined in Section 1.

Define L_source,stage to be the supremum of the ACTUAL closest-distance
payoffs over all source selections and independent parameter arrays,
including the source k=0 member in Section 4. Define L_main,stage
similarly, including the main k=0 Huber member in Section 5. Define
L_both,stage to be the supremum over the union of these TWO separately
specified actual families, and
L_source,stage^H=max{L_source,stage,H_base}. Then

    L_source,stage(H_(m,tau))=1/[2(1+S_m)],            (80S)
    L_main,stage(H_(m,tau))=L_both,stage(H_(m,tau))
       =L_source,stage^H(H_(m,tau))=1/(1+2S_m).        (80M)

In particular, for EVERY integer m>=1536,

    B_(n_m)L_both,stage(H_(m,1/4))<237/250,
    B_(n_m)L_both,stage(H_(m,tau))<79/80,
                                      1/5<=tau<=1/4. (81)

The same bounds hold for the other three named optima. For each fixed
0<tau<=1/4 all four have the exact B-scaled limit

    Z_1/(2exp(tau)).                                  (82)

PROOF. Sections 2--5 jointly derive all local states, all-row static
gluing, global smoothness, physical query values, minimum sets and
closest radii. Section 7 bounds the actual source payoff by a common
parameter-independent upper functional and proves its all-count bound
(50). Sections 8--9 bound the actual main payoff by its true first
radius and actual incoming blocks, establish the low-C discrete surplus
independently at each stage, and prove deletion with different
neighboring coefficients, giving (72). These establish (79S),(79M).

For (80S), the source k=0 payoff divided by H_base equals
1-1/(2M_m). Since M_m>=2^m>=32, this is at least
63/64>9801/10000. Thus every nonempty source selection is strictly below
its source k=0 member. The main k=0 member attains H_base, while all
nonempty main and source members are strictly below it by (79S),(79M).
This proves every equality in (80M). Equations (77),(78) then give
(81),(82). For L_source,stage, its ratio to H_base tends to one because
M_m>=2^m tends to infinity. No parameter supremum is exchanged with a
limit, and all components have been proved in this same joint draft.

EXACT SCOPE. Independent stage parameters yield no escape WITHIN THESE
TWO ACTUAL FAMILIES on the stated hierarchy, according to this candidate.
This is not an upper bound for unrestricted W_n(H_m), not a statement
for tau>1/4, not an obstruction to a different support curve, branching
architecture, extra checkpoint, or a different objective family, and
not a resolution of the original all-word/all-horizon exact-profile
minimax Goal. The older accepted tau=1 witness is not contradicted.
All new mathematics here remains UNVERIFIED; no fact has been submitted
or accepted in this round.

## 13. Retrieval, resources, and notation

# Exact fixed-integer-ray thresholds for five- and six-step seeds

Status: COMPLETE JOINT PROOF DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. This is max3 round38,
the ONE analytical round allocated by TASK.md and guidance3acdda5439874057
after completed37. Completed ceiling38 / attempted ceiling39 are unchanged.
There is no verifier authorization. No new numerical evaluation, DP,
interval replay, solver, machine algebra, consult or subagent was used.
All rational polynomial checks below are written-out mathematical arguments.

The sole accepted mathematical input is fact04219554e41d9750, including
its Sections2--3,7--9 and10--13. Its general seed substitution is USED,
not offered again as a new result. The earlier longer-seed draft
174b1eeb2cdd259f is awareness only; its scalar comparison is rederived
here. No unverified finding is a premise. The original all-function
exact-profile target remains OPEN, and no actual improving seed is supplied.

## 1. Statement and original benchmark

For a nonnegative integer n and a nonnegative word h of length n, W_n(h)
is the supremum of

  2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L ||x_0-x_star||^2)

over every finite Euclidean dimension, every convex differentiable f
with L-Lipschitz gradient and a minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, under
x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Here L>0; this is W=2R.

For a,d>=0 put

  Omega(a,d)=(a+d+sqrt((a+d)^2+4ad))/2,
  G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2.

Retain the ORIGINAL ALL-split recurrences

  C_0=B_0=1,
  C_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),
  B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)),
  U_n^ZJ=1/B_n,   lambda=1+sqrt(2),   rho=log_2(lambda).

The accepted fact defines the positive finite limits

  Z_b=lim_(j->infinity) lambda^(-j) B_(b*2^j-1),  integer b>=1.

Freeze b=3 for BOTH lengths before making any limiting comparison. Define

  Gamma5_c107=7977607/806000,
  Gamma6_c107=387153999/32240000.

For r in {5,6}, Gamma_r_c107 means the corresponding displayed constant.
Every dyadic depth j below is a nonnegative integer.

The new strict sufficient bounds are

  Z_18/Z_3 < Gamma5_c107 < 99/10 < 292666/29275,             (1)
  Z_21/Z_3 < Gamma6_c107 < 1201/100 < 90/7.                (2)

Consequently, CONDITIONAL on an actual full-certified primitive seed
of length r in {5,6} and capacity A=1+sum h>Gamma_r_c107, the original
substitution from the accepted fact, with n_j=3*2^j-1, has actual horizon

  N_j=(r+1)(3*2^j-1).

Let epsilon_c107=1-Gamma_r_c107/A, so 0<epsilon_c107<1. For EVERY integer

  j >= ceil(log(6/epsilon_c107)/log(3/2)),                  (3)

the substituted physical word satisfies

  W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j)) < (1-epsilon_c107/2) U_(N_j)^ZJ.    (4)

In particular each of the following separate sufficient conditions gives
the uniform strict margin 1/16000 for EVERY j>=27:

  r=5, A>=99/10,      N_j=18*2^j-6;
  r=6, A>=1201/100,   N_j=21*2^j-7.                      (5)

The full seed certificate meant here is stated in Section6. The constants
are upper enclosures, not a lower barrier or a global optimum over rays.
In particular (1)--(2) compare sufficient bounds; they do NOT establish
that the true ratios at b=3 are below the true ratios at b=1.

## 2. All-split comparison with primitive capacities

We prove jointly that

  G(a,lambda d)<=lambda Omega(a,d)   for all a,d>=0.       (6)

When a=d=0 both sides are zero. Otherwise put w=Omega(a,d)>0 and t=a/w.
The equation w^2-(a+d)w-ad=0 gives

  0<=t<=1,    d/w=(1-t)/(1+t).

The larger root of P(v)=v^2-(4a+lambda d)v+4a^2 is G(a,lambda d).
Since lambda^2=2lambda+1, substitution and expansion give

  P(lambda w)/w^2
    =lambda^2-4lambda t-lambda^2*(1-t)/(1+t)+4t^2
    =4t*(t-1/sqrt(2))^2/(1+t) >=0.

Also w>=a+d, so lambda w>=lambda(a+d)>=2a+lambda d/2,
the vertex of P. A quadratic of positive leading coefficient is
nonnegative to the right of its vertex only at or beyond its larger
root. This proves (6), including either zero argument.

Both kernels are increasing in each nonnegative argument directly from
their displayed formulas. Since B_0=1<=lambda C_0, induction over every
ORIGINAL split k+l=n-1 gives

  G(C_k,B_l)<=G(C_k,lambda C_l)
             <=lambda Omega(C_k,C_l)<=lambda C_n.

Taking the maximum over all splits proves B_n<=lambda C_n. The accepted
symmetric identity C_(b*2^j-1)=lambda^j C_(b-1) therefore implies

  Z_b<=lambda C_(b-1),  for every fixed integer b>=1.      (7)

No optimizing split for B_n has been selected or discarded.

## 3. Exact discrete primitive identities and rational enclosures

The accepted fact's Section2 gives the balanced identities for U_N=C_(N-1),
with U_0=0:

  U_(2a)=lambda U_a,
  U_(2a+1)=Omega(U_a,U_(a+1)).

They are statements about the symmetric ALL-split recurrence, not a
balanced assumption about B. Set the following new local quantities:

  c107=C_2=Omega(1,lambda),
  p107=C_4=Omega(lambda,c107),
  u107=C_8=Omega(lambda^2,p107),
  v107=C_10=Omega(p107,lambda*c107),
  w107=C_20=Omega(lambda*p107,v107).

The identities also give C_5=lambda*c107, C_9=lambda*p107, and
C_17=lambda*u107. Thus (7) yields

  Z_18<=lambda^2*u107,     Z_21<=lambda*w107.              (8)

Here are complete rational upper checks. First

  12/5<lambda<169/70,   lambda^2=2lambda+1<204/35,

because (7/5)^2<2<(99/70)^2. For positive a,d define

  P_(a,d)(x)=x^2-(a+d)x-ad.

Its positive root is Omega(a,d). Each proposed x in the table below
exceeds a+d; hence P_(a,d)(x)>0 proves x>Omega(a,d).

quantity    a                 d                  x          P_(a,d)(x)
c107        1                 169/70             502/125    2405/1093750
p107        169/70            502/125            1923/250   4084/437500
u107        204/35            1923/250           407/25     3943/43750
v107        1923/250          42419/4375         419/20     854258/17500000
w107        324987/17500      419/20             23841/500  804318/8750000

The exact substitutions used in the last two rows are

  lambda*c107 < (169/70)(502/125)=42419/4375,
  lambda*p107 < (169/70)(1923/250)=324987/17500.

For an explicit audit of the larger rows, the u107 numerator on
denominator43750 is

  11595430-9630027-1961460=3943.

The v107 numerator on denominator17500000 is

  7680793750-6374791700-1305147792=854258.

The w107 numerator on denominator8750000 is

  19893764835-16488721692-3404238825=804318.

All entries are strictly positive. Monotonicity and the preceding exact
identities prove, in particular,

  u107<407/25,     w107<23841/500.                         (9)

We will also need c107>4. The polynomial defining c107 has value
16-4(1+lambda)-lambda=12-5lambda<0 at4, so its positive root exceeds4.

## 4. The accepted finite endpoint gives the denominator

Use ONLY the saved endpoint in accepted04219554e41d9750 Sections8--9:

  D80=1208925819614629174706176,
  L3071=77954524439662110401113475160,
  B_3071>=L3071/D80,
  L3071-64000D80=583271984325843219918211160.

No part of the defining DP or certificate check is rerun. Subtracting the
displayed integer480D80 gives the purely textual comparison

  L3071-64480D80=2987590910821216059246680>0.               (10)

The accepted lower ray enclosure at b=3,j=10 has finite index3071,
so

  Z_3>=lambda^(-10) B_3071
     >=lambda^(-10) L3071/D80
      >64480/lambda^10.                                  (11)

The elementary power identities

  lambda^5=41+29sqrt(2),
  lambda^6=99+70sqrt(2),
  lambda^10=3363+2378sqrt(2),
  lambda^12=19601+13860sqrt(2)

follow by multiplication using lambda^2=2lambda+1. Since the conjugates
of the even powers are their positive reciprocals,

  lambda^10+lambda^(-10)=6726,
  lambda^12+lambda^(-12)=39202.

It follows that

  lambda^10<6726,
  lambda^12<39202,
  lambda^11<6726*(169/70)=1136694/70<16239.               (12)

Equations (8)--(12) now give

  Z_18/Z_3 < lambda^12*u107/64480
            < 39202*(407/25)/64480
             =7977607/806000=Gamma5_c107,

  Z_21/Z_3 < lambda^11*w107/64480
            < 16239*(23841/500)/64480
             =387153999/32240000=Gamma6_c107.             (13)

The strict comparisons to the convenient capacities are

  99/10-Gamma5_c107=1793/806000>0,
  1201/100-Gamma6_c107=48401/32240000>0.

The improvement over the previously sufficient b=1 bounds needs no
assumption that those unverified proofs are accepted:

  292666/29275-99/10=28435/292750>0,
  90/7-1201/100=593/700>0.

This proves (1)--(2) as one joint argument from the accepted input.

## 5. Quantitative approach to the fixed denominator ray

The accepted Section9 gives, with n_j=3*2^j-1,

  0<=Z_3-lambda^(-j)B_(n_j)<=E(3,j),

  E(3,j)=8sqrt(2)*3^(rho-1)*2^(-j)
          +(3/25)*4^(rho/2)*lambda^(-j/2)
          +(9/10000)*lambda^(-j).

Define q107_j=(2/3)^j. The accepted bound1<rho<4/3 implies
3^(rho-1)<3/2, since3<(3/2)^3. Also sqrt(2)<3/2,
4^(rho/2)=lambda<5/2, and sqrt(lambda)>3/2. Consequently

  E(3,j)
    <18*2^(-j)+(3/10)*lambda^(-j/2)+(1/1000)*lambda^(-j)
    <=(18+3/10+1/1000)q107_j<19q107_j.                   (14)

Equation (11) and lambda^10<6726 give

  Z_3>64480/6726>19/2,

where the last comparison is128960>127794. Hence

  B_(n_j)>lambda^j Z_3(1-2q107_j).                       (15)

The accepted lower comparison B_n>=C_n and the exact symmetric identity
give the second useful estimate

  B_(n_j)>=C_(n_j)=lambda^j*c107>4lambda^j.              (16)

Neither estimate evaluates a new B value.

## 6. Actual seed requirement and the physical output

Fix r in {5,6}. The hypothesis is an ACTUAL nonnegative word
h=(h_0,...,h_(r-1)), s=sum h_i and A=1+s>Gamma_r_c107, together with
nonnegative real Lambda_ij for EVERY ordered distinct i,j in{0,...,r}
and Lambda_ii=0. Let e be the all-ones column of length r+1 and set

  T_ii=1,   T_it=h_t for t<i,   T_it=0 for t>i,
  L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
  omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,
  D_prim=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(r-1)(2-h_(r-1)),-s(s+2)).

Require the FULL original-coordinate conditions

  L_flow^T e=omega,
  M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2 >=0 on R^(r+1).

This means30 multipliers and all6 formal gradients for r=5, or42
multipliers and all7 formal gradients for r=6. A zero physical step
does not permit deletion or equality of its formal gradient coordinate.
No rank, support, symmetry or remainder-zero condition is imposed.

Accepted04219554e41d9750 Sections10--13 applies for these general lengths.
For each n choose any fully expanded maximizing tree of the ORIGINAL
B_n recurrence. Replace its n primitive empty leaves by this actual h;
leave its single terminal dominant empty leaf empty. Retain all internal
nodes and recompute the physical bridges specified in that accepted fact:

* A primitive join with capacities A_left,A_right uses bridge1+d_pp,
  where d_pp(A_left+A_right+d_pp)=A_left*A_right.
* A dominant join with primitive capacity A_left and right mass D_right
  uses bridge1+A_left/zeta, where
  zeta=(1+sqrt(1+8A_left/D_right))/2.

Use the accepted names Hsub_n for the resulting word and Dsub_n for
its mass1+2sum Hsub_n. Its conclusions, including payment for the
untouched dominant leaf and the Huber lower witness, are

  length(Hsub_n)=(r+1)n,
  W_((r+1)n)(Hsub_n)=1/Dsub_n,
  Dsub_n>=A B_n(1-ell_n)^2,
  ell_n=(1-A^(-1/2))/sqrt(B_n).

These are cited accepted results, not new composition claims.

For n=n_j, (16) gives 0<=ell_(n_j)<q107_j/2. Also

  N_j=(r+1)n_j=3(r+1)2^j-(r+1).

By monotonicity of B and its accepted lower ray enclosure at
b=3(r+1),

  B_(N_j)<=B_(3(r+1)2^j-1)<=lambda^j Z_(3(r+1)).          (17)

Thus the finite output is compared at its TRUE horizon; no offset is
silently removed. For q107_j<1/3, (15)--(17) and (13) give

  B_(N_j) W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j))
     < (Gamma_r_c107/A)
        /[(1-2q107_j)(1-q107_j/2)^2]
     <=(Gamma_r_c107/A)/(1-3q107_j),                     (18)

because

  (1-2q)(1-q/2)^2=1-3q+(9/4)q^2-(1/2)q^3>=1-3q

for0<=q<1/3. The already accepted shifted-ray statement also gives the
exact conditional limiting ratio

  B_(N_j) W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j)) -> Z_(3(r+1))/(A Z_3),

with the same finite offset. The finite bound (18) is stronger than an
unspecified eventuality.

If q107_j<=epsilon_c107/6, (18) implies

  B_(N_j) W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j))
    <(1-epsilon_c107)/(1-epsilon_c107/2)
    <1-epsilon_c107/2.

The final strict inequality follows by subtracting: the square
(1-epsilon_c107/2)^2 exceeds1-epsilon_c107 byepsilon_c107^2/4.
Condition (3) ensures exactly q107_j<=epsilon_c107/6, proving (4).

For the convenient capacities in (5), the respective lower margins are

  1-Gamma5_c107/(99/10)=1793/7979400>1/8000,
  1-Gamma6_c107/(1201/100)=48401/387202400>1/8000.

For the second strict comparison,
48401*8000-387202400=5600>0; the first follows from
1793*8000=14344000>7979400. Increasing A only increases epsilon_c107.
Finally

  (3/2)^27=((3/2)^10)^2*(3/2)^7
           >57^2*17=55233>48000,

using59049>57*1024 and2187>17*128. Thus for EVERY j>=27,
q107_j<1/48000<epsilon_c107/6. Equation (4) gives the claimed strict
factor1-1/16000 at the respective actual horizons.

## 7. Scope and handoff

The deliverable of this round is the new JOINT UNVERIFIED threshold
proof, with the concrete full-seed interface and an explicit starting
depth. Both strict thresholds improve the assigned sufficient constants.
No seed existence, all-word lower bound, global ray optimum, or ordering
of the true b=1 and b=3 ratios is inferred. Dyadic rescaling alone is
not a new phase: the definition gives Z_(2b)=lambda Z_b, hence preserves
Z_((r+1)b)/Z_b. Nothing here asserts density or continuity of phases.

The alternating weak seeds discussed in0421d0c1066b8f2d are NOT rescued
by this calculation. That unverified object's separate pointwise
domination claim is not a premise here. A genuinely stronger actual
seed must meet Section6 before the conditional consequence can be used.

The H_c<0 geometric lane remains deferred and unchanged. The original
all-n/all-nonnegative-word exact-profile Goal remains OPEN. Under the
current zero formal allowance there is no fact_submit, no new fact_id,
and no claim of independent verification. This is the completion of the
one assigned analytical pilot, not a renewal or completion of the project.
