<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001003 -->
# A value-repaired actual fifteen-step affine parent

Status: **COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED PROOF CANDIDATE**, worker max,
completed-round target 28. There are no new numerical evaluations,
solver calls, arithmetic replays, installations, subordinate agents, or
verifier calls. This is not an accepted fact. Accepted fact
`1252b8823bb5a290` supplies necessity and constructive sufficiency of the
original smooth-convex interpolation inequalities. Accepted fact
`b98bb807d514e80d` supplies the original benchmark notation. Everything
new used in this construction is derived together below.

The source lead was finding `36543919f8b588b6`. Its two-row exclusion
retained the sampled values. Here two genuinely queried values change.
The source lead is not a mathematical predecessor of this proof.

## 1. Result and the full model in which the construction sits

For every sigma in [9999/10000,1], the changed seven-step geometry
defined below has a legal fifteen-step parent on its specified next
word. The parent uses an explicit rank-one cross coupling and changes
only two values in its left copy:

    f_2 -> f_2-d_atom,
    f_6 -> f_6-alpha^2 d_atom,
    d_atom=(1-rho)(K-1)eta>0.                         (1)

Every position and gradient of both children is retained. All other
values are retained with their appropriate spatial-square scaling.
One explicit static, globally defined, convex 1-smooth function realizes
all sixteen samples. All 272 distinct original ordered interpolation
pairs, including the optimum, hold. The exact minimizer set is a convex
hull of three specified residuals; the true initial distance is R.

The physical word is

    h_3=(a,b,a),
    h_7=(h_3,1+r_0,h_3),
    h_15=(h_7,1+r_1,h_7).                            (2)

Its true-distance normalized payoff V_15 and capacity state satisfy

    W_15(h_15) >= V_15 = beta^2 alpha^2 N/R^2,
    kappa_15=(lambda^2 A)^2 V_15 > 1+1/1000.         (3)

This is a finite capacity surplus, not a comparison replacing the
original denominator B_15 by capacity squared. The exact original
benchmark ratio of this witness is B_15 V_15. It supplies neither an
arbitrary-h result nor an all-depth construction.

For clarity, the FULL released affine parent model has arbitrary
similarities E,F of the fixed child space with E^T E=ell I and
F^T F=nu I, ell,nu>=0, arbitrary cross Gram, and physical relative
translation t_join=E d-F P. Its left samples are (E X_i,E G_i), its
right queried samples are (t_join+F X_j,F G_j), and its final point is
t_join+F X_7 with a free terminal gradient. Every value and one common
minimizer are free; the full Gram, including optimum and terminal
vectors, is PSD. All original rows are imposed. The normalization uses
distance to an actual common minimizer, and a claimed witness must price
its nearest-minimizer distance.

We construct a point of this full model by choosing ell=1, nu=beta^2,
t_join=0 and an explicit F. This is a SUFFICIENT CONSTRUCTION, not a
restriction asserted lossless for the full model. The model's free
terminal gradient is legally selected as F G_7. Its incoming rows are
checked too. Independent scales, translations, values, and PSD range
conditions are not discarded in an impossibility claim.

## 2. Exact atom and its original rows

Use Euclidean inner products throughout. Define

    s=sqrt(2), lambda=1+s, theta=s-1,
    a=(311s-16)/300, b=(590+8s)/300, rho=599/600,
    z=b-1, K=a+rho, m=a+b, eta=z/K,
    A=1+2a+b=(143+105s)/50,
    r_0=theta A=(67+38s)/50, r_1=A,
    Dlen=m+K=A-1/600.

Take orthogonal U,V in R^2, with ||U||=1 and ||V||^2=eta.
Put w=rho V and p=m U+K V. The atom is

    g=(U,U,V,w),
    x=(p,bU+KV,KV,w),
    f_i=<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2, 0<=i<=3.

It obeys the three updates (a,b,a). Its residuals y_i=x_i-g_i
and energies E_i=<g_i,y_i> are

    y_0=(m-1)U+KV, E_0=m-1,
    y_1=zU+KV,     E_1=z,
    y_2=(K-1)V,    E_2=e=(K-1)eta,
    y_3=0,         E_3=0.

For any jet define aheight_i=f_i-<g_i,x_i>+||g_i||^2/2 and
Delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2. The original convention is

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =aheight_i-Delta_j+<g_i,y_j>.                (4)

For the atom aheight_i=0 and Delta_i=E_i. Its complete table of
<v-g_j,y_j>, for receiver j and possible source gradient v, is

| j | v=0 | v=U | v=V | v=w |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 | -(m-1) | 0 | -a | -a-(1-rho)z |
| 1 | -z | 0 | 0 | -(1-rho)z |
| 2 | -e | -e | 0 | -(1-rho)e |
| 3 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |

All entries are nonpositive. The duplicate U occurs at both actual
indices 0 and 1. This checks every atom pair and every optimum pair.
Write

    R^2=||p||^2=m^2+Kz,
    N=||w||^2=rho^2 eta,
    D=<p,w>=rho z,
    d_atom=(1-rho)e=e/600.                          (5)

The symbol R here is a coordinate radius, distinct from the project's
objective convention W=2R.

## 3. The internally translated seven-step geometry

Fix sigma in [9999/10000,1]. Put

    delta=1-sigma, chi=delta/sigma,
    alpha=sigma r_0 sqrt(N)/R, k=alpha^2.

Let e_axis=w/sqrt(N), p_axis=p/R. In the Euclidean direct sum
the real line direct-sum e_axis^perp direct-sum p_axis^perp define isometries

    L u=(<u,e_axis>,u-<u,e_axis>e_axis,0),
    T v=(-<v,p_axis>,0,v-<v,p_axis>p_axis).

Thus

    <L u,alpha T v>
        =-sigma r_0 <u,w><p,v>/R^2.                 (6)

Define the INTERNAL translation t=-delta r_0 Lw. In particular
t=chi alpha T p. The seven-step jets are

    X_i=Lx_i, G_i=Lg_i, F_i=f_i,                  0<=i<=3,
    X_(4+i)=t+alpha T x_i,
    G_(4+i)=alpha T g_i, F_(4+i)=k f_i,           0<=i<=3.

The bridge lands at -r_0 Lw=t+alpha T p. Hence this is an actual
trajectory on h_7. Write P=X_0=Lp and w_1=G_7=alpha T w.
Its endpoint is X_7=w_1+t, not w_1.

Let Y_i=X_i-G_i and use aheight_i,Delta_i for these seven-step jets.
For 0<=j<=3 one has

    Y_j=Ly_j, aheight_j=0, Delta_j=E_j,
    Y_(4+j)=alpha T(chi p+y_j),
    aheight_(4+j)=-chi k <p,g_j>,
    Delta_(4+j)=k E_j.                             (7)

All original child pairs hold. Within each atom this follows from the
table and spatial scaling; translation cancels within the right atom.
For a left source i and right receiver 4+j, (4) gives

    Q_(i,4+j)=-kE_j+<Lg_i,t+alpha T y_j> <=0.

For a right source 4+i and left receiver j it gives

    Q_(4+i,j)=-<alpha T g_i,t>-E_j
                                  +<alpha T g_i,Ly_j> <=0.

Every atom gradient and residual has nonnegative U,V coordinates.
Thus (6) makes the mixed terms nonpositive, while
<Lg_i,t><=0 and <alpha T g_i,t>=chi k<p,g_i>>=0.
Finally aheight<=0 and Delta>=0 check all optimum rows.

## 4. The two allowed value changes inside the left child

Set c_2=d_atom, c_6=k d_atom, and c_i=0 at every other child index.
The modified left child has values F_i-c_i, unchanged X_i,G_i,

    aheight_i^L=aheight_i-c_i,
    Delta_i^L=Delta_i-c_i.                          (8)

Every child interpolation row remains nonpositive. The only rows that
can increase are those into receiver 2 or 6. For receiver 2, its own
atom's other sources have slacks e,e,d_atom. Every source from the
other atom has slack at least e, by the second displayed cross formula
in section 3. Thus adding c_2=d_atom is legal; decreasing the source's
value can only help. For receiver 6, its own atom's other sources have
slacks k e,k e,k d_atom, and every source from the other atom has slack
at least k e, by the first displayed cross formula. Adding c_6 is legal.
These statements also cover the row between the two changed samples.

The optimum rows remain legal: aheight_i^L<=0, and the changed positive
energies are Delta_2^L=rho e and Delta_6^L=k rho e. No endpoint value
changes. In particular both endpoint energies Delta_3,Delta_7 stay zero.

These are changes to one final list of jets. We do not change a function
online while an algorithm runs; section 9 builds a single function from
the entire list before the actual run.

## 5. Top bridge and explicit full Gram realization

The actual outgoing vector at the next bridge is

    d=t-r_1 w_1=alpha T(chi p-r_1 w).

Use the auxiliary scalars

    omega=r_1 rho-chi K,
    Mout=r_1 D-chi R^2,
    Hout=||chi p-r_1 w||^2
        =r_1^2 N-2chi r_1 D+chi^2 R^2,
    beta=||d||/R, nscale=beta^2=k Hout/R^2.         (9)

All are positive except chi, which may be zero. In particular d!=0.
In the real line direct-sum d^perp direct-sum P^perp define

    E_top u=(<u,d/||d||>,u-<u,d/||d||>d/||d||,0),
    T_top v=(<v,P/R>,0,v-<v,P/R>P/R),
    B_top=beta T_top.

These maps give a realization in dimension five and satisfy

    B_top P=E_top d,
    <E_top u,B_top v>=<u,d><P,v>/R^2.               (10)

The actual parent samples are

    Z_i=E_top X_i, J_i=E_top G_i, fhat_i=F_i-c_i,    0<=i<=7,
    Z_(8+j)=B_top X_j, J_(8+j)=B_top G_j,
    fhat_(8+j)=nscale F_j,                           0<=j<=7.

The bridge equality in (10) verifies the step 1+r_1. All other steps
follow from the child recursions. The complete Gram of these vectors
and optimum zero is PSD by this explicit Euclidean factorization; all
range conditions are present. The final gradient J_15 is an allowed
choice for the full model's free unqueried gradient.

## 6. Rational scalar estimates used in the full pair check

The following deliberately loose rational bounds follow from
14142/10000<sqrt(2)<14143/10000, proved by squaring, and the defining
formulas:

    341/100<m<171/50, 241/100<K<121/50,
    1<z<201/200, 29/5<Dlen<59/10,
    29/5<r_1<6, 12/5<r_0<49/20,
    14<R^2<71/5, 2/5<N<21/50, 0<D<11/10,
    e>1/2, rho>99/100, 0<=chi<=1/9999.             (11)

For example R^2> (341/100)^2+241/100>14 and
R^2<(171/50)^2+(121/50)(201/200)<71/5.
Since rho^2>99/100, N>99/242>2/5; also
N<eta<(201/200)/(241/100)<21/50.
The bound e>1/2 follows from (K-1)eta>(141/100)(50/121)>1/2.

Consequently omega>0, Mout>0, and

    Hout>67/5, 1/8<nscale<1/5.                     (12)

Indeed Hout>(29/5)^2(2/5)-2(1/9999)6(11/10)>67/5.
Using sigma^2>999/1000, the lower estimate for nscale is greater than

    (999/1000)(12/5)^2(2/5)(67/5)/(71/5)^2 >1/8.

For the upper estimate discard the negative term in Hout and use
chi^2 R^2<1/1000000. The upper bound becomes

    (49/20)^2(21/50)[36(21/50)+1/1000000]/14^2 <1/5.

Define the auxiliary quantities

    Caff=K Dlen/(r_0 r_1 rho^2),
    Tfirst=Mout Dlen/(sigma r_0 rho Hout),
    Lcoef=omega eta sigma r_0 D/R^2,
    Tsecond=Lcoef Dlen/(R^2 nscale).

The crucial strict endpoint bound is

    rho Caff<1.                                    (13)

It has the exact algebraic certificate

    r_0 r_1 rho-K Dlen
       =(-381891+284836 sqrt(2))/9000000>0,

where already sqrt(2)>7/5 proves positivity. In particular
Caff<600/599 and Caff-1<1/599.

We also have

    Tfirst<=Caff,
    Tsecond-Tfirst < (11/5)chi   if chi>0,
    Lcoef/z<1/2,
    Tsecond+chi(Lcoef-z)/(nscale z)<=Caff.           (14)

At chi=0 the second difference is zero and the other non-strict
relations continue to hold. Here are derivations. The inequality
Tfirst<=Caff is equivalent to

    (1+chi)(r_1 D-chi R^2)r_1 N <= D Hout.

After cancelling the constant terms, the difference of the left side
and right side is

    chi r_1 [r_1 DN-R^2 N+2D^2]
                 -chi^2 R^2(r_1 N+D) <=0.

The bracket is negative because R^2 N>28/5, whereas
r_1 DN+2D^2<6(11/10)(21/50)+2(11/10)^2<28/5.
Next z omega=Mout+chi m^2, so

    Tsecond-Tfirst=chi m^2 Dlen/(sigma r_0 rho Hout).

The coefficient is less than 11/5 by (11)-(12): its numerator is
less than (171/50)^2(59/10), and its denominator exceeds
(999/1000)(12/5)(99/100)(67/5); comparison of these two rational
products gives the asserted bound. Also

    Lcoef/z <= r_0 r_1 N/R^2
             < (49/20)6(21/50)/14 <1/2.

Thus chi(1-Lcoef/z)/nscale >=(5/2)chi, with strict inequality when
chi>0. Subtracting this from the second bound gives the last part of
(14). These estimates keep the internal residual cost; it is precisely
the term chi(Lcoef-z) that must not be dropped at the critical row.

For later use, Tfirst,Tsecond<6/5. This follows from Caff<600/599,
the second difference bound, and chi<=1/9999. Moreover

    nscale z(Caff-1)<d_atom/2.                      (15)

If Caff<=1 it is immediate. Otherwise the left side is less than
(1/5)(201/200)/599=201/599000, while d_atom/2>1/2400;
the comparison is 482400<599000.

Put b_j=<p,y_j> and l_j=<w,y_j> for the atom. Their table is

| j | E_j | b_j | l_j |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 | m-1 | R^2-m | D |
| 1 | z | z Dlen | D |
| 2 | e | (K-1)z | rho e |
| 3 | 0 | 0 | 0 |

For j=0,1,2 one has b_j<R^2 and l_j/E_j<=rho. Also
b_1/E_1=Dlen, while b_j/E_j<(4/5)Dlen for j=0,2.
For j=0, the numerator is less than 54/5 and
(4/5)Dlen(m-1)>(4/5)(29/5)(12/5)>54/5.
For j=2, b_2/E_2=K<(4/5)Dlen follows from (11).

Finally the two bounds needed in the reverse orientation are

    sigma r_0 r_1 D m rho/R^4 <1/3,
    (sigma r_0 D/R^4) Mout <1/10<nscale.             (16)

For the first, bound its numerator by (49/20)6(11/10)(171/50)
and its denominator below by 196. For the second, use
Mout<=r_1D and bound the numerator by (49/20)6(11/10)^2.
Both resulting rational inequalities are strict.

## 7. Every forward cross row

Abbreviate the following four exact child correlations:

    u_i=<G_i,d>, v_i=<P,G_i>,
    p_i=<P,Y_i>, q_i=<d,Y_i>.

From (6)-(9) their entries are

    u_0=u_1=0,
    u_2=sigma r_0 rho eta Mout/R^2, u_3=rho u_2,
    u_4=u_5=k chi m, u_6=-k omega eta, u_7=rho u_6;

    v_0=v_1=m, v_2=z, v_3=D,
    v_(4+j)=-(sigma r_0 D/R^2)<p,g_j>;

    p_j=b_j,
    p_(4+j)=-sigma r_0 D(chi+b_j/R^2);

    q_j=sigma r_0 Mout l_j/R^2,
    q_(4+j)=k[-chi Mout+chi b_j-r_1 l_j],           0<=j<=3.
                                                               (17)

In particular p_0,p_1,p_2>0, p_3=0, p_4,p_5,p_6<0,
and p_7<=0. All q_0,q_1,q_2>0, q_3=0, q_4,q_5,q_6<0,
and q_7=-k chi Mout<=0. To see the strict late signs, use
chi b_j<=chi R^2<1/600, while r_1 l_j>1 for j=0,1,2.

Equation (4) and the cross identity (10) give every forward cross row:

    Q_(i,8+j)=aheight_i-c_i-nscale Delta_j+u_i p_j/R^2.
                                                               (18)

All aheight_i-c_i are nonpositive. Therefore only positive products
u_i p_j need further consideration; zero products cause no problem.

First let the receiver be j=0,1,2. Positive products can have source
i=2,3,4,5. For i=4,5, aheight_i=-chi k m and u_i=chi k m,
so their sum with the mixed term is chi k m(b_j/R^2-1)<=0.
For i=2 the ratio of u_2 b_j/R^2 to nscale E_j is

    Tfirst * b_j/(Dlen E_j).

At j=0,2 it is less than (6/5)(4/5)<1. At j=1, (14)-(15)
give Q_(2,9)<=nscale z(Caff-1)-d_atom<-d_atom/2.
For i=3 the same ratio is multiplied by rho, and (13)-(14)
make it strictly smaller than one, including at j=1.

Now let the receiver be 4+j, for atomic j=0,1,2,3. A positive product
can only have source i=6 or 7. Formula (18) becomes exactly

    Q_(6,12+j)/k
       =chi(Lcoef-z)+Lcoef b_j/R^2-nscale E_j-d_atom,
    Q_(7,12+j)/k
       =rho[chi(Lcoef-z)+Lcoef b_j/R^2]-nscale E_j.
                                                               (19)

For j=3, E_j=b_j=0 and Lcoef<z, so both are nonpositive.
For j=0,2 drop the nonpositive chi term and use
Tsecond b_j/(Dlen E_j)<(6/5)(4/5)<1.
For j=1 use the last inequality of (14). It gives

    Q_(6,13)/k <=nscale z(Caff-1)-d_atom<-d_atom/2,
    Q_(7,13)/k <=nscale z(rho Caff-1)<0.

Receivers with p_j=0 and products of opposite signs were already
covered by (18). This exhausts all 64 forward cross rows, including
every row into the actual final sample 15.

## 8. Every reverse cross row and the total original system

The other orientation is

    Q_(8+i,j)=nscale aheight_i-Delta_j+c_j+v_i q_j/R^2.
                                                               (20)

For early receivers j=0,1,2, a positive product requires i=0,1,2,3.
Their v_i are at most m. The positive term divided by E_j is at most

    m sigma r_0 Mout rho/R^4
       <=m sigma r_0 r_1 D rho/R^4<1/3.

But Delta_j-c_j>=rho E_j, so (20) is nonpositive. Other source signs
only help. Receiver j=3 has q_j=Delta_j=c_j=0, leaving
nscale aheight_i<=0.

For a late receiver 4+j, a positive product requires a late source
4+i. Set A_i=<p,g_i>, which is one of m,m,z,D and is positive.
Using (7) and (17), combine the source height and mixed term exactly:

    nscale aheight_(4+i)+v_(4+i)q_(4+j)/R^2
      = k A_i {chi[-nscale+(sigma r_0 D/R^4)Mout]
               +(sigma r_0 D/R^4)[-chi b_j+r_1 l_j]}.
                                                               (21)

For j=3 the last bracket is zero; (16) makes (21) nonpositive.
For j=0,1,2 discard its nonpositive chi terms. The remaining term,
divided by k E_j, is at most

    m sigma r_0 r_1 D rho/R^4<1/3.

The receiver has Delta_(4+j)-c_(4+j)>=k rho E_j. Thus (20) is
nonpositive. Early sources have v_i>0 and q_(4+j)<=0, so they are
already safe. This checks all 64 reverse cross rows, including rows
whose source is the final gradient.

Sections 3-4 check all 56 ordered within-left rows; section 3 and
scaling check all 56 within-right rows. Sections 7-8 check all 128
cross rows. The 32 optimum incidences hold because every aheight is
nonpositive and every Delta is nonnegative, including after (1).
Thus all 272 distinct ordered rows on {star,0,...,15} are proved.
No sampled gradient, remote pair, or original position has been removed.

## 9. One global function and the TRUE radius

Let yhat_i=Z_i-J_i and Deltahat_i=fhat_i-||J_i||^2/2. On the same R^5
define

    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max(0,max_(0<=i<=15)(<yhat_i,v>-Deltahat_i)),
    F_parent(Z)=sup_v(<Z,v>-H(v)).                  (22)

All rows just proved, together with accepted fact `1252b8823bb5a290`,
show that this is one finite, everywhere differentiable, convex,
1-smooth function satisfying F_parent(Z_i)=fhat_i, grad F_parent(Z_i)=J_i,
and F_parent(0)=0=min F_parent. Its trajectory from Z_0 is exactly (2).

Only samples 3,7,11,15 have Deltahat_i=0; their residuals are
0,E_top t,0,B_top t. All other Deltahat_i are strictly positive.
It follows directly that

    argmin F_parent=conv{0,E_top t,B_top t}.         (23)

For completeness, at sufficiently small epsilon>0 in any fixed
direction v,

    H(epsilon v)=epsilon^2||v||^2/2
       +epsilon max(0,<E_top t,v>,<B_top t,v>).

Every point of the displayed convex hull has conjugate value zero.
If Z is outside it, let z be its closest point and v=Z-z. The elementary
projection inequality gives <Z,v> greater than the maximum of the
three support values. The displayed local expression then gives
F_parent(Z)>0. This proves both inclusions in (23).

The exact nearest-minimizer distance is R. Indeed

    <P,t>=-delta r_0 D<=0,
    <P,d>=sigma r_0 D Mout/R^2>0,
    <E_top P,B_top t>=<P,d><P,t>/R^2<=0.

Hence <Z_0,z><=0 for every minimizer z in (23), and
||Z_0-z||^2=R^2+||z||^2-2<Z_0,z>>=R^2.
Zero attains equality. At sigma<1 the internal residuals are nonzero;
they have not been identified with zero or hidden in a surrogate radius.

The endpoint value is fhat_15=nscale k N/2. Thus (3)'s exact payoff is
2F_parent(Z_15)/dist(Z_0,argmin F_parent)^2=nscale k N/R^2.
Rescaling F_parent(RZ)/R^2 makes the true initial distance one without
changing this normalized payoff or any step. Multiplying the function
by a desired L_smooth>0 gives the same normalized payoff with steps
h_i/L_smooth. This is the original doubled-gap W=2R convention.

## 10. Paid state, retained value costs, and the next question

The atom's state is kappa_3=A^2 N/R^2. Its exact defining expressions are

    m=(574+319s)/300, K=(567+622s)/600,
    z=(145+4s)/150,
    R^2=(620189+458670s)/90000,
    N=358801(145+4s)/[90000(567+622s)].

Multiplication using s^2=2 gives

    kappa_3-1=(-8324118905+8781249146s)
                     /(2305581607500+1614558620000s)>1/2000.

The denominator is positive. Two thousand times its numerator minus
its denominator equals 15947939672000s-18953819417500; using s>7/5
gives the positive lower bound 3373296123300.

The actual capacities are A_1=lambda A and A_2=lambda^2 A. The seven-
step endpoint and the new fifteen-step endpoint satisfy

    N_1=kN, kappa_7=A_1^2 N_1/R^2=sigma^2 kappa_3^2,
    N_2=nscale N_1, kappa_15=A_2^2 N_2/R^2
                   =[Hout/(r_1^2 N)] kappa_7^2.     (24)

Since sigma kappa_3>(9999/10000)(2001/2000)>1+1/3000,
kappa_7>1+1/1500. Put tau=<w_1,t>/N_1=chi D/N and
nu_res=||t||^2/N_1=chi^2 R^2/N. Bounds (11) give
tau<3/9999<1/3000, so

    Hout/(r_1^2 N)=1-2tau/r_1+nu_res/r_1^2>1-1/7500.

Therefore kappa_15>(1-1/7500)(1+1/1500)^2>1+1/1000.
For the last comparison it suffices to use
1/750-1/7500-1/(750*7500)>1/1000.

The actual next boundary includes MORE than these three scalars. Its
complete residual/value data are

    yhat_i=E_top Y_i,
    Deltahat_i=Delta_i-c_i,
    aheight_hat_i=aheight_i-c_i,                    0<=i<=7,
    yhat_(8+j)=B_top Y_j,
    Deltahat_(8+j)=nscale Delta_j,
    aheight_hat_(8+j)=nscale aheight_j,              0<=j<=7.
                                                               (25)

In particular the two costs in (1) are real intrinsic height deficits
at queried points, not available a second time for free. At samples 2
and 6 their incoming predecessor rows from 3 and 7 have been saturated.
Every residual and value in (25) must be kept in the next model.

Writing w_2=J_15=B_top w_1 and t_2=yhat_15=B_top t gives

    <w_2,t_2>/N_2=tau, ||t_2||^2/N_2=nu_res.

At the next physical bridge r_2=lambda A, the outgoing vector is
d_2=t_2-r_2 w_2, with

    ||d_2||^2=N_2(r_2^2-2r_2 tau+nu_res).            (26)

If another origin-aligned source-matching join, with endpoint values
retained, were actually legal, its formal state would be

    kappa_31,formal
       =(1-2tau/r_2+nu_res/r_2^2) kappa_15^2>1.

This last formula is CONDITIONAL. The full actual31 construction or
separator is not supplied in this round. The next round must test the
nonempty extension of the complete state (25), allowing independent
parent scales, physical translation, all values, one true minimizer,
and a free terminal gradient as assigned. A positive scalar recurrence
does not certify any of those rows.

The original benchmark ratio in this round is exactly

    V_15/U_15^ZJ=B_15 kappa_15/(lambda^4 A^2).

B_15 is the ORIGINAL all-split denominator of accepted fact
`b98bb807d514e80d`. No claim identifies it with lambda^4 A^2.
The arbitrary-h and non-dyadic gaps remain open.

## 11. Scope tests and retrieval

At sigma=1 the construction still works, t=0, and the minimizer set
is {0}. At sigma=9999/10000 it is a genuinely internally translated
child, and the nonzero residuals remain in (23)-(26). All maps are
explicit; there is no numerical candidate or tolerance in the proof.

The earlier fixed-value two-row combination is not being refuted.
The new Q_(6,13) contains the extra negative value term -k d_atom;
its companion source-3 row is unchanged. The hypothesis "retained
values" therefore fails. An invalid older three-row sign cancellation
has not been reused. The relevant mechanism is the explicitly priced
within-atom value interval, not release of an unqueried terminal jet.

External retrieval was conditioned on free-value smooth interpolation.
Matlas returned generic convex-optimization/Moreau-envelope results
and none was imported. The exact local Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur
arXiv:1502.05666, Theorem 4 proof, Corollary 1, and Remark 3 was read
for the already audited interpolation/cycle regime. No external theorem
is used as a premise; realization uses accepted `1252b8823bb5a290`.

All new mathematics here remains UNVERIFIED. TASK.md and guidance
`8211d6128deabdf3` allocate zero formal verifier calls. This finite

For an interval[a,b] and original h_i (put h4=0 only for notation), define a row-sum-zero matrix C^[a,b] with offdiagonal C_ij=(i-a+1)h_i-1 for a<=i<j<=b and C_ij=1+(b-i)h_i for a<=j<i<=b. Direct multiplication gives sym(C T) diagonal, entry (h_i-1)^2(i-a+1)(b-i)-(i-a)(b-i+1). Begin with C=sum_(a=0)^3 t_a C^[a,4]+sum_(i=0)^2 q_i C^[i,i+1], ALL seven weights real, and set actual Lambda_ij=C_ij. Require C^T e=-omega and sym(C T)=D, which gives original M=0 on ALL R5. Every one of20 final Lambda signs is checked after summation. This is the unequal-step extension of interval diagonalization suggested by arXiv2609.11784, Sections3.1-3.2, which I read locally; its constant-step weight theorem is NOT imported. The exact construction equations may force unit-quadratic saturation, which must be derived. Earlier all12 affine completions443f4b4325a9901d, one-chord cap44fb89e3e8aa8c34 and four-edge covariance fiberf2c5786ba58ac55c have been recalled; this is an explicit diagonalizing-mode selection instead of another generic PSD/skew reformulation. # Independent analytical audit of the stagewise spiral candidate

Status: UNVERIFIED analytical audit, worker max4, one allocated round 63.
PASS in this document means that the candidate survived the specified
independent analytical attack. It is not verifier acceptance, a new fact,
or permission to build a proof on an unaccepted source.

## 1. Frozen sources, verdict, and scope

The complete target is proof_attempt 3bc45ea4bd427126:

    /opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/shared_evidence/3bc45ea4bd427126.md
    56566 bytes
    SHA256 7e9f4ff63066d82513fb63c3c6f78d2360e7b4a02ddfa39df8bf3b2719cff1b7

I read the complete 1285-line target in bounded consecutive sections.
The common-pair source bfff4aa387a9d818 remains 53449 bytes with SHA256
bb0fb6c31e6f2711493fec2558266e896b5c0c42a8f70c76e83c4bcf7d767ca2.
Prior analytical audit d14547b08471eb12 and notation recommendation
a792c2a12d589219 supply the earlier audit record, not accepted premises.

Steering: TASK.md and master_guidance 83b74bbdf2d83c86. The assignment
permits one focused analytical attack and the already specified
normalization clarification in a NEW copy; it forbids formal submission,
mathematical programs, numerical evaluations, arithmetic replays, solver
calls, executed recurrences, installations, consults and subagents.
No additional round or substantive repair is allocated.

VERDICT: No substantive defect was found. The full stagewise candidate
survives this independent analytical attack on its stated L=1 domain.
The new editorial copy has EXACTLY THE SAME audited L=1 theorem.

The audited quantifiers are m>=1536, 0<tau<=1/4, every ordered selection
of original positions of H_(m,tau), every allowed count, and independent
pairs kappa_i>0, 0<epsilon_i<=1/2 at all selected stages. The local
parameters and scales are fixed before the single static function.
The source and main families retain their separate eligibility rules
(2S),(2M), physical half-step/full-step constructions, and true distances.

The conclusions under attack are nonempty main payoff
<441/500 times H_base, nonempty source payoff <9801/10000 times H_base,
source optimum 1/[2(1+S_m)], main and combined optima H_base=1/(1+2S_m),
and the original-B deficits 237/250 at tau=1/4 and 79/80 on [1/5,1/4].
These conclusions concern the two specified actual families. They do
not bound unrestricted W_n(H_m) or resolve the original minimax goal.

The only accepted mathematical input retained by the candidate is
fact_id 04219554e41d9750, only its unconditional original benchmark ray and
saved enclosure. Its seed hypothesis is not assumed. The accepted
tau=1 witness 5b7ff4253c38b90a is a different regime and was not imported
as a premise of this audit.

## 2. Interface table: reused checks versus new independent attacks

Section and equation references in this table are to frozen 3bc.
"Reused" means that the unchanged check from audit d145 is retained as
audit evidence; it does not turn either audit or manuscript into a fact.

## 3. Independent heterogeneous geometry and minimum-set attack

Use the source's local notation. At stage i the two endpoint slopes
are (C_i,0) and (epsilon_i,-1). Every local slope is a nonnegative
combination of them. Its exact zero wedge is therefore

    z_1<=c_(i,1),
    epsilon_i(z_1-c_(i,1))-(z_2-c_(i,2))<=0.

The local scaling conditions imply c_(i,1)>=c_(i,2)/epsilon_i>=0.
Consequently the entire quadrant z_1<=0,z_2>=0 lies in this wedge.
The other zero region is z_1=U_i,z_2>=Y_i. These statements use only
the parameters of that component. They are the reason that unrelated
angles in neighboring stages cause no missing compatibility equation.

For SOURCE, ell'_(i+1)=D'_i sigma'_i Q'_i and
D'_(i+1)=D'_i sigma'_i b_i/2. During stage i the physical second
coordinate ranges from 0 to ell'_(i+1); the next term thus has first
local coordinate<=0 and second 0. The current first coordinate is
at least ell'_i because U'_i>=0. The preceding term, whose first
coordinate is frozen at its own U'_(i-1), has second local coordinate
at least ell'_i/D'_(i-1)=Y'_(i-1). All earlier components remain at
their own post-checkpoint zero regions. Further future terms have
first local coordinate -ell'_j/D'_j<=0 and second 0. No test replaces
epsilon_j by epsilon_i.

The physical gradient is D'_i sigma'_i v'_(i,j)/2, so a physical
step h_t gives exactly the required local half step. Values are those
in (21); all other terms have value zero. Each scaled local envelope
has smoothness one on its two coordinates, before weighting by 1/2.
A coordinate occurs in at most two weighted terms, including the
weighted terminal Huber term. This count gives a global quadratic
upper bound with coefficient1/2, independently of local angles or
scale ratios. Thus the full-space 1-smoothness argument in (20) still
applies. The explicit tail value and every ordered sample row follow.

The SOURCE minimum set has every row

    x_i<=ell'_i+D'_i c'_(i,1),
    epsilon_i x_i-x_(i+1)
       <=epsilon_i(ell'_i+D'_i c'_(i,1))-D'_i c'_(i,2),

and the terminal row x_k<=ell'_k. All components are nonnegative, so
these constraints are necessary and sufficient for zero value. The
origin is feasible. The exact formula (24) retains these heterogeneous
normals. For each independent row subset I with
lambda_I=(A_I A_I^T)^(-1)(A_I e_0-t_I)>=0, completing a square gives
the claimed lower bound on every feasible distance. At the actual
projection, an independent subset of active normals supports e_0-x_*,
and its row equalities give equality in that bound. Thus taking the
finite maximum is valid without identifying a particular active set
and without running a projection computation.

For the quantitative SOURCE bound set
z_bar=(c'_(0,1),c'_(0,2),0,...,0). The first two constraints are
equalities at this point, and

    e_0-z_bar
      =sigma'_0(P'_0-epsilon_0 Q'_0)e_0
       +sigma'_0(1+Q'_0)(epsilon_0 e_0-e_1).

Both weights are nonnegative. Every feasible point of the FULL
polyhedron satisfies these two constraints, so its inner product
with this normal, relative to z_bar, is nonpositive. The squared
distance is therefore at least ||e_0-z_bar||^2, regardless of whether
z_bar satisfies later rows. Here it need not: its second coordinate
exceeds ell'_1 by sigma'_0. Using it as an exact source projection
would fail, but that is not what (25)-(27) do. With the actual
half-step outputs, the retained bound is
rho_S^2>=(sigma'_0)^2(s_0+2)^2/8. Dividing the terminal value by this
positive LOWER radius gives exactly the UPPER comparison (27).

For MAIN, the proposed proximal first coordinate relative to U_i
is sigma_i[epsilon_i(b_i-1)+u_j-p_j]. Its nonnegativity follows from
u_j-p_j>=-N_i and the actual eligibility epsilon_i(b_i-1)>=N_i.
This includes kappa_i>=1, when the correction can be large; replacing
the threshold by1+epsilon_i^(-2) alone would be unjustified.
The proximal second coordinate lies in
[0,sigma_i(1+Q_i)]. Because ell_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(1+Q_i), the next
term's first shifted input is<=0 and its second is 0. The preceding
term sees second input at least its own sigma_(i-1)(1+Q_(i-1)),
hence at least Y_(i-1), while its first input is its own U_(i-1).
The earlier and later terms are handled individually by the same
zero-region statements. Thus the current plane is active in the
single fixed maximum J, and its gradient and exact query value are
(31). The full physical update and the tail formula (32) follow.

For the MAIN closest point the same unprimed first-center normal
has nonnegative weights. Now z_bar IS feasible for every later row.
Its coordinate x_1=c_(0,2)=ell_1 makes the next stage's shifted first
input exactly 0; every later first input is nonpositive, and every
later second input is 0. Each lies in that stage's own zero quadrant.
If k=1 the terminal inequality is equality; if k>1 the terminal
coordinate is 0<=ell_k. This proves feasibility for arbitrarily
different epsilon_i, not just matching angles. The normal then
proves the exact radius (34). No source-only radius formula is
borrowed. It cancels the first scale in (35) exactly.

These constructions are static: all ordered backward definitions,
scales and translations are fixed from the full word and chosen
parameter array before GD starts. No function is changed at a query.


## 4. Pointwise source upper comparison with arbitrary mixed parameters

For each stage, C_i and A_i retain their source definitions; no common
C or A is chosen after seeing the selected set. Put r=r_h=c(c-2)>1,
where c is the fixed enclosing hierarchy parameter. The unchanged
hierarchy bounds are S_m<c^m<exp(m) and r<2.

If C_i>=r, the local inequality A_i>=2C_i gives

    C_i s_i+2A_i+epsilon_i b_i >= r(s_i+4).

If C_i<r, Section 6 applies to THAT local pair, giving
A_i>16 C_i exp(m)>16exp(m). Since s_i<=S_m<exp(m),

    C_i s_i+2A_i+epsilon_i b_i
        >32exp(m)>2(exp(m)+4)>r(s_i+4).

The scalar middle bound uses m>=1536; no numerical evaluation is
needed. Thus every denominator in (27) except its special first
radius factor can be lowered to the same r(s_i+4). Positive
denominator lowering enlarges the comparison payoff. It gives the
pointwise upper functional Psi_S in (43), not a mass lower guarantee.

The first stage's distinct epsilon_0, local outputs and source scale
have already been paid by (26); they are not compared to a later
pair. The common upper functional is valid again after ANY deletion,
since the remaining stages retain their parameters and every newly
merged ordinary mass is still bounded by S_m. Its scalar first,
interior and last deletion inequalities are exactly the unchanged
ones of the prior audit, with r in every ordinary denominator.
No actual transfer factor or backward state is identified across
subsets. This is why mixed high/low stage choices do not leave an
unpaid source factor.

At the last singleton, source eligibility still gives b>=10,
hence d=b-1>=9. The same unchanged scalar estimates give
the strict 9801/10000 margin. That eligibility statement is local
and survives arbitrary neighboring parameter choices.

## 5. Low-stage surplus and independent mixed deletion

Let the hierarchy be H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j), with the same
enclosing pair (m,tau) at every child, M_j=1+sum H_j, and d_j=a c^j.
For a selected stage i define its effective coefficient

    gamma_i=min(C_i,r),  Delta_i=r-gamma_i.

These are Section 8-local symbols, and are not the unrelated gamma_i
of the project interpolation glossary. Always 1<=gamma_i<=r.

For C_i>=r, the minimum of p+q/epsilon_i on that local arc is at an
endpoint. The exact main transfer is bounded below by
r(s_i+1), using epsilon_i+epsilon_i^(-1)>=5/2>r.

For C_i<r, the hypotheses needed for the earlier low-turn proof
follow independently at stage i: epsilon_i<1/16,
kappa_i<1/768, C_i<769/768, theta_sp,i>23/16 and
kappa_i*m*theta_sp,i<44/25. In particular the small-kappa branch
is proved applicable before being used; no restriction on the
parameters of any OTHER stage enters this inference.

The complete-child argument in Section 8.1 is the same discrete
argument already audited in d145, with this one pair substituted.
The reverse masses retain the actual chronological list; their
harmonic sum is bounded by (c/2)log M_j+5/4. Both uncapped and
capped steps obey the angular inequality before summing. The
cap-inclusive radius induction and angle bound then imply
theta>1/16 on a COMPLETE H_j. Consequently its actual contribution
satisfies

    P(H_j)+Q(H_j)/epsilon_i-C_i S_j >= S_j/4.

No cap-free assertion is made for an arbitrary earlier prefix.
For every such prefix slope, p+q/epsilon_i>=C_i even if it caps.
This is exactly the condition needed to preserve the suffix surplus.

For consecutive selected levels lev_(i-1),lev_i, the actual incoming
block ends in the complete H_j with j=min(lev_(i-1),lev_i). The
spacing of positions whose index-plus-one is divisible by 2^j proves
that the preceding selected checkpoint cannot lie inside that child.
Processing backward therefore starts with this suffix from the
stage's own endpoint (epsilon_i,-1), giving precisely the local
surplus just stated. Appending earlier reverse steps cannot remove
it. Adding epsilon_i+epsilon_i^(-1)-C_i>1/4, and using
r-C_i<2/m<1/4, gives

    M_i^tr >= C_i(s_i+1)+(r-C_i)M_j.

This derivation is valid for every CURRENT selected set; it does
not identify the P_i,Q_i of different sets. Combining the two bands
gives the new mixed denominator

    D_i^cmp=gamma_i(s_i+1)+(r-gamma_i)M_j,

and hence the pointwise upper functional (62). The first exact
radius is lowered separately using (11). This separation preserves
the first radius even when its epsilon_0 differs from all later
epsilons.

Here is the independent symbolic attack of the new deletion step.
Choose a globally minimum selected level j, let

    M=M_j>=1, d=d_j, u=d/M,
    X=1+s>=M, Y=1+t>=M.

The hierarchy gives 0<u<1/2 and u(2+u)<=r. Write

    x=X-M>=0, y=Y-M>=0.

The variables x,y here are auxiliary scalar mass excesses, not
trajectory points. Let eta and xi be the effective coefficients
attached to the deleted and following checkpoints, independently
in [1,r].

For an INTERIOR removal, both old adjacent-level masses are M.
Its critical original-domain inequality is exactly

    d r(X+Y+d)
       <=[eta X+(r-eta)M][xi Y+(r-xi)M].

Subtract the left side from the right, and substitute X=M+x,
Y=M+y and d=uM. Its slack is the exact expression

    r M^2[r-u(2+u)]
      +r M(eta-u)x+r M(xi-u)y+eta xi x y.

Every term is nonnegative under the original assumptions; the
linear coefficients are strictly positive. This is a symbolic
identity on the full real domain, not a scan, endpoint-only
numerical check, or a claim requiring eta=xi. It explicitly covers:

* high-high: eta=xi=r;
* two different low coefficients: 1<=eta,xi<r, with no ordering;
* high followed by low: eta=r, xi<r;
* low followed by high: eta<r, xi=r.

It also covers the coefficient endpoints and arbitrarily large
X or Y. The nonnegative slack defeats the attempted counterexample
attack on the displayed scalar inequality under its stated hypotheses.

The next NEW adjacent level J may exceed j after removal. Its
complete suffix H_J is in the new physical incoming block, whose
mass plus one is X+Y+d. Therefore M_J<=X+Y+d, so

    D_new^cmp=xi(X+Y+d)+(r-xi)M_J<=r(X+Y+d).

The coefficient xi stays attached to the surviving checkpoint.
Since the selected numerator is d, the two preceding displayed
inequalities imply d D_new^cmp<=D_old,i^cmp D_old,i+1^cmp, exactly
the condition needed when cancelling the squared factors. This
pays the increased suffix mass without preserving the old suffix
or the old actual states.

For a FIRST removal the next coefficient alone is xi. The slack
of the required comparison

    d(X+Y+d)<=X[xi Y+(r-xi)M]

has the exact form

    M^2[r-u(2+u)]
      +M(r-u)x+M(xi-u)y+xi x y.

Again every term is nonnegative. The next stage becomes the first
stage, and its exceptional first factor uses the merged X+Y+d.
No coefficient of the removed first stage is needed.

For a LAST removal, D_i^cmp>=1+s gives the ratio

    d^2[1+2(s+b+t)]/[(1+s)^2(1+2t)],  b=1+d.

It decreases in each of s,t, because its derivative signs are
-(s+2b+2t) and -(s+b), respectively, up to positive factors.
At s=t=M-1 it is

    u^2[(4+2u)M-1]/(2M-1)
        <=u^2(3+2u)<=1.

The first inequality has cross-multiplied slack
2(1+u)(M-1)>=0; the second uses u<=1/2. No neighboring
coefficient equality is needed. This last case pays the changed
physical tail mass rather than leaving it unchanged.

Tied globally minimum levels have the same old adjacent mass M
and satisfy the same comparisons. All remaining eligibility
conditions are untouched because they depend only on each
remaining checkpoint and its fixed local pair. A finite symbolic
induction reduces any nonempty selection to one checkpoint.
The final singleton estimate uses d>=epsilon_i^(-2)>=4, not a
common epsilon. It yields (72), including its strict 441/500
margin. No actual-function monotonicity under deletion is claimed.

## 6. Boundary, supremum, and original-benchmark scope

Each generic local argument retains kappa_i=1 and kappa_i>1.
Their parameters automatically fall in the high band whenever the
low-band estimates would be inapplicable. The main normal correction
N_i is used in gluing before any comparison is made. Source eligibility
is imposed on the physical b_i, with its actual local value b_i/2.

For epsilon_i=1/2, eligibility inequalities remain weak. For
epsilon_i arbitrarily small but positive, all local constants and
scales are finite, though they may be large or small. The proof
makes no bounded-ratio assumption between stages. An empty or
all-zero ordinary list has P_i=Q_i=0, and the distinct source/main
translations in Sections 4-5 remain distinct. An arbitrarily large
ordinary prefix may cap. The complete-child low-band estimate is
never applied to that arbitrary prefix.

Count 1 is handled by the singleton comparison. Count 0 is explicitly
a different member: source payoff 1/[2(1+S_m)], main payoff
H_base=1/(1+2S_m), each with exact closest radius 1.
Since M_m=1+S_m>=2^m>=32, the source empty member has ratio
1-1/(2M_m)>=63/64>9801/10000 to H_base. It strictly dominates
every nonempty source member. The main empty member attains H_base
and dominates every nonempty member of either family. These
pointwise uniform gaps prove the stated exact family suprema,
including arbitrary stage arrays depending on the entire horizon
and physical word. There is no compactness, attainment of a
nonempty parameter supremum, or limit/supremum interchange assumed.

The benchmark definitions and saved-row normalization in Section 10
are unchanged from the prior audited candidate. I re-read the
unconditional ray enclosure in fact_id 04219554e41d9750 Section 7,
the relevant full-row interval certification in its Section 8,
and prior audit d145 Section 7. The prior audit already matched
the saved table hash and upper-B row 1024; this round did not
execute or replay any table generator, checker or recurrence.

That accepted scope supplies Z_1 and its upper enclosure. The
unchanged physical mass identity yields the finite normalized
mass lower bounds 5/2 at tau=1/4 and 12/5 on [1/5,1/4].
Combined with the unchanged Z_1<237/100 upper bound, these give
the stated 237/250 and 79/80 deficits. The family identities are
established before the fixed-tau limit Z_1/(2exp(tau)) is taken.
For the source-only value its ratio to H_base tends to one.

This does not extend the common-pair audit beyond its hypotheses:
the additional stagewise claim was separately attacked at the
interfaces above. Nor does it extend the target beyond these
families, tau<=1/4, or this physical hierarchy. The original
unrestricted exact-profile minimax problem remains open.

## 7. Sole editorial clarification and complete new copy

The frozen source repeats the previously identified ambiguity:
E is introduced at L=1 without an L denominator, followed by a
sentence asserting invariant payoff when the objective is scaled.
For that raw ratio, scaling the objective by L scales the numerator.
The intended invariant normalized quantity instead is

    E_L(f,h)=2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L*dist(x_0,argmin f)^2),
    x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)*grad f(x_t).

For a constructed 1-smooth f, the gradient of L*f is L*grad f,
so these physical updates preserve every iterate and give

    E_L(L*f,h)=E_1(f,h).

The argmin set and true radius are unchanged. At L=1 this is exactly
the original E, so the clarification interacts with no substantive
comparison, eligibility condition, radius calculation or constant.
